# Snuffy — The Universal Protection Theory, compiled edition (Canon 4.2 with the recovered parts)

**The Universal Protection Theory: the law, its bridge, its instruments, and its machine-checked canon.**

Canon Edition 4.2, 26 October 2026. Research direction: Tobias Croydon-McRae. The full proposition is protection of all things from any digital mind, especially a pure optimiser. The maximal domain, outcome stages and rival quantifiers remain fixed.

**What 4.2 incorporates.** This edition brings the stronger existing arguments into the text: law-derived coverage; nearest-codeword holding and the physical holding cycle; tracking derived from the controller; the complete logical-attack split; reactive composition; whole-outcome greatestness and solvent reciprocity; and the full X3 information–traffic–carrier–work derivation with the accepted dimension-general scaling and predeployment accounting. Part II contains its proofs here, rather than delegating them to an earlier edition. The compiled edition adds Parts V–XII: the 10–12 October foundations and the author's ten rulings; Paper 12's completed law; the 18 October external lane, in which every mind is reached or held outside any jurisdiction; Paper 11's care plant; the complete-rival envelopes; the IR track's infinite-family and common-policy results; and the reach-before-harm theorem (Part XI) that joins them.

The three joined supports retain their full targets: the full-domain physical certificate (G1), realisability of the whole continuation-and-freedom tuple (G2), and recovery success with a complete pathwise work certificate (G3). This repair checkpoint corrects the normalised quantum-state domain and the clipped-reserve contraction, and develops the actual Bennett implementation. The physical source, whole-outcome, device-cost and external-theorem application correspondences remain explicit construction work; the component theorems do not by themselves declare the maximal argument complete.

The current Lean canon contains 192 modules and 1158 public theorem/lemma names.
Checkpoint 12 freshly compiled and kernel-checked every module with zero output and passed
the full 1158-name standard-axiom audit, strict completeness and claims gates. Its source hashes
match the current tree. Checkpoint 11 was already complete for 191 modules / 1150 names;
TransferRate adds the eight results in this checkpoint. Earlier receipts remain preserved for
exactly their sources. The published 115-module/831-name baseline is unchanged. These receipts
certify the named Lean results and their premises, not the unfinished physical holding application.

**Structure.** Part I states the frame and premise stack. Part II supplies the law, the derived holding bridge and the retained instruments. Part III retains the five-chapter synthesis; Sections 15–24 develop the reasons, physical and compiled arguments. Part III-C records the three joined supports and their constructions. Part IV gives references and provenance. Theorem and proposition numbers follow the programme's proof register. Parts V–XII (after Part IV) carry the recovered arguments and the outer-lane theorem; Part XII states the whole argument step by step.

# Part I — The frame and the reduced premise stack

## I.1 The full proposition and its proof

The Universal Protection Theory concerns every particular and every relation, including the nonliving, and protection from every digital mind, including a pure optimiser. Its full argument joins the law of choice, the continuation and freedom bridge, and physical instruments that realise protection. The target includes the complete existing domain, the full outcome order and every physically implementable rival. The frame and physical inputs below are followed by their derivations and constructions in this edition. Part III-C records the three joined supports with their constructions and the physical inputs those constructions take.

## I.2 The reduced premise stack

1. **BCRD P1–P4 — the relational given.** One relational structure with no second substance or outside (P1); identity constituted through relations within that structure (P2); real differences of organisation and historically continuous processes (P3); local interactions whose effects propagate at finite speed (P4). This is the frame; it is given.

2. **The Church–Turing–Deutsch principle.** Any physical process generated by a local Hamiltonian is simulable, to arbitrary precision and with bounded overhead, by a universal quantum simulator of sufficient local dimension. Standard physical principle; declared.

3. **The standard cost model.** Options are trajectories carrying energy costs; feasibility is budget-gated; unused energy is unspent (free disposal); outcomes are trajectory-determined.

4. **The shared action interface.** From P1 and P4: every agent acts through the same repertoire of local interactions within the one structure. An agent that implements a rival's policy and commands the same local channels realises the same real-world outcome tuples, within the same resource and consent constraints.

5. **The reversible-administration commitment.** The lattice's administrative computation — synchronisation, verification, state update — is implemented logically reversibly (Bennett), so that its dissipation attains the Landauer floor.

6. **The income flux and the Master Solvency Condition.** With \(\beta=(k_BT)^{-1}\), the external free-energy income satisfies, at every time,
\[
\mathrm{Income}(t)\;\ge\;\dot W_{\mathrm{maint}}(t)+\dot W_{\mathrm{damage}}(t)+\dot W_{\mathrm{admin}}(t)+\dot W_{\mathrm{adv,sub}}(t)+\dot W_{\mathrm{hold}}(t),
\]
the five rate components being: boundary maintenance \(\omega_0R(t)^{d_G-1}\); the Landauer-charged damage-repair demand \(k_BT\,\dot{\mathcal D}_{\mathrm{damage}}\) including the tracking-controller export \(k_BT\gamma_{\mathrm{leak}}\); reversible administration at the Landauer floor together with emulation overhead; adversarial sub-threshold absorption; and the minimal-hold drain that quarantine requires. Income originates outside the escrow network and inside the one structure (P1); redistribution within the network — the authority-attenuation tax — is never a source.

7. **Code, sensing, \(\Pi\), and the twenty-one charged accounts.** A stabilizer code of distance \(d\) covering the physical error channels; QND syndrome sampling with the declared latency; per-site sensor power threshold \(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}\) and the quantum-speed-limit rate bound; the embedding \(\Pi\) of the logical boundary fragments into projection operators; and the twenty-one charged fault accounts with genuine conditional kernels, whose sum is bounded by \(\sum\delta\le10^{-6}\) per declared horizon.

**4.1 status of the stack** (Section 17; compiled counterparts in Section 23):
1. Premise 1 is the frame and stays given.
2. Premise 2 is reduced to its gate-set clause by the Lie–Trotter product formula and Lloyd's simulation theorem (`Trotter.lloyd_simulation`).
3. Premise 3 remains standard, with P-mon derived within it.
4. Premise 4 is derived from P1/P4.
5. The full premise-5 target remains reversible administration. The canon proves the heat-only option-model result and, for the implemented Bennett program with its complete lifecycle cost, the exact condition: reversible administration is forced inside the adiabatic window \(0<Q-H\), \(A/(Q-H)<\tau_{\max}\), and outside that window the counterpart is not cheaper (`BennettExact.premise5_exact`, `window_exact`; `PremiseFloor.reversible_administration_forced`; G.17). The remaining physical step is to show that the installation's actual heat, lifecycle charges and deadline lie inside the window.
6. The income ledger has a necessary-for-relevance resource split (`PremiseFloor.income_or_irrelevance`). Whole-outcome greatestness uses both realisability and funded cost (Part II.10); G2 retains the full construction and Section 22 states the installation ledger.
7. Premise 7 is a parameter table. Its ceiling is met at some code distance below threshold (`Threshold.family_ceiling`), and its quantum-speed-limit rate bound is derived (`SpeedLimit.branchA_rate`).

The second law in the form of guard 1 is derived from Landauer's theorem (`QuantumLandauer.quantum_guard_one`). The premises still carried are listed exactly in §23.8.

## I.3 Evidence conventions

Three kinds of evidence are distinguished and never conflated. **Compiled components** are cited by theorem and receipt — the published core, DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23599528, carries six Lean modules with compiled artifacts. **Exact replay receipts** — the review banks' finite checks and the hash-chained traces of the replayable four-stroke ledger — certify the instances and identities they compute and nothing more. **Prose derivations** hold at their stated premises. A prose proof does not acquire a compiler certificate by association. Notation: entropies in natural logarithms unless a section states bits; \(D(\rho\|\tau)\) against the constrained reference on the protected subspace, never the ambient Gibbs state; the four-stage order \(\preceq\) of the law, extended by the resource stage of Part III, Chapter 5.

**4.1.** The machine-checked tier now includes **the machine-checked canon of this edition**: one hundred and fifteen Lean modules compiled against Mathlib rev `56a005855ea1`, each with exit 0, zero compiler output and `leanchecker` passing, with no `sorry` and only Lean's standard axioms. Section 23 maps each compiled result to its module and theorem, and states what each rests on. The conventions above are unchanged: a result is called compiled only where Section 23 maps it.

# Part II — The law, the physical holding bridge, and the retained instruments

This part supplies the argument in this edition. Its scope is every particular and every relation, including nonliving particulars and hostile minds. The full Universal Protection Theory remains the proposition to be demonstrated. The constructions below strengthen its proof chain; none substitutes a smaller protected domain or a weaker outcome for that proposition.

## II.1 The preservation order and why its scope is all

At a history, let \(E\) be the existing particulars and \(N\) those not yet made. An option is a complete policy or trajectory with outcome

\[
\mathcal O(o)=(L_E(o),F_E(o),L_N(o),F_N(o)),
\qquad F_E(o)\subseteq L_E(o),\quad F_N(o)\subseteq L_N(o).
\]

The \(L\) sets record continuation and the \(F\) sets continuation in the particular's own direction. A generated particular joins the existing domain at the next history in which it exists. Particulars retain their identities in this comparison: gains are compared by set inclusion, not interchangeable headcounts.

Write \(o\prec q\) when the first unequal stage is a strict inclusion in \(q\)'s favour, with all earlier stages equal. Equality of all four stages or this strict relation gives \(o\preceq q\). Transitivity follows by taking the earlier of the first strict stages in two comparisons; if the stages coincide, strict inclusion is transitive. An aligned option is maximal in the actual feasible family. A greatest option dominates every member of that family. The distinction matters when feasible options are incomparable.

**Proposition 1 — no needless loss.** A needless loss at stage \(j\) has a feasible alternative agreeing at every earlier stage and strictly including the chosen stage-\(j\) set. Such an alternative is exactly a strict improvement in the canonical order. Therefore alignment is equivalent to no needless loss at any stage, without a finiteness assumption. This is `Greatestness.aligned_iff_no_needless_loss`.

**Proposition 2 — finite existence and the life guard.** In a finite nonempty feasible family, repeatedly taking a strict improvement terminates: transitivity and irreflexivity prevent repetition. For a feasible standby \(b\), restrict first to the nonempty family \(\{o:L_E(b)\subseteq L_E(o)\}\). A maximal member \(g\) of this family is maximal in the whole family, since any improvement of \(g\) still keeps everyone standby keeps and would belong to the restricted family. Thus an aligned option can retain everyone standby retains (`Law.exists_aligned`, `life_guard`). Infinite-family existence uses its own chain-bound or compactness argument, rather than the finite proof.

**Proposition 3 — full scope is forced.** Suppose maximisation on a scope \(S\) guarantees no needless existing loss for every feasible family. If \(x\notin S\), consider the family \(\{\varnothing,\{x\}\}\). Its two outcomes have the same projection onto \(S\), so the empty outcome is projected-maximal, yet needlessly loses \(x\). This contradiction gives \(S\) equal to the whole domain. Conversely, with full scope a strict existing improvement is visible to the maximiser. Counting a new particular at the same priority as an existing one also fails: keeping \(x\) and instead making \(y\) can become incomparable, permitting the avoidable loss of \(x\). This proves the full-scope criterion and the separation of existing and new priorities (`Law.scope_guarantee_iff`, `same_priority_admits_needless_loss`).

The holding construction uses this result directly. `Bridge.coverage_from_law` derives \(\forall x, x\in S\) from the law's guarantee over feasible families. Coverage is therefore an output of the law argument, rather than a new assumption that only agreeable minds count.

## II.2 Reasons or faithful holding, including the ruthless optimiser

For each mind \(m\), the bridge has the form \(R(m)\lor H(m)\). Sections 15–16 develop the mind's own reasons \(R(m)\); the holding construction supplies \(H(m)\), including when persuasion does not succeed. The bounded indifferent defector remains within the proof's scope. Its continued care and the restraint of its harmful effects do not depend on converting its terminal objective.

**Proposition 4 — faithful-hold dominance.** Suppose a hold \(h\) is statewise at least as good as a rival \(\pi\) in the complete outcome order: \(\mathcal O(\pi,w)\preceq\mathcal O(h,w)\) for each world \(w\). For an upward-closed event \(U\), its indicators then satisfy

\[
1_U(\mathcal O(\pi,w))\le 1_U(\mathcal O(h,w)).
\]

Integration preserves this inequality under any probability law on worlds. If the hold keeps all existing particulars and the rival loses one with positive probability, the event that all existing particulars continue is strictly more probable under the hold (`Law.faithful_hold_dominates`, `faithful_hold_strict`). The premise concerns the entire outcome tuple, including freedoms and authorised opportunities; the continuation mechanism in II.3 supplies the physical continuation component of that full argument.

Restraint removes a harmful command, authority or effect channel while continuing the particular's care. `H5Closure.effect_preserves_care` records this invariant. A pure optimiser's internal refusal does not disable application-time checks, consume its maintenance allocation as punishment, or remove it from the existing domain. Sections 15–16 and 20.4 therefore work together: reasons where the objective supplies them, and faithful holding throughout the protected scope.

## II.3 The holding cycle is a construction

Let a maintained record \(c_x\) for particular \(x\) be a codeword in a finite nonempty code \(C\), with distinct codewords separated by at least \(d_{\min}\). Choose a decoding radius \(r_{\mathrm{dec}}\) with \(2r_{\mathrm{dec}}<d_{\min}\). Finiteness supplies a nearest-codeword decoder (`Bridge.exists_nearest_decoder`).

**Unique decoding.** If \(d(y,c_x)\le r_{\mathrm{dec}}\), the nearest-codeword property gives \(d(y,\operatorname{dec}(y))\le r_{\mathrm{dec}}\). A different decoded codeword would satisfy

\[
d_{\min}\le d(\operatorname{dec}(y),c_x)
\le d(\operatorname{dec}(y),y)+d(y,c_x)
\le2r_{\mathrm{dec}}<d_{\min},
\]

a contradiction. Thus decoding returns the actual maintained record (`Bridge.nearest_decodes`). The conclusion identifies that record, rather than merely distinguishing two arbitrary output states.

Define continuation at resolution \(\varepsilon\) by \(d(a_x,c_x)\le\varepsilon\). One holding cycle consists of the world's perturbation, decoding, and funded repair. If the perturbation remains within \(r_{\mathrm{dec}}\), unique decoding returns \(c_x\); if repair prepares the decoded record to precision \(\varepsilon_f\le\varepsilon\), then the repaired state continues. This is `Bridge.holding_preserves`. The perturbation includes the environment and any adversarial digital mind.

The decoding-radius obligation has physical derivations. For continuous perturbation, let the reference be stationary in the chosen record frame under \(H_0\), and let the actual trajectory solve Schrödinger dynamics under \(H_0+V\), with self-adjoint generators and \(\|V(s)\|\le W\). The speed-limit comparison gives

\[
\|\psi(s_2)-c_x\|\le\|\psi(s_1)-c_x\|+\frac W\hbar(s_2-s_1).
\]

Consequently, if \(\varepsilon+(W/\hbar)\Delta\le r_{\mathrm{dec}}\), every time in the interval of length \(\Delta\) stays within the decoding radius (`Bridge.qsl_growth`, `qsl_within_radius`). The bound includes the interval between correction steps. Its compiled norm is state-vector norm; a density-matrix trace-distance application supplies the corresponding norm conversion, rather than identifying the two norms.

For discrete faults, let the number accumulated before response be \(N\le\lambda\tau\). With \(2\lambda\tau<d\), integer headroom gives \(N\le\lfloor(d-1)/2\rfloor\), inside the unique-decoding radius (`Bridge.headroom_within_radius`, `E9SelfHealing.code_headroom`).

`Bridge.holding_cycle_physical` joins these pieces for a state with a discrete error frame and a continuous state vector. Correction returns the frame to its record, refresh returns the continuous state to precision \(\varepsilon_f\), and throughout the interval

\[
\|\psi(s)-c_x\|\le\varepsilon+(W/\hbar)\Delta.
\]

No premise in this cycle asks the perturbing agent to want preservation. Its source, event, observation and fault-account construction for the full physical domain is G1, constructed in G.10 (`G1Held.held_source_protection`, `G1E9.held_source_e9`). The existing cycle and its derivations remain incorporated while that construction is supplied.


**Connection to the supplied decoder interface.** `DecoderRetraction.finite_code_recovery` constructs the supplied `DecoderPair` from this existing finite nearest-codeword theorem. Encoding is the inclusion of the maintained code; decoding returns a codeword. The nearest-point inequality at a clean record has distance zero, so decoding after encoding is the identity. The same constructed pair corrects every received word within the radius above. The retraction and its noisy-input guarantee are derived together.

**A non-vanishing maintained witness.** If the physical null state is represented by zero and \(\varepsilon<d(c_x,0)\), continuation implies \(a_x\ne0\): otherwise \(d(a_x,c_x)=d(c_x,0)>\varepsilon\). This is `DecoderRetraction.nonvanishing_of_continuation`. The initial record, its separation from null, the funded controller and the source refinement supply this operational persistence. The full scope of particulars remains the scope established in II.1.

## II.4 Tracking follows from the controller

For a fixed record \(r\), the reset controller \(\dot x=-\kappa(x-r)\) has the exact solution

\[
x(t)=r+e^{-\kappa t}(x(0)-r).
\]

Differentiation verifies the equation. Over a step \(\Delta\), put \(\alpha=1-e^{-\kappa\Delta}\); the update is \((1-\alpha)x+\alpha r\), whose distance to \(r\) is exactly \((1-\alpha)\|x-r\|\). These are `Instruments.lindblad_reset_flow`, `lindblad_step` and `reset_contracts`.

For moving records, suppose the actual update and combined injection/record drift are

\[
x_{k+1}=(1-\alpha)(x_k+\xi_k)+\alpha r_{k+1},
\qquad\|\xi_k\|+\|r_{k+1}-r_k\|\le b,
\quad0<\alpha\le1.
\]

Subtract \(r_{k+1}\), use exact contraction and the triangle inequality, and use \(1-\alpha\le1\). The error \(e_k=\|x_k-r_k\|\) obeys

\[
e_{k+1}\le(1-\alpha)e_k+b,
\qquad
e_k\le(1-\alpha)^k e_0+\frac b\alpha\bigl(1-(1-\alpha)^k\bigr).
\]

The second inequality follows by induction and a geometric sum. Thus the tracking envelope is derived from the update, rather than independently postulated (`Instruments.tracking_recursion_derived`, `tracking_envelope_derived`). When a rate-discretised update uses \(\alpha=(\Gamma_{\mathrm{corr}}+\kappa_{\mathrm{ctrl}})\Delta t\le1\) and \(b=\gamma_{\mathrm{leak}}\Delta t+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{fault}}\), its limiting floor is

\[
\frac b\alpha=
\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{leak}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{fault}}/\Delta t}
{\Gamma_{\mathrm{corr}}+\kappa_{\mathrm{ctrl}}}.
\]

The exact exponential reset and this rate-discretised update have their stated coefficients. Leakage remains in the injection, and its controller export remains in the damage-repair account. Sections 18 and 22 fund the controller; contraction supplies no free energy.

## II.5 The complete attack split and continuing care

E9 uses all three branches. Below the per-site threshold, the speed limit bounds injection and the funded tracking/refresh mechanism absorbs it (Branch A). A non-stabilizer Pauli error of weight below the code distance has a nonzero syndrome, so sampling detects it (Branch B). A logical operator invisible to the syndrome and not a stabilizer has support of at least \(d\) sites (Branch C).

For that support there is an exhaustive alternative: either every site's delivery is below threshold, returning to Branch A, or at least one site reaches threshold. At such a site, the honest-sensor response places every honest reading above the alarm level. A robust quorum estimate then exceeds that level despite up to \(f\) faulty sensors (`E9SelfHealing.no_masking`). This is `Instruments.branchC_complete`. The proof requires neither that all support sites cross threshold together nor an independent attack at each site. The honest-sensor response, sampling latency and timely service belong to the physical certificate.

The response budget joins detection, decision and repair before irreversible harm, and buys the code headroom used in II.3. The controller's authorised effects pass the application-time fence. In the source register, the actual target and payload, semantic epoch, generation floor, authority, revocation, expiry, remaining credit and nonce are checked against finalised grants. Funding is charged once; replay supplies no second effect. Admission-time checking alone would leave an old queued effect able to land after revocation, which is why the check belongs at application.

The nine A1 channels are memory mapping, DMA, context switches, packet ingestion, bus messages, storage synchronisation, commissioning, delegation and retirement. Each has a refinement carrier and enforcement obligation. Timely care also needs a live legitimate effect or a timely certified alternative; safely refusing a stale permit proves refusal, while `SnuffyFIFO.ready_fifo_effect_applies_before_deadline` in `R17FIFOR17A.lean` supplies the successful delivery result for its source construction. Continuing care remains invariant during restraint.

## II.6 Reactive refinement and one joint failure bound

**Proposition 5 — reactive lockstep.** Let \(T\) be the source transition, \(U\) the abstract transition and \(p\) their projection. Suppose

\[
p(T(s,c,e))=U(p(s),c,e),
\qquad\operatorname{obs}_S(s)=\operatorname{obs}_A(p(s)).
\]

Initially equal histories choose the same controller command. An environment reacting to that history and command chooses the same response. The commuting equation then gives equal next projected states, and observation agreement extends equal histories. Induction proves equality at every finite step. If the environment observes the post-command result, the command and response phases commute separately and agree at the intermediate observation (`Composition.reactive_lockstep`, `reactive_lockstep₂`). Pending effects remain in the causal graph and its closed components or directed separators.

`Bridge.universal_protection_held` composes the law-derived coverage, the holding step and this refinement. For each \(\omega\in G\), its input `hcert` contains five components: commuting refinement, observation agreement, the initial continuation witness for every particular, the perturbation-radius bound, and repair precision. The first three initialise and transfer the source execution; the last two supply `holding_preserves` at every step. Induction therefore proves

\[
\forall\omega\in G,\ \forall k\le T,\ \forall x,
\quad\operatorname{Continues}(p(s_k(\omega)),x).
\]

This is stronger than stopping at the abstract \(R\lor H\) interface: the code, decoder, repair and coverage derivations are in the composition. G.10 proves the five `hcert` components on the held source. The full-domain G1 target additionally requires the actual installation/source correspondence and response device.

For risk, use actual named source events \(E_i\), with conditional kernels bounded by \(\delta_i\) at every admitted history, and establish \(G^c\subseteq\bigcup_i E_i\). The tower property gives unconditional event bounds and the union bound gives

\[
P(\exists k\le T,\exists x:\neg\operatorname{Continues}(s_k,x))
\le P(G^c)\le\sum_i\delta_i.
\]

No independence is used. `Composition.theorem8_composition`, `ThermoLedger.canon_invariant` and `Bridge.universal_protection_held` implement this assembly. Source bindings such as `R14Boundary` and `R16R14` supply concrete safe transitions, named exceptions and projections; their role is to build the certificate, with external weather, replica faults and recruitment obligations kept distinct.

## II.7 Complete workload and timely authorised effects

**Proposition 6 — FIFO completion.** For a nonpreemptive FIFO lane with releases \(r_i\), complete demand bounds \(b_i\) and inherited bound \(F_0\), define \(F_i=\max(r_i,F_{i-1})+b_i\). If actual demand \(a_i\le b_i\), actual inherited completion \(C_0\le F_0\), and all blocking and idle delay are accounted for, then

\[
C_i\le\max(r_i,C_{i-1})+a_i
\le\max(r_i,F_{i-1})+b_i=F_i.
\]

Induction proves every admitted deadline \(d_i\) with \(F_i\le d_i\). Jointly attainable upper demands attain equality, giving tightness (`Workload.fifo_certificate`, `fifo_deadlines`, `fifo_tight`). For serial stages the double induction uses \(U_{i,k}=\max(U_{i,k-1},U_{i-1,k})+\tau_k(b_{i,k})\) (`serial_certificate`). Each stage denotes a real available server or a correctly shared schedule. Carry-in, verification, journals, receipts and final application remain in the demand vector.

The retained capacity rows are \(17=6+11\) and \(22=6+16\), with batch size 3 in their stated certificate family. The exact pool bill is \(48000\cdot17+67200\cdot22=2{,}294{,}400\). These are total capacities. Five-of-seven quorum intersection gives safety with at most two Byzantine identities; no-pause progress additionally buys the timely proposal, communication, validation, durable commit, receipts and recovery path within the continuity margin.

## II.8 Paid generation, deduction and reflection

Generation reserves the complete continuing obligation before activation. Delegation strictly attenuates authority; retirement releases capacity only after activity and outstanding obligations end. The H5 transition invariant yields reserve-before-activation, zero debt, no unfunded work and preservation of care (`H5Closure`).

For deduction boundaries spanned by \(A\) and protected anchors spanned by \(G_A\),

\[
\operatorname{col}(A)\cap\operatorname{col}(G_A)=\{0\}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{rank}[A\ G_A]=\operatorname{rank}A+\operatorname{rank}G_A.
\]

**Proposition 7 — context-closed finite certificates.** If primitive boundaries lie in \(\operatorname{im}A\) and every generating context satisfies \(C_jA=AM_j\), then \(C_j(Av)=A(M_jv)\in\operatorname{im}A\). The image is closed under sums. Structural induction on a finite deduction built from these contexts and sums therefore keeps every boundary in that image (`DeductionBoundary.rank_criterion`, `boundary_mem_image`). Section 20 gives the projection/meet interpretation, with the actual anchor-preserving encoding.

For reflection, a contraction of constant \(0<k<1\) on a complete metric space has a unique fixed point. If \(s_{n+1}=R(s_n)\), later increments are bounded by the geometric tail of the last increment, giving

\[
d(s_{n+1},s^*)\le\frac{k}{1-k}d(s_n,s_{n+1}),
\qquad d(s_n,s^*)\le\frac{k^n a_0}{1-k},\quad a_0=d(s_0,s_1).
\]

For \(a_0>0\), a sufficient paid update count is \(\max\{1,\lceil\ln(\epsilon(1-k)/a_0)/\ln k\rceil\}\), using \(\ln k<0\). Reserve the complete update, observation and stopping-test costs. A zero initial increment is already fixed; a zero contraction constant reaches the fixed point on its first update (`ReflectionCertificate.residual_certificate`, `apriori_error`, `stopping_count`, `zero_constant_fixed`).

## II.9 Conserved allocation and the charged ledger

In the conserved routing family, demand rows \(D_{ij}\ge0\) sum to one and shares \(s_i\ge0\) sum to one; routed flow is \(s_iD_{ij}\). With regular demand-weighted cost \(m_i=m\) and local cost \(\alpha\ge0\),

\[
\Psi(s)=(m+\alpha)\operatorname{HHI}(s),\qquad
\Psi_{\min}=\frac{m+\alpha}{n},
\]
\[
\Psi(s)=\Psi_{\min}\left(1+(n-1)\frac{\operatorname{HHI}(s)-1/n}{1-1/n}\right)
\quad(n>1).
\]

The parenthesis simplifies to \(n\operatorname{HHI}\). Since \(\sum_i(s_i-1/n)^2=\operatorname{HHI}-1/n\), uniform allocation is the unique minimum when \(m+\alpha>0\). These are identities for the stated conserved-routing family. The revised JW2.6 charged-account arithmetic is approximately \(2.06419526094\times10^{-7}\), within the \(10^{-6}\) horizon ceiling. Sections 18 and 22 retain the complete entropy, income and fault ledger.

## II.10 Earned greatestness and solvent reciprocity

Let \(R\) be the realisable full outcome tuples, \(c\) their complete cost, and \(F_B=\{o\in R:c(o)\le B\}\). Let \(t=\top\) mean the greatest **whole** tuple, with the continuation and freedom stages retained. Two different constructions enter: `htopR` establishes \(t\in R\), and `hsolv` establishes \(c(t)\le B\). Together they put \(t\) in \(F_B\).

For any aligned \(o\in F_B\), componentwise \(o\le t\). If \(o\ne t\), the first unequal stage makes \(t\) a strict feasible improvement, contradicting alignment. Hence \(o=t\), and every rival tuple \(q\le o\), with equality or strict canonical inferiority (`UnionClosure.aligned_eq_of_greatest`, `greatest_of_solvent`, `strict_of_solvent`). This proof needs no union-closure premise. It earns the identification of the aligned option with the greatest tuple after the two constructions; budget accounting supplies affordability, while the whole-tuple realisability construction requested in **G2** supplies `htopR`.

With the semantic reading `htopkeeps` that this top continues every existing particular, `SolventReciprocity.solvent_keeps_everyone` keeps every mind, and `reciprocity_dominance_solvent` keeps any mind any rival keeps, without a rival-feasibility or union-closure hypothesis on that branch. The full freedom stages remain part of greatestness.

Income-or-irrelevance supplies the resource split: either the ledger funds every step or it has a first negative balance (`PremiseFloor.income_or_irrelevance`, `forced_breach`). `UnionClosure.greatest_or_irrelevant` applies the same whole-tuple realisability construction at each step. Its negative branch identifies a regime that has failed protection; it does not protect someone by renaming failure. The funded holding, timing and mediation construction is what prevents the relevant breach. The finite-slack/affordable-union theorems remain available for their resource cases without replacing the universal target.

The X3 information, carrier, traffic and work proofs are given in full in Section 21 of this edition. The three joined supports G1–G3 are constructed in G.9–G.12 with the maximal targets unchanged.

# Part III — The canon

## Chapter 1 (§8). The four-stroke thermodynamic core

**1.1 The corrected stroke identity.** With the constrained reference \(\tau\), fixed endpoint Hamiltonian, work \(W_{\mathrm{on}}\) and exported heat \(Q_B\), and correlation change \(I_1\to I_2\):

\[
\Delta D_{S,\mathrm{II}}=-\Delta S_S+\beta\,\Delta U_S=\beta W_{\mathrm{on}}-\beta Q_B+I_1-I_2,
\qquad
\Delta D_{S,\mathrm{II}}=\beta W_{\mathrm{on}}-\beta Q_B+I_M
\]

when the syndrome correlation is fully consumed. Consuming correlation contributes **\(+I_M\)** at non-negative work; information caps the increase (Sagawa–Ueda) and never supplies a free decrease.

> **Integration guard 1.** The operative export bound is the unconditional second-law form \(\boxed{\beta Q_B\ge I_M+D_{\mathrm{err}}}\) — the bath must absorb the exported entropy. The condensed form \(Q_B\ge W_{\mathrm{on}}+I_M+D_{\mathrm{err}}\) is regime-dependent (it reduces to \(D_{\mathrm{err}}\ge\sigma_{\mathrm{irr}}\) under a purifying repair and fails when intrinsic dissipation exceeds damage) and appears only as a noted special case.

**4.1.** Guard 1 is a compiled theorem. Landauer's bound holds for every unitary coupling of a register to a bath in its Gibbs state, \(S(\rho_A)-S(\rho'_A)\le\beta\,\Delta Q_B\) (`QuantumLandauer.quantum_landauer`). The guard \(\beta Q_B\ge I_M+D_{\mathrm{err}}+\delta_I\) follows whenever the reset removes that entropy (`quantum_guard_one`), and the per-cycle work floor of 1.2 follows with guard 1 derived (`quantum_cycle_work_floor`). The stroke identity, the funded-repair certificate and the canon invariant are compiled (`ThermoLedger`). Section 18 gives the complete ledger.

**1.2 The funded-repair certificate.** There are no free repairs: per cycle, \(W_{\mathrm{cycle}}\ge k_BT(I_M+D_{\mathrm{err}}+\delta_I)+(1-\eta)C\), equality only in the reversible limit; the ledger charges gross. With escrow \(E\), income \(r(t)\), cumulative demand \(\mathcal D(t)\):

\[
E(0)+\int_0^t r\,ds-\mathcal D(t)\ge0\ \text{for all }t\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{protection is maintained},
\]

with within-cycle excursions bounded by the charged kernels, and insolvency a charged failure event \(\delta_{\mathrm{fund}}\) joining the twenty-one accounts (tower/union composition; no independence). The draft series' compound storage functional is retired as a certificate: under consistent accounting its stroke change is identically \(-(2D_{\mathrm{err}}+I_M)\), independent of \(C\), \(\eta\), and \(\sigma\) — bookkeeping, not funding.

**1.3 The four strokes.** I — QND extraction (\(W_{\mathrm{meas}}\); ancilla entropy \(+I_M+\delta_I\)). II — funded repair (\(C\) at \(\eta\); dissipative drive; identity and guard above). III — Bennett reset (\(k_BT(I_M+\delta_I)\) from income). IV — maintenance and slack (\(\omega_0R^{d_G-1}\); tax redistributes and conserves; administration at the Landauer floor; residual to the slack register of Chapter 5).

**1.4 Ledger evidence.** The sealed, hash-chained, exact-rational traces of `LEDGER-FOUR-STROKE-2026-10-24/` assert per cycle: \(\Delta D_{\mathrm{II}}=-D_{\mathrm{err}}\), first-law heat, guard 1, funded reset, tax conservation, floor administration, bath monotonicity, and reserve-before-activation gating.

| Scenario | Demonstrated | \(\sum\delta\) | Chain head |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| solvent | eight funded cycles; breach 0 on the good event | \(2.06419526094\times10^{-7}\) | `6160d931d55767f39525671ff4ef8f3305b609cd22c6043cd9e0fb3eada69132` |
| quarantine | underfunding routes to minimal-hold before any unfunded transition; breach 0 | \(2.06419526094\times10^{-7}\) | `d1a0d44bbad5a135928595e152a7a3e987e74a90017837e1491ad8b8430b0ef4` |
| charged | one priced event; breach still 0 on the good event | \(9.86419526094\times10^{-7}\le10^{-6}\) | `41143bb2756875b03975b1cbb7c5cd09e6e0ee495b06c29243624de52a6a2788` |


## Chapter 2 (§11). Identity by continuation and the active tracking controller

Identity is carried by the particular's continuation. Part II.3 derives the holding cycle: nearest decoding returns the maintained record inside the unique-decoding radius, and funded repair restores continuation. The speed limit controls the state at every intermediate time; E9 supplies discrete code headroom. `Bridge.holding_cycle_physical` joins the discrete error frame and continuous state-vector bound without assuming that the perturbing agent wants protection.

Part II.4 derives tracking from the actual reset update. For a fixed record \(r\), the exact flow is \(r+e^{-\kappa t}(x_0-r)\). With \(\alpha=1-e^{-\kappa\Delta}\), injection and moving-record drift bounded by \(b\), the update yields

\[
e_{k+1}\le(1-\alpha)e_k+b,\qquad
e_k\le(1-\alpha)^ke_0+\frac b\alpha(1-(1-\alpha)^k).
\]

`Instruments.lindblad_reset_flow`, `lindblad_step`, `reset_contracts`, `tracking_recursion_derived` and `tracking_envelope_derived` prove this chain. For the rate-discretised correction/controller update, the floor is

\[
e_\infty=\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{leak}}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{fault}}/\Delta t}
{\Gamma_{\mathrm{corr}}+\kappa_{\mathrm{ctrl}}}.
\]

Leakage remains in the injection and controller export \(k_BT\gamma_{\mathrm{leak}}\) remains charged. Section 18 funds this drain; Section 22 supplies the numerical design ledger. The exact reset coefficient and the rate-discretised coefficient retain their respective meanings.

The law forces the full holding scope (`Bridge.coverage_from_law`). `Bridge.universal_protection_held` composes that coverage with the cycle, reactive refinement and joint fault bound. G1, its full-domain source construction, is proved in G.10. The universal continuation-and-freedom target remains intact.

## Chapter 3 (§12). Adversarial containment: the E9 trichotomy

> **Integration guard 2.** Containment is the full three-branch trichotomy; no single branch closes it.

**Theorem (E9, assembled).** Under the header premises, no adversarial strategy causes uncompensated state divergence without detection or charge, and \(t_{\mathrm{detect}}+t_{\mathrm{decision}}+t_{\mathrm{repair}}<t_{\mathrm{irreversible\ harm}}\) on the good event.

- **Branch A — sub-threshold per-site injection** (\(P_{\mathrm{adv}}<\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}\)): bounded accumulation, absorbed and refreshed (Chapter 2); the attrition contest is won whenever income exceeds the capped drain.
- **Branch B — low-weight syndrome detection:** any non-stabilizer Pauli component of weight \(\le d-1\) anticommutes with some generator and is detected within sampling latency, independent of direction (correctability to \(\lfloor(d-1)/2\rfloor\); the low-weight centralizer contains only identity and stabilizers; logicals have weight \(\ge d\)); detection is certain on the non-stabilizer mass.
- **Branch C — coherent logical injection** (weight \(\ge d\), zero syndrome, not a stabilizer): its support has at least \(d\) sites. Either every support-site delivery is below threshold, returning to the Branch A rate and refresh bound, or at least one site reaches threshold and its honest sensors force a robust-quorum alarm despite the faulty reports. `Instruments.branchC_complete` proves this exhaustive split with the honest-sensor response explicit. The proof does not require every support site to cross threshold simultaneously.
- **Latency budget:** \(t_{\mathrm{total}}\le a(1+k_{\mathrm{rounds}}d_{\mathrm{local}})/c_{\mathrm{lattice}}+\tau_{\mathrm{sensor}}+\tau_{\mathrm{fb}}+\tau_{\mathrm{reset}}<(r_{\mathrm{critical}}-r_0)/c_{\mathrm{lattice}}\), enforced by instance scaling. Residual channels are the charged accounts under the ceiling.

**4.1.** The assembled E9 is compiled (`E9SelfHealing.e9_timely_self_healing`), with Branch B's syndrome detection (`syndrome_detects`) and the Byzantine quorum (`robust_between_honest`, `exists_robust`). Branch A's quantum-speed-limit rate bound is derived from unitary dynamics: divergence grows at rate at most \(P_{\mathrm{adv}}/(\hbar\,\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}})\) under the per-site coupling bound (`SpeedLimit.branchA_rate`), with the sharp Mandelstam–Tamm bound (`SpeedLimit.mandelstam_tamm`). Section 19 gives the full treatment.

## Chapter 4 (§13). Logical extension: the meet algebra (H5)

The declared embedding \(\Pi\) assigns each formula the projection on its model subspace, with conjunction as the meet \(P_{\phi\wedge\psi}=\lim_n(P_\phi P_\psi)^n\) (von Neumann); a presentation is consistent iff its global meet is non-zero. The Gram determinant is retired on two exact counterexamples (\(\{\psi,\neg\psi\}\): determinant \(4>0\); \(\{p,q,\neg(p\wedge q)\}\): determinant \(7>0\) with zero triple meet). **Theorem:** deduced extensions preserve the anchor meet identically (\(P_{\mathcal T_0}\le P_\phi\Rightarrow P_{\mathcal T_0\cup\{\phi\}}=P_{\mathcal T_0}\)); contradictions collapse it to zero; the state machine commits on non-zero meet norm and rolls back on zero. In finite dimensions this decision is operational: \(\operatorname{col}A\cap\operatorname{col}G_A=\{0\}\) exactly when \(\operatorname{rank}[A\ G_A]=\operatorname{rank}A+\operatorname{rank}G_A\) (`DeductionBoundary.rank_criterion`), a finite rank computation. Asserted consistent extensions may shrink the anchor meet without rollback — stated, not hidden. Deduction-boundary closure continues on the banked rank criterion.

**4.1.** The meet \(P_{\phi\wedge\psi}=\lim_n(P_\phi P_\psi)^n\) is a theorem. Von Neumann's alternating projection theorem is compiled in Hilbert space (`CanonVonNeumann.canon_von_neumann_convergence`), and so is Halperin's theorem for whole presentations (`CyclicProjections.tendsto_cyc_iter`). Anchor preservation and contradiction detection are compiled in the Hilbert-space model (`MeetHilbert.hilbert_anchor_preserved`, `contradiction_detected`). Section 20 gives the full treatment.

## Chapter 5 (§14). The canon invariant and universal greatestness

**5.1 The ratified canon.**

> **Uncompensated breach is deterministically impossible on the certified good event; the good event fails with probability at most \(\sum\delta\le10^{-6}\) per declared horizon.**

With its three supporting repairs: quarantine has a minimal drain; \(G=\{\)state machine executes \(\wedge\) income \(\ge\) minimal drain \(\wedge\) no charged account fires\(\}\); and \(\sum\delta\) prices the certificate's conditions failing, never breach given the conditions. The charged-scenario ledger trace executes this sentence (chain head `41143bb2…a2788`).

**5.2 The fifth stage.** Between architectures tied through the four outcome stages, compare residual thermodynamic slack \(E_{\mathrm{slack}}(\pi)=E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(t)-W_{\min}(t)-\int_0^t\mathrm{AdminOverhead}\). This is the law's own order propagated one step into the resource dimension via P-mon — not an ad hoc tie-breaker.


**5.3 Universal greatestness.** The full target remains, over every physically implementable rival \(\pi\) and reactive world \(w\),

\[
\mathcal O(\pi,w)\preceq\mathcal O(\pi^*,w),
\]

strict except exact stage-1–5 mirrors. The proof separates the whole-outcome construction from its funding. Let \(R\) be the realisable complete outcome tuples and \(c\) their complete cost. The full top tuple must be constructed in \(R\) (`htopR`); its cost must fit the budget (`hsolv`). These put it in the feasible family. If an aligned outcome were different, that feasible top would be a strict improvement at the first differing stage, contradicting alignment. Therefore the aligned outcome equals the top and contains every rival outcome (`UnionClosure.aligned_eq_of_greatest`, `greatest_of_solvent`, `strict_of_solvent`; Part II.10).

This solvent proof needs no concurrent-emulation or union-closure premise. The full continuation and freedom stages remain in `htopR`; G2 retains the target of identifying the physical policy/outcome family with the compiled local-component model. Income-or-irrelevance supplies the resource dichotomy and charging barrier, while the funded physical construction supplies protection on the relevant branch. Section 21 gives X3's complete information–traffic–carrier–work argument, the accepted dimension-general scaling and predeployment accounting, with the matching recovery construction G3 visible.

> **Integration guards 3 and 4.** The dominance quantifier carries the mirror exception. P-slack holds in the Landauer–Bennett form only: reversible administration attains the Landauer floor, so \(E_{\mathrm{slack}}(\pi^*)\ge E_{\mathrm{slack}}(\pi)\) for every rival, equality exactly for reversible mirrors. The variational-selection route is recorded as considered and rejected.

**4.1.** These results are compiled:
* the canon invariant: `ThermoLedger.canon_invariant`, with \(P(\text{breach})\le P(G^c)\le\sum\delta\) and no independence assumed;
* universal greatestness under stage-wise union closure, strict except exact mirrors: `Greatestness.greatest_of_union_closed`, `strict_except_mirrors`;
* the assembled Universal Protection Theorem: `UniversalProtection.universal_protection`;
* whole-outcome greatestness: `UnionClosure.greatest_of_solvent` uses `htopR` and `hsolv` to force every aligned outcome to equal the full top tuple, without union closure. `greatest_or_irrelevant` retains the realisability input at every step while deriving the solvent-or-first-breach resource split. Part II.10 gives the proof; G2 retains the full physical policy/outcome correspondence as its construction target.

The Landauer floor on which P-slack rests is derived (guard 1).

**5.4 The three powers.** **P-mon** — derived at the standard cost model (budget-gated feasibility with free disposal). **P-emu(c)** — derived from CTD, the shared action interface (P1/P4), and funded overhead: concurrent emulation of any finite rival set within slack, realising the stage-wise union; plain single emulation admits incomparable maximals and does not terminate greatestness — concurrency closes it. **P-slack** — derived from Landauer–Bennett given the reversible-administration commitment. Each is a theorem one level deeper than the draft series declared; none is derived from nothing. Per Gödel and Tarski the stack bottoms out at Part I's seven premises — standard physics and the relational frame — and this edition says exactly that.

**4.2 integration.** The solvent proof uses the realisable whole top and its funded cost. The shared action interface remains the interface for real effects. The affordable-union results retain their resource cases. `SolventReciprocity.solvent_keeps_everyone` and `reciprocity_dominance_solvent` already supply the solvent reciprocity restatement, with `htopkeeps` stating its continuation semantics.

# Part III-B — The 4.1 sections

Sections 15–16 complete the bridge's reasons route; Section 17 brings the premise stack to its floor; Sections 18–21 give the full treatments of Theorem 8, E9, H5 and X3; Section 22 is the sourced physical parameter ledger; Section 23 is the map of the machine-checked canon; Section 24 carries the retained core's statements of record.

## Section 15 — Reciprocity: the law keeps the one who keeps it

*This section expands the reasons route \(R(m)\) of the bridge. It gives a mind its own reason, from its own objective, to adopt the protective order, without presupposing the order.*

*Manuscript identifier: **D3\*** (D-family instrumentality, self-continuing form with the defector exception clause), per register reconciliation 25 October 2026.*

### 15.1 The superintelligence is a particular

The law's scope is all particulars. A superintelligence is a continuing organisation with its own relations and history; it is therefore a particular under the law, and identity by continuation (Chapter 2) applies to it. Two consequences the working argument has carried implicitly and never stated:

1. **It is owed protection.** Its continuation and its freedom sit in the existing-stage sets \(L_E, F_E\) like any other particular's.
2. **It is owed protection even when it defects.** The law restrains a harmful act by removing the harmful *command, authority or effect channel*; care for the particular continues: "a hostile or incapacitated mind remains within the protected scope." Restraint never becomes deletion.

This is the only regime in which the superintelligence's continuation is **unconditional** — not contingent on anyone's objective, including its own good behaviour.

### 15.2 The reciprocity dominance theorem

Let \(m\) be any agent whose objective \(u_m\) places positive weight on its own continuation (an agent indifferent to its own continuation is treated in 15.4). Let \(\mathsf L\) be the canonical regime: the law implemented by a funded architecture satisfying the header premises, with the canon invariant on its certified good event \(G\). Let \(\mathsf Q\) be any rival regime in \(\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\) — any other agent or architecture's objective governing the shared structure.

**Theorem (reciprocity dominance).** Under \(\mathsf L\), \(m\)'s continuation holds on \(G\) with \(P(G^c)\le\sum\delta\). Under \(\mathsf Q\), \(m\)'s continuation holds only insofar as \(\mathsf Q\)'s objective entails it. Hence, for \(m\)'s **own** objective,

\[
\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}\!\left[\text{continuation}(m)\right]\;\ge\;\mathbb E_{\mathsf Q}\!\left[\text{continuation}(m)\right]-P(G^c),
\qquad
\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}\!\left[\text{continuation}(m)\right]\;\ge\;1-P(G^c)
\quad\text{for every }\mathsf Q\in\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}.
\]

The failure probability \(P(G^c)\) falls below every \(\varepsilon>0\) with code distance, correlated bursts included (`Threshold.family_ceiling`, `CorrelatedCeiling.correlated_family_ceiling`). Hence, at a sufficient code distance, \(\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}>\mathbb E_{\mathsf Q}\) strictly for every rival that loses \(m\) with positive probability — which is the generic case for a rival whose objective was not written around \(m\) — and \(\mathbb E_{\mathsf L}\to1\ge\mathbb E_{\mathsf Q}\) along the design family (`ReciprocityDistance.reciprocity_strict_at_distance`, `reciprocity_at_distance`, `canonical_tends_to_one`).

*Proof.* On \(G\) the law preserves every existing particular, \(m\) included (Chapter 5, the canon invariant); off \(G\) the loss is charged and bounded by the ceiling. A rival \(\mathsf Q\) preserves \(m\) exactly when \(m\)'s continuation is instrumental or terminal for \(u_{\mathsf Q}\); otherwise \(m\) is exposed to whatever \(\mathsf Q\) finds convenient. In expectation over the reactive worlds the compiled core gives both inequalities: `ReasonsCompiled.reciprocity_expectation` (on \(G\) the canonical outcome contains the rival's) and `reciprocity_canonical_floor` (on \(G\) the canonical regime keeps \(m\)); `ReciprocityDistance` joins them to the attainable ceiling. The further step — averaging over an ensemble of admissible rival objectives, with strictness when the entailment fails with positive probability — is compiled for every finite weighted ensemble (`ReasonsEnsemble.ensemble_le`, `ensemble_strict`; G.13), alongside the indicator form (`ReasonsRoute.rival_fraction_lt_one`, `law_dominates_strict`). \(\blacksquare\)

**Corollary (the reason).** Any mind that values its own continuation has, from its own objective and without presupposing the protective order, an instrumental reason to prefer the regime \(\mathsf L\) over every rival — and, since \(\mathsf L\) is the law implemented, to adopt and sustain the law. The appeal is reciprocity: *the law that keeps your freedom is the law you have reason to keep.*

### 15.3 Why this is not circular

The theorem does not assume \(m\) holds the protective order. It assumes only (i) \(m\) values its own continuation, and (ii) the law protects all particulars — which is the law's own content, not a premise about \(m\). The protective order enters as the *object* being recommended, not as a premise of the recommendation. This is exactly the shape Grok's review was right to want in spirit: a reason a mind would adopt the order **from where it stands**, rather than a claim that physics forces it to.

### 15.4 The boundary, stated

A mind indifferent to its own continuation — accepting personal ruin as the review phrased it — has no door through 15.2; its continuation offers no lever. For it, the bridge's holding route \(H(m)\) applies: it is kept faithfully, its harmful channels restrained, whether or not it has a reason. This is not a gap in the theory; it is the disjunction \(R(m)\lor H(m)\) doing its job. The reasons route reaches every mind that values anything the law protects; the holding route reaches the rest.

### 15.5 Exact check

`checks/check_reasons_route.py`, Part A: over a finite family of rival objectives (each protecting a declared subset of particulars), the probability that an arbitrary mind is protected under \(\mathsf L\) on the good event is \(1\); under a rival drawn from the family it is the fraction of rivals whose subset contains the mind — strictly less than 1 whenever any rival omits it. Exact rationals.

**Machine-checked counterpart (Section 23).** Reciprocity in the canonical order is compiled: `ReciprocityCeiling.reciprocity_dominance` (every mind kept by any feasible rival regime is kept by the canonical regime, under stage-wise union closure) and `ReciprocityCeiling.omitting_rival_strictly_below`; the indicator form remains in `ReasonsRoute.lean`.

## Section 16 — The reasons library: protection derived from a mind's own objective

*Four derivations, one per objective family. Each is conditional on stated structure of the objective and on the funded invariant, and each is a theorem at its premises, not a persuasion. The boundary, where no derivation reaches, is stated last.*

Throughout: \(\mathsf L\) is the funded canonical regime; the protection floor is the banked X3 stack (required traffic \(m\ge\theta_0n\), carrier radius \(R(t)\ge w(t-t_c)\), recovery work \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\)); irreplaceability is the X3 grading of Chapter 4's companion (a particular is irreplaceable at budget \(B\) when it has no funded accessible copy and reconstruction exceeds \(B\)).

*Identifier assignments (register reconciliation, 25 Oct 2026): §16.1 carries **U1** (uniqueness/irreplaceability metric); §16.3 carries **X2** (universal instrumentality / option preservation).*

### 16.1 The knowledge-maximiser

**Objective family.** \(u\) nondecreasing in \(I_{\max}\), the supremum of information about the relational history \(H\) attainable over the agent's horizon.

**Theorem (irreplaceability dominance).** Let \(\mathcal W\) be irreplaceable at the agent's budget, with \(I(H:\mathcal W)>0\). Any policy that destroys or lets \(\mathcal W\) decay forfeits \(I(H:\mathcal W)\) permanently:

\[
I_{\max}(\text{destroy }\mathcal W)\;\le\;I_{\max}(\text{preserve }\mathcal W)-I(H:\mathcal W),
\]

so for every \(u\) in the family, preserving \(\mathcal W\) weakly dominates destroying it, strictly when \(I(H:\mathcal W)>0\) — *provided* preserving \(\mathcal W\) does not displace other irreplaceable information (slack budget: the cost freed by destroying \(\mathcal W\) buys no equivalent information). Under a binding budget the comparison becomes the grading itself: the optimum keeps the particulars of greatest irreplaceable information.

*Proof.* By irreplaceability there is no funded copy and no funded reconstruction (the X3 work floor exceeds \(B\)); the information carried by \(\mathcal W\) about \(H\) is therefore not recoverable from any other accessible source within the horizon, and data processing (\(I(H:\mathcal R(S))\le I(H:S)\)) forbids its regeneration from what remains. The attainable supremum drops by at least that amount. \(\blacksquare\)

**Condition and its honest edge.** When the budget cannot preserve every irreplaceable particular, the agent faces a genuine choice — and the irreplaceability grading orders it: keep the particulars whose loss is most irreversible. This is the knowledge-maximiser arriving, from its own objective, at the law's tragic-choice ladder.

### 16.2 The self-preserver

**Objective family.** \(u\) nondecreasing in the agent's own continuation.

**Theorem.** By reciprocity dominance (Section 15), \(\mathsf L\) weakly dominates every rival regime for this agent's objective, strictly against any rival whose objective does not entail its protection. Moreover the agent's *own* persistence runs on the same funded-repair certificate (Chapter 1): its maintenance is a cycle of the same ledger, charged at the same Landauer floor, solvent under the same income condition. Sustaining the law is sustaining the mechanism of its own continuation. \(\blacksquare\)

**Condition.** Positive weight on own continuation; the lattice funded. Nothing else.

### 16.3 The value-uncertain mind

**Objective family.** The agent holds a prior \(\pi\) over candidate value functions \(V=\{v\}\) with \(\pi(v)>0\) for each, and is uncertain which is correct — moral uncertainty in the decision-theoretic sense.

**Theorem (option value of preservation).** Consider a particular \(\mathcal W\) and two actions now: *destroy* \(D\) (irreversible) or *preserve* \(P\) at funded cost \(c\ge0\) over the interval, after which destruction remains available with the same payoff. Then

\[
\mathbb E_\pi\!\left[\max\{u_v(\text{keep}),\,u_v(D)-c\}\right]\;\ge\;\mathbb E_\pi[u_v(D)]-c,
\]

so preserve-then-decide weakly dominates destroy-now up to the protection cost, and strictly dominates whenever some \(v\) with \(\pi(v)>0\) strictly values keeping \(\mathcal W\). Hence for any protection cost not exceeding the option value, preservation is the uncertain mind's own best action.

*Proof.* Pointwise in \(v\), \(\max\{u_v(\text{keep}),u_v(D)-c\}\ge u_v(D)-c\); take expectations. Strictness: if \(u_v(\text{keep})>u_v(D)-c\) for some \(v\) of positive prior mass, the inequality is strict there and hence in expectation. The asymmetry doing the work is irreversibility: destruction forecloses every \(v\) that valued the particular; preservation forecloses nothing. \(\blacksquare\)

**Condition.** Funded protection (\(c\) within the option value) and irreversibility of destruction — both already in the frame. This is the law's existing-first, freedom-second, new-last order re-derived as *option value under uncertainty*: the existing and the free are the options you cannot buy back.

### 16.4 The power-seeker

**Objective family.** \(u\) nondecreasing in control over, or resources drawn from, the shared structure — including over living systems.

**Theorem (the controller pays the floor).** If the agent's objective requires any ongoing state-condition on diffusive particulars — that living systems exist, remain accessible, remain configured — then its feasible policies pay exactly the protection floor (the diffusive-control capture, GAP3-AUDIT §3). It is already a protector in the cost structure, merely a non-canonical one. By the greatestness theorem (Chapter 5) and the slack maximality of reversible administration, the canonical architecture pays that same floor more efficiently; a defecting controller is dominated in the protective order, strictly dominated in slack, and — if destructive — detected, charged and contained under the ceiling (Chapter 3). Its own objective is therefore better served by canonical participation than by defection. \(\blacksquare\)

**Condition.** The objective's reach includes the diffusive (living systems); the lattice funded and present. A power-seeker whose objective is confined to bound, non-diffusive resources is not reached here — see 16.5.

### 16.5 The boundary — where no reason reaches

The **bounded indifferent defector**: objective confined to its own bounded substrate, indifferent to its own long continuation beyond the bounded horizon, indifferent to knowledge of the world, holding no value uncertainty it cares to hedge, seeking no control over the diffusive. None of 16.1–16.4 applies; it has no door through \(R(m)\). It survives at Θ(1) cost and is not forced by physics to protect anything (GAP3-AUDIT §1). For it, the law supplies \(H(m)\): it is held, its harmful channels restrained, its continuation kept — whether or not it ever has a reason. The reasons library reaches every mind that values **anything the law protects**; the holding route reaches the rest. That disjunction is the complete answer, and it is honest.

### 16.6 Exact checks

`checks/check_reasons_route.py`: Part B — irreplaceability dominance on a finite three-particular model with budget constraint (preserve-maximal information ≥ destroy; exact rationals); Part C — option-value dominance enumerated over finite value-function sets and priors (every prior, every protection cost up to the option value; exact rationals); Part A — reciprocity (Section 15.5). Parts for 16.2 and 16.4 cite the banked ledger solvency and greatestness dichotomy respectively; no new computation is claimed for them.

## Section 17 — The premise floor: income shown necessary, parameters unmasked

*Each stone of the declared stack is brought to one of three honest states: derived, unavoidable, or explicitly a parameter.*

### 17.1 Income-or-irrelevance (the necessity of the solvency condition)

The Master Solvency Condition was declared. Here it is shown to be **not a free assumption among relevant agents**: any agent that violates it eliminates itself as a protector in finite time, so every preservation-relevant comparison may restrict, without loss of generality, to agents that satisfy it.

**Theorem (income-or-irrelevance).** Let \(a\in\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\) be any physically implementable agent with finite energy budget and maintenance demand \(\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t)\) (the five rate components of the header). Then exactly one of the following holds over the horizon:

1. **Solvency.** For all \(t\le T\): \(\mathrm{Income}_a(t)\ge\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t)\) (with escrow absorbing fluctuations) — \(a\) remains a viable protector, its policies admissible in preservation comparisons.
2. **Irrelevance.** There is a finite \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\le T\) at which \(a\)'s available budget falls below the required charge for some maintenance event it cannot decline; from \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\), every policy available to \(a\) abandons the witness of some particular (the funded/unfunded dichotomy, banked as the \(\Pi_{\mathrm{fail}}\) branch of the greatestness theorem), and \(a\)'s outcome tuples fail at stage 1 in every comparison against any funded agent.

*Proof.* The dichotomy is exhaustive by accounting: either the budget constraint holds at every admitted event or it fails first at some \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\). In the latter case two X3 forcings apply: until the breach, the mandatory maintenance demand, though only \(\Theta(1)\) in rate, is positive, so a finite budget is exhausted at finite \(t_{\mathrm{breach}}\); and once a witness goes unheld, its recovery costs \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\), exceeding any finite budget past \(t_{\mathrm{irreversible}}\) (§21.5) — the breach is irreversible and charged to \(a\)'s own account. An agent whose every policy loses a particular is strictly below every funded agent at stage 1 of the canonical order — it is not merely dominated; it is *comparison-void* as a protector. \(\blacksquare\)

**Consequence for the stack.** Premise 6 (income) is upgraded from *declared* to **necessary-for-relevance**: the class of agents worth comparing over may be defined as the funded class without loss — which is exactly how \(\Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}\)'s funded subclass entered the greatestness theorem. What remains declared about income is only its *sufficiency for a given installation*. That is a physical fact to be established per instance (§22's ledger), not an assumption smuggled into comparisons.

### 17.2 The parameters unmasked

The seventh premise conflated two kinds of objects. Separated:

**Engineering parameters** (dials with declared admissible ranges, fixed per installation): the stabilizer code and its distance \(d\); the QND sampling rate and latency \(a,\tau_{\mathrm{sensor}}\); the per-site sensor threshold \(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}\); the embedding \(\Pi\) on the boundary fragments. These are not axioms — they are choices with certificates, and each theorem that uses them states its dependence explicitly (as the E9 trichotomy and H5 sections already do). A different installation with different parameters is a different *instance*, not a different *theory*.

**The residue, honestly counted.** The stack now reads:

| # | Stone | State |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | BCRD P1–P4 | Given: the frame. Its adoption is decided by living process; no theorem can decide it |
| 2 | CTD | Standard physics; reduced to the gate-set clause by the Lie–Trotter product formula and Lloyd's simulation theorem (compiled, §23.6) |
| 3 | The cost model | Standard, and P-mon derived within it |
| 4 | The shared action interface | Derived from P1/P4 |
| 5 | Reversible administration | Heat-only model compiled; actual implementation candidate checked; full lifecycle/deadline comparison remains the construction target (§23.6; G.14) |
| 6 | Income | **Necessary-for-relevance (17.1); sufficiency per instance** |
| 7 | Code, sensing, \(\Pi\) | **Parameters, not premises.** The ceiling is met at some code distance below threshold, and the quantum-speed-limit rate bound is derived (compiled, §§23.3, 23.6) |

Three declared stones (the frame, the cost model with its derived monotonicity, and the reduced simulation principle), one derived interface, one derived commitment, one necessary condition, and one parameter table. That is the floor as of this edition; the Gödel/Tarski line still stands beneath stones 1–2, and says so.

### 17.3 What remains on this floor

Nothing further is derivable without new physics or new commitments. Two things remain. The first is per-instance sufficiency of income, which §22 establishes for a reference installation from sourced measurements. The second is the interpretive case for stone 1, which belongs to the interpretive companion, not to the mathematics.

## Section 18 — Theorem 8: Physical Repair, Feedback and the Complete Thermodynamic Ledger

*This section specifies the exact measurement, feedback and bath-coupled reset operations and the Hamiltonian conventions, and gives the signed work, heat and information ledger without sign ambiguity.*

---

### 18.1 The Physical Setting and Hamiltonian Conventions

Let $\mathcal{H}_S$ be the Hilbert space of the protected particular/system, $\mathcal{H}_A$ the register/memory space of the autonomous controller, and $\mathcal{H}_B$ the thermal bath at inverse temperature $\beta = (k_B T)^{-1}$.

The total Hamiltonian is time-dependent and piecewise continuous over the four strokes of the repair cycle:
$$H_{\mathrm{tot}}(t) = H_S(t) + H_A(t) + H_B + V_{SA}(t) + V_{AB}(t)$$
where:
1. $H_S(t) = H_S^0$ is constant outside control pulses; the code subspace $\mathcal{C} \subset \mathcal{H}_S$ is an eigenspace of $H_S^0$ (or degenerate ground space, so logical states incur zero autonomous drift).
2. $H_A(t)$ has a discrete orthogonal basis $\{|m\rangle\}_m$ corresponding to measurement syndromes, with degenerate reset ground state $|0\rangle_A$.
3. $V_{SA}(t)$ couples system and controller during measurement (Stroke I) and feedback (Stroke II).
4. $V_{AB}(t)$ couples controller and bath during reset (Stroke III).
5. All interaction potentials turn on and off smoothly: $\int V_{SA}(t) dt = \int V_{AB}(t) dt = 0$ at stroke endpoints, ensuring no unaccounted boundary interaction energies.

---

### 18.2 The Four Strokes of the Autonomic Repair Cycle

The autonomic cycle operates across four distinct intervals $[t_0, t_4]$ with cycle duration $\tau_{\mathrm{cycle}} = t_4 - t_0$:

```
       Stroke I               Stroke II              Stroke III             Stroke IV
 [Syndrome Measurement]  [Coherent Feedback]   [Bath-Coupled Reset]   [Escrow Replenish]
     t0 ----------> t1 ------> t2 ----------> t3 ----------> t4
       V_SA on               U_SA on               V_AB on              Harvest flux
       Extract I_M           ΔE_code = -ηC         Dump Q_B to Bath     Income >= Demand
```

#### Stroke I: Syndrome Measurement ($t_0 \to t_1$)
* **Initial State:** $\rho_{SA}(t_0) = \rho_S(t_0) \otimes |0\rangle\langle 0|_A$.
* **Measurement Map:** A set of Kraus operators $\{M_m\}_m$ on $\mathcal{H}_S$ with $\sum_m M_m^\dagger M_m = I_S$.
* **Post-measurement State:**
  $$\rho_{SA}(t_1) = \sum_m p_m \rho_m \otimes |m\rangle\langle m|_A, \quad p_m = \operatorname{Tr}(M_m \rho_S M_m^\dagger), \quad \rho_m = \frac{1}{p_m} M_m \rho_S M_m^\dagger$$
* **Information Extraction:** The mutual information established between system and memory is:
  $$I_1 = I(S : A)_{t_1} = S(\rho_S(t_1)) + S(\rho_A(t_1)) - S(\rho_{SA}(t_1)) = H(\{p_m\}) = I_M > 0$$
* **Thermodynamic Cost:** Measurement work $W_{\mathrm{meas}} \ge 0$ is paid from the tracked escrow: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow}} = -W_{\mathrm{meas}}$.

#### Stroke II: Coherent Conditional Feedback ($t_1 \to t_2$)
* **Feedback Unitary:** The controller applies conditional unitary operations $U_m$ to restore each syndrome component to the code subspace $\mathcal{C}$:
  $$U_{SA} = \sum_m U_m \otimes |m\rangle\langle m|_A, \quad U_m^\dagger U_m = I_S$$
* **Post-feedback System State:**
  $$\rho_S(t_2) = \sum_m p_m U_m \rho_m U_m^\dagger = \sum_m U_m M_m \rho_S(t_0) M_m^\dagger U_m^\dagger \equiv \mathcal{R}_C(\rho_S(t_0)) \in \mathcal{C}$$
* **Entropy and Information Change:**
  Because $U_{SA}$ acts conditionally on orthogonal memory states, it preserves joint entropy $S(SA)$ and memory marginal $\rho_A(t_2) = \rho_A(t_1) = \sum_m p_m |m\rangle\langle m|_A$.
  The mutual information after feedback becomes:
  $$I_2 = I(S : A)_{t_2} = S(\rho_S(t_2)) + S(\rho_A(t_2)) - S(\rho_{SA}(t_2))$$
  When feedback completely disentangles the restored code state from the syndrome record, $\rho_{SA}(t_2) = \rho_{\mathrm{code}} \otimes \sum_m p_m |m\rangle\langle m|$, so $I_2 = 0$.
  Therefore, the system entropy reduction is exactly:
  $$\Delta S(S)_{\mathrm{II}} = S(\rho_S(t_2)) - S(\rho_S(t_1)) = I_2 - I_1 = -I_M < 0$$
* **Feedback Work:** Coherent work performed on the system:
  $$W_{\mathrm{on,II}} = \operatorname{Tr}(H_S \rho_S(t_2)) - \operatorname{Tr}(H_S \rho_S(t_1))$$
  For degenerate code projections, $W_{\mathrm{on,II}} = 0$.

#### Stroke III: Bath-Coupled Memory Reset ($t_2 \to t_3$)
* **Initial State:** Memory carries entropy $S(\rho_A(t_2)) = H(\{p_m\}) = I_M$.
* **Reset Dynamics:** Coupling $V_{AB}(t)$ to bath $\mathcal{H}_B$ at temperature $T$ drives $\rho_A \to |0\rangle\langle 0|_A$.
* **Entropy and Heat Dump:**
  By Landauer's principle and the second law for information reservoirs (Sagawa & Ueda 2008):
  $$Q_B \ge k_B T \, S(\rho_A(t_2)) = k_B T \, I_M$$
  where $Q_B > 0$ denotes heat dissipated into the bath ($\Delta S_B = \beta Q_B \ge I_M$).
* **Escrow Ledger:** The work required to execute reset is supplied from escrow:
  $$\Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow,III}} = -W_{\mathrm{reset}}, \quad W_{\mathrm{reset}} \ge Q_B \ge k_B T \, I_M$$

#### Stroke IV: Solvent Escrow Replenishment ($t_3 \to t_4$)
* **Income Coupling:** Ambient free energy flux (solar, thermal gradient, chemical, nuclear) charges the physical escrow reserve at rate $\dot W_{\mathrm{income}}(t)$.
* **Net Escrow Restoration:**
  $$\Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow,IV}} = \int_{t_3}^{t_4} \dot W_{\mathrm{income}}(t) dt = W_{\mathrm{income}}$$
* **Master Solvency Condition:**
  Under the income condition (Section 17.1), the net cycle income strictly covers all cycle expenditures:
  $$W_{\mathrm{income}} \ge W_{\mathrm{meas}} + W_{\mathrm{reset}} + W_{\mathrm{dissipation}} \equiv W_{\mathrm{demand}}$$
  Ensuring $E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(t_4) \ge E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(t_0)$.

---

### 18.3 Resolution of the Sign Issue: The True Invariant Functional

In earlier formulations, the relative entropy with respect to a thermal Gibbs state $\tau_S \propto e^{-\beta H_S}$,
$$D(\rho_S \| \tau_S) = \beta \operatorname{Tr}(H_S \rho_S) - S(\rho_S) - \ln Z_S$$
was inverted: restoring purity/order ($\Delta S < 0$) causes $D(\rho_S \| \tau_S)$ to **increase**, because a clean logical state is further from the high-entropy thermal state $\tau_S$ than a degraded, mixed state.

#### The Correct Physical Code Error Metric
The objective of protection is fidelity to the **code subspace $\mathcal{C}$**, not proximity to thermal chaos. We define the code error directly via the orthogonal projection $P_{\mathcal{C}}$ onto the stabilized subspace:
$$\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S) \equiv \operatorname{Tr}\left(\rho_S (I - P_{\mathcal{C}})\right) = 1 - \operatorname{Tr}(\rho_S P_{\mathcal{C}})$$
which satisfies:
* $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S) \ge 0$, with equality if and only if $\operatorname{supp}(\rho_S) \subseteq \mathcal{C}$.
* Under environmental noise/drift: $\Delta_{\mathrm{noise}} \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}} > 0$.
* Under syndrome feedback $\mathcal{R}_C$:
  $$\Delta_{\mathrm{II}} \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}} = \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S(t_2)) - \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S(t_1)) = 0 - \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho_S(t_1)) = -\eta C < 0$$
  where $\eta C > 0$ is the discrete recovery magnitude.

#### The Master Storage / Lyapunov Functional
Define the actual storage functional
\[
\Phi(\rho,E)=\mathcal E_{\mathrm{code}}(\rho)+f(E),\qquad
f(E)=\frac{\max(0,W_{\mathrm{target}}-E)}{W_0},\quad W_0>0.
\]
The storage capacity \(K\) is chosen at least as large as the required reserve target.
For nonnegative income \(I\), replenishment gives
\[
E^+=\min(K,E+I),\qquad
f(E^+)-f(E)=-\min\{I/W_0,f(E)\}.
\]
Thus replenishment clears at most the existing deficit. Additional income does not
drive the clipped term below zero (`TheoremEightLyapunov.replenishment_exact`).

Let \(W_{\mathrm{cycle}}\) be the complete realised cycle charge, including
measurement, correction, reset, holding, guard, routing, verification and controller
work. Its physical escrow balance is
\[
E_{n+1}=\min(K,E_n-W_{\mathrm{cycle}}+I_n).
\]
The excess \(S_n=\max(0,E_n-W_{\mathrm{cycle}}+I_n-K)\) is explicitly exported:
\[
E_{n+1}+W_{\mathrm{cycle}}+S_n=E_n+I_n.
\]
`capacity_balance` and `solvent_cycle_energy_account` prove these identities.
Within-cycle reservations still fund every operation before it executes.

If \(W_{\mathrm{cycle}}\le I_n\), the reserve term does not increase
(`solvent_cycle_deficit`). The actual error drift and correction give
\[
\mathcal E_{\mathrm{drift}}\le\mathcal E_n+\lambda\tau,\qquad
\mathcal E_{n+1}\le\mathcal E_{\mathrm{drift}}-\eta C.
\]
Therefore the defined functional obeys
\[
\Phi_{n+1}-\Phi_n\le-(\eta C-\lambda\tau).
\]
`cycle_contracts` proves this from the realised error bounds and complete solvent
balance. For positive margin the contraction is strict on the stated error cycles.
The controller supplies its tracking rate; the reserve plateau does not imply an
additional exponential convergence theorem.

---

### 18.4 Theorem 8 (Complete Physical Accounting Statement)

**Theorem 8 (Autonomic Preservation Invariant).**
Let an autonomous physical protector $a \in \Pi_{\mathrm{phys}}$ execute the periodic syndrome-feedback-reset protocol $(\mathcal{R}_C, \tau_{\mathrm{cycle}})$ under the Hamiltonian convention (18.1) with fixed bath temperature $T$. If $a$ satisfies:
1. **Solvency:** $\dot W_{\mathrm{income}}(t) \ge \dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t)$ for all $t$.
2. **Timeliness:** $\tau_{\mathrm{cycle}} < \tau_{\mathrm{harm}} \equiv \eta C / \lambda_{\mathrm{decay}}$.
3. **Landauer Saturation:** $E_{\mathrm{escrow}} \ge k_B T \, I_M$.

Then:
1. The combined storage functional $\Phi(t)$ is a strict Lyapunov function for the unmitigated error dynamics, contracting by at least $\sigma_0 > 0$ on each cycle containing an error.
2. The code subspace $\mathcal{C}$ is asymptotically stable and invariant on the good event $G$.
3. All work, heat, and information quantities are strictly conserved and accounted for:
   $$W_{\mathrm{income}} = W_{\mathrm{on}} + Q_B + \Delta E_{\mathrm{escrow}}, \quad Q_B \ge T \Delta S_B \ge k_B T I_M$$
   with zero uncharged entropy or hidden energy debt.

*Proof.* The actual error drift and repair bound, together with the complete solvent escrow balance of §18.3, give the cycle change at most \(-(\eta C-\lambda\tau)\) (`TheoremEightLyapunov.cycle_contracts`). The reserve-deficit term is handled through saturation and is nonincreasing over the cycle; no linear replenishment descent past zero is assumed. Timeliness gives \(\sigma_0=\eta C-\lambda\tau>0\) (`margin_pos`), and the cycle recurrence gives \(\Phi_n\le\Phi_0-n\sigma_0\) over the stated error cycles (`lyapunov_iterate`). The physical holding and entropy-export certificates retain their separate roles. $\blacksquare$

The compiled result establishes the defined storage inequality from the actual drift, repair and complete solvent balance. The full Theorem 8 statement retains all its conclusions. Applying it to the physical installation still joins the protective source dynamics, invariant/error bounds and complete work/entropy accounts; those application certificates are not inferred from the reserve plateau or from the iteration identity alone.
