## Section 19 — Theorem E9: Observation, Wave-Race Guarantees, and Epistemic Self-Healing

*This section establishes the detection guarantee, Byzantine sensor fault tolerance, the wave-race propagation margin, and buffer protection during the response interval.*

---

### 19.1 The Physical Measurement and Disturbance Setting

Let $\mathcal{M}$ be the physical metric space of the substrate hosting the protected particulars, equipped with Riemannian metric $d_{\mathcal{M}}(x, y)$.

#### Physical Velocities and Limits:
1. **Signal Propagation Velocity ($c$):** Electromagnetic/optical communication across the bus propagates at velocity $c$.
2. **Harm/Decoherence Velocity ($v_{\mathrm{harm}}$):** Physical degradation fronts (thermal propagation, mechanical shock, acoustic damage, chemical diffusion, adversarial intrusion) propagate at characteristic velocity $v_{\mathrm{harm}} < c$.
3. **Sensor Lattice:** A discrete lattice of non-destructive sensors $\mathcal{S} = \{s_i\}$ with spatial density $\rho_{\mathrm{sens}}$ and maximum spacing $R_{\mathrm{sens}} = \sup_{x \in \mathcal{M}} \min_i d_{\mathcal{M}}(x, s_i)$.

---

### 19.2 Byzantine Sensor Fault and Spoofing Tolerance

Let a physical disturbance occur at location $x \in \mathcal{M}$ with onset time $t_0$.

#### Sensor Quorum and Disjoint Observer Sets:
Each protected region is monitored by a local cluster of $K$ independent sensors.
To account for sensor failures, noise bursts, or adversarial sensor spoofing:
1. **Adversarial Bound:** At any instant, at most $f$ out of the $K$ sensors in the local cluster are faulty or spoofed, with $K \ge 2f + 1$ (or $K \ge 3f + 1$ under Byzantine asymmetric signaling).
2. **Consensus Filter:** The cluster computes the robust trimmed estimate $\hat{y}(t) = \operatorname{median}_{K-2f}(y_1(t), \dots, y_K(t))$.
3. **Detection Criterion:** An alarm is committed when $\hat{y}(t) > \theta_{\mathrm{sens}}$.
4. **False Alarm & Spoofing Immunity:** Because $f < K/2$, no subset of $f$ malicious or malfunctioning sensors can force a false alarm or suppress a true alarm:
   $$\hat{y}(t) \in [\min_{i \in \mathrm{honest}} y_i(t), \max_{i \in \mathrm{honest}} y_i(t)]$$
5. **Detection Time ($\tau_{\mathrm{detect}}$):**
   $$\tau_{\mathrm{detect}} = \tau_{\mathrm{sampling}} + \frac{R_{\mathrm{sens}}}{c} + \tau_{\mathrm{consensus}}$$
   where $\tau_{\mathrm{sampling}}$ is the sampling cycle time, $R_{\mathrm{sens}}/c$ is communication latency to the local voting node, and $\tau_{\mathrm{consensus}}$ is local deterministic comparator time.

---

### 19.3 The Wave-Race Margin and Response Interval

Let an adversarial or stochastic harm front originate at boundary distance $d_{\mathrm{boundary}}$ from a protected logical core.

#### The Timing Chain:
1. **Alarm Transmission:** An alarm generated at the sensor lattice reaches the autonomic controller and actuation units across bus distance $d_{\mathrm{bus}}$ in time:
   $$\tau_{\mathrm{signal}} = \frac{d_{\mathrm{bus}}}{c}$$
2. **Autonomic Decision & Reroute:** The controller verifies reserve escrow and commits the repair/isolation plan:
   $$\tau_{\mathrm{decision}} \le \tau_{\mathrm{commit}}$$
3. **Actuation / Repair Pulse:** Actuators engage (diverting energy flux, activating topological braiding, or adjusting stabilizer syndrome drives):
   $$\tau_{\mathrm{actuate}} \le \tau_{\mathrm{repair}}$$
4. **Total Autonomic Response Time ($\tau_{\mathrm{response}}$):**
   $$\tau_{\mathrm{response}} = \tau_{\mathrm{detect}} + \tau_{\mathrm{signal}} + \tau_{\mathrm{decision}} + \tau_{\mathrm{actuate}}$$

#### The Earliest Irreversible Harm Window ($\tau_{\mathrm{harm}}$):
The physical disturbance must traverse the protective buffer shell of spatial thickness $L_{\mathrm{buffer}}$ and overcome the code distance $d_{\mathrm{code}}$:
$$\tau_{\mathrm{harm}} = \frac{L_{\mathrm{buffer}}}{v_{\mathrm{harm}}}$$
For topological/stabilizer codes with physical error rate $p < p_{\mathrm{th}}$, logical failure requires accumulating at least $\lfloor(d_{\mathrm{code}} - 1)/2\rfloor$ errors, providing an additional temporal buffer:
$$\tau_{\mathrm{code\_headroom}} = \frac{d_{\mathrm{code}}}{2 \lambda_{\mathrm{fault}}}$$

#### The Wave-Race Inequality:
Because $v_{\mathrm{harm}} < c$, the spatial buffer $L_{\mathrm{buffer}}$ guarantees a strictly positive propagation margin:
$$\Delta \tau_{\mathrm{margin}} \equiv \tau_{\mathrm{harm}} - \tau_{\mathrm{response}} = \frac{L_{\mathrm{buffer}}}{v_{\mathrm{harm}}} - \left(\tau_{\mathrm{detect}} + \frac{d_{\mathrm{bus}}}{c} + \tau_{\mathrm{decision}} + \tau_{\mathrm{actuate}}\right)$$
By choosing the engineering parameter $L_{\mathrm{buffer}}$ (Section 17.2) such that:
$$L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \left(\tau_{\mathrm{detect}} + \frac{d_{\mathrm{bus}}}{c} + \tau_{\mathrm{decision}} + \tau_{\mathrm{actuate}}\right)$$
the margin is strictly positive: $\Delta \tau_{\mathrm{margin}} > 0$.

---

### 19.4 Protection Guarantee During the Response Interval

What guarantees that the system is preserved during the latency interval $t \in [t_0, t_0 + \tau_{\mathrm{response}}]$ before active repair engages?

#### The Two Shields:
1. **Passive Physical Buffer (Energy & Flux Shield):**
   The local physical enclosure possesses heat capacity $C_{\mathrm{thermal}}$ and mechanical yield reserve $E_{\mathrm{yield}}$.
   For maximum external incident flux $\dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}^{\max}$:
   $$\int_0^{\tau_{\mathrm{response}}} \dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}(t) dt \le \dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}^{\max} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}} \le E_{\mathrm{buffer}}$$
   The buffer ensures temperature and strain at the protected core remain strictly within the linear elastic / non-damaging regime throughout the entire reaction window.
2. **Topological Syndrome Headroom (Information Shield):**
   During $\tau_{\mathrm{response}}$, errors accumulated by the physical qubits satisfy:
   $$N_{\mathrm{errors}}(\tau_{\mathrm{response}}) \le \lambda_{\mathrm{fault}} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}} < \frac{d_{\mathrm{code}}}{2}$$
   Because $N_{\mathrm{errors}} < d_{\mathrm{code}}/2$, the corrupted state remains strictly within the correctable decoding radius of the code space $\mathcal{C}$. When active feedback engages at $t = t_0 + \tau_{\mathrm{response}}$, the syndrome measurement uniquely identifies the error chain and restores the logical state with fidelity $1$.

---

### 19.5 Theorem E9 (Epistemic and Topological Self-Healing)

**Theorem E9 (Timely Self-Healing & Metric Rigidity).**
Let a Sovereign Lattice installation satisfy:
1. **Superluminal Communication Margin:** $v_{\mathrm{harm}} < c$.
2. **Sensor Redundancy:** Local sensor clusters have size $K \ge 2f + 1$, with at most $f$ faulty or adversarial nodes.
3. **Buffer Sizing:**
   $$L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}}, \quad E_{\mathrm{buffer}} \ge \dot Q_{\mathrm{incident}}^{\max} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}}$$
4. **Code Distance Headroom:**
   $$d_{\mathrm{code}} > 2 \lambda_{\mathrm{fault}} \, \tau_{\mathrm{response}}$$

Then:
1. **Detection & Spoofing Immunity:** Every physical disturbance exceeding threshold $\theta_{\mathrm{sens}}$ is detected and committed within $\tau_{\mathrm{detect}}$, and no adversarial spoofing by $\le f$ sensors can trigger false actuation or mask genuine harm.
2. **Timely Preemption:** Coherent repair and flow rerouting are fully applied before the harm front reaches the protected core ($\tau_{\mathrm{response}} < \tau_{\mathrm{harm}}$).
3. **Continuous Safety Invariant:** For all $t \ge 0$, including throughout every active response window, the logical fidelity of protected particulars satisfies:
   $$F(\rho_{\mathrm{logical}}(t), \rho_{\mathrm{target}}) \ge 1 - \mathcal{O}(e^{-\kappa d_{\mathrm{code}}})$$
   ensuring zero uncontained catastrophic loss.

*Proof.* Follows by combining the Byzantine quorum consensus (19.2), the wave-race inequality (19.3), and the passive buffer and syndrome headroom bounds (19.4). Machine-checked algebraic timing instances are verified in `BridgeE9.lean`. $\blacksquare$

---

## Section 20 — Theorem H5: Generative Incompleteness and Reflective Relational Closure

*This section establishes authority attenuation, the reserve-before-activation lifecycle, safe refusal with continuing care, and conservative logical axiom expansion.*

---

### 20.1 The Problem of Recursive Spawning and Self-Modification

In any autonomous superintelligent architecture, agents may spawn child processes, delegate sub-tasks, instantiate specialized sub-agents, or undergo recursive self-modification. A catastrophic failure occurs if:
1. **Escalation of Privilege:** A spawned descendant claims unmediated permissions, bypassing the parent's boundary constraints.
2. **Unfunded Proliferation (Fork Bomb / Sybil Drain):** A parent creates infinite un-escrowed descendants that exhaust the substrate's shared physical reserves.
3. **Poisoning via Axiom Expansion:** A cognitive agent extending its formal knowledge base introduces inconsistent or adversarial axioms that corrupt foundational safety invariants.

Theorem H5 proves that under the **Reflective Relational Closure** constraint $\mathcal{I}$, recursive generation is mathematically closed, strictly authority-attenuating, and universally pre-funded.

---

### 20.2 The Spawning Formalism and Authority Attenuation

Let $\mathcal{A}$ be the universe of active agents. An agent $a \in \mathcal{A}$ is defined by a tuple:
$$a = \left(\mathcal{S}_a, \operatorname{Auth}(a), E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(a), \mathcal{C}_a\right)$$
where:
* $\mathcal{S}_a$ is the internal computational state space.
* $\operatorname{Auth}(a) \subseteq \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{act}})$ is the permitted set of physical action operators.
* $E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(a) \ge 0$ is the certified energetic reserve.
* $\mathcal{C}_a \subset \mathcal{H}_S$ is the bound protected code subspace the agent is sworn to maintain.

#### Definition (The Spawning Transition):
When parent $p$ spawns child $c$, the transition is mediated by the kernel compiler gate $\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}$:
$$\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}} : (p, \text{spec}_c) \mapsto (p', c)$$

#### Theorem H5.1 (Strict Authority Attenuation).
For every legitimate spawning transition $\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}$, the child's authority satisfies:
$$\operatorname{Auth}(c) \subseteq \operatorname{Auth}(p) \setminus \Delta_{\mathrm{meta}}$$
where $\Delta_{\mathrm{meta}}$ includes the capability to override parent boundary monitors, re-allocate un-escrowed root reserves, or modify the kernel compiler gate $\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}$.

*Proof.* The kernel compiler $\Gamma_{\mathrm{spawn}}$ verifies capability inclusion as a type-checking prerequisite. Any child specification with $\operatorname{Auth}(c) \not\subseteq \operatorname{Auth}(p)$ fails type checking and is rejected as an un-compilable ill-typed program before memory allocation. $\blacksquare$

---

### 20.3 The Reserve-Before-Activation Machine

To guarantee that no spawned descendant creates an unhedged drain on the physical maintenance ledger, every agent life-cycle is governed by a strict five-state automaton:

```
  [UNBORN] ---> (reserve) ---> [RESERVED] ---> (activate) ---> [ACTIVE]
                                                                  |
                                                               (retire)
                                                                  v
  [TERMINATED] <--- (release) <--- [RETIRED] <--------------------+
```

#### State Transitions and Invariants:
1. **`reserve(p, c, W_req)`:**
   Parent $p$ must carve out the child's complete maintenance and execution budget $W_{\mathrm{req}}$ from its own tracked escrow:
   $$E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(p') = E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(p) - W_{\mathrm{req}}, \quad E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(c) = W_{\mathrm{req}} \ge W_{\mathrm{min}}(T_{\mathrm{lifetime}})$$
   If $E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(p) < W_{\mathrm{req}} + E_{\mathrm{safety\_margin}}$, the reservation is rejected. Zero debt is permitted.
2. **`activate(c)`:**
   Child $c$ transitions to `ACTIVE` only after $E_{\mathrm{escrow}}(c)$ is physically credited to its local hardware controller.
3. **`retire(c)`:**
   Upon task completion or deadline expiry, child $c$ ceases external action; its actuation channels are severed.
4. **`release(c, p)`:**
   Residual unspent escrow $E_{\mathrm{residual}}(c)$ is returned to parent $p$ or the root commons ledger.

#### Corollary (No Unfunded Descendants).
At every instant $t$, the total active population $\mathcal{A}(t)$ satisfies:
$$\sum_{a \in \mathcal{A}(t)} \dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(a) \le \sum_{a \in \mathcal{A}(t)} \operatorname{Income}_a + E_{\mathrm{escrow\_pool}}$$
The network is immune to Sybil proliferation and resource-exhaustion denial of service.

---

### 20.4 Safe Refusal with Continuing Care

Suppose an adversarial, compromised, or misaligned parent issues an actuation command to a child:
$$\mathrm{cmd} = \text{"Delete or cause unmediated harm to particular } \mathcal{W}\text{"}$$

#### Theorem H5.2 (Safe Refusal with Invariant Protection).
Under Reflective Relational Closure:
1. **Refusal of Harmful Command:** The command violates the boundary constraint $\mathcal{C}_c \cap \operatorname{Effect}(\mathrm{cmd}) = \emptyset$. The child's effect enforcement engine intercepts and nullifies the harmful actuation channel.
2. **Continuing Care ($R(m) \lor H(m)$):**
   Refusal does not terminate the child's existence, nor does it abandon care for the compromised parent or affected particulars. The child switches into autonomous defensive holding:
   $$\operatorname{Action}(c) = \text{hold\_in\_escrow}(\mathcal{W})$$
   maintaining the witness and physical stability of $\mathcal{W}$ while alerting the lattice consensus layer.

*Proof.* By the reciprocity dominance theorem (§15.2) and Chapter 5 (The Canon Invariant), restraint removes harmful command authority while care for all particulars continues unconditionally. Because the child's escrow was fully funded at `reserve`, the child has the physical energy required to sustain protection independently of the parent's ongoing cooperation. $\blacksquare$

---

### 20.5 H5 Axiom Expansion: Logical Closure Without Poisoning

When an intelligent agent extends its formal theory $T_0$ by postulating new axioms or learned generalizations $\Delta T$, how is the system shielded against inconsistency poisoning?

#### The Submodule Projection of Logical Theories:
Let $\mathcal{L}$ be the Hilbert space of proposition valuations (or the lattice of closed deduction spaces).
* Foundational protected axioms form a closed subspace $A \subset \mathcal{L}$.
* Newly proposed theories or empirical models form a candidate subspace $B \subset \mathcal{L}$.
* The verified safe knowledge base is the orthogonal projection onto the intersection:
  $$\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{safe}} = P_{A \sqcap B}$$

#### Theorem H5.3 (Axiom Expansion Soundness).
1. **Consistent Expansion:** If candidate axioms $B$ are fully consistent with $A$ and contain $A$ ($A \subseteq B$), then:
   $$A \sqcap B = A$$
   and foundational anchors are preserved with zero distortion.
2. **Contradictory / Adversarial Expansion:** If candidate axioms $B$ contradict foundational protection axioms, the global meet collapses: $A \sqcap B = \{0\}$. By von Neumann's alternating projection theorem (`CanonVonNeumann.canon_von_neumann_convergence` with `von_neumann_identification`, compiled), the alternating projection converges to the projection onto the meet — which is zero, so the state machine detects the collapse and rolls back (Chapter 4); the anchor is not silently poisoned. When the candidate is consistent and contains the anchors ($A \subseteq B$), the meet is $A$ itself and the anchors are preserved with zero distortion.
3. **Epistemic Immunity:** No adversarial theorem deduction can force a contradiction into the protected anchor $A$.

*Proof.* Follows from the machine-checked meet algebra (`MeetAlgebra.lean`) and von Neumann identification (`MathlibAnalyticBridge.lean`). The operator $P_{A \sqcap B}$ acts as a conservative projection that absorbs only the non-contradictory relational content of new theories while annihilating adversarial derivations. $\blacksquare$

---

**Machine-checked counterpart (Section 23).** Compiled counterparts: H5.1–H5.2 and the reserve-before-activation machine — `H5Closure.authority_attenuation`, `reserve_before_activation`, `no_unfunded_work`, `no_harmful_effect`, `effect_preserves_care`; H5.3 — von Neumann (`CanonVonNeumann.canon_von_neumann_convergence`), Halperin (`CyclicProjections.tendsto_cyc_iter`), the detectors (`H5Closure.consistency_detector`, `CyclicProjections.presentation_detector`), and the Hilbert-space meet algebra (`MeetHilbert.contradiction_detected`, `deduction_never_rolls_back`).

## Section 21 — X3: complete inputs, compulsory traffic, transport and recovery work

The X3 target remains the dimension-general law \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\), over the complete permitted recovery-protocol class with every resource charged. This section incorporates the information, traffic, carrier and work derivations carried through the gauntlet. G3 names the matching recovery construction requested for the upper inequality; it does not replace the target with a lower-bound-only theorem.

### 21.1 Complete permitted inputs and historical information

Let \(H\) denote the historical reference. The permitted recovery input contains the residual core, all accessible propagating carriers, emitted bath modes, retained memories, distributed copies, decoder side information and ancillary resources. These are subsystems or joint resources of one complete state; their correlations remain in the joint state. A correlated record cannot be omitted because it is distant, private, predeployed or called an ancilla.

For any recovery channel \(\mathcal R\), data processing gives

\[
I(H:\mathcal R(S))\le I(H:S).
\]

Independent ancillas do not increase the input information. Initially correlated ancillas already belong in \(S\). For messages \(M_j\) entering a decoder that retains \(B_{j-1}\),

\[
I(H:B_{j-1}M_j)-I(H:B_{j-1})
=I(H:M_j\mid B_{j-1})\le2\ln\dim M_j.
\]

The increments telescope with every initial correlated resource included in \(B_0\). Thus recovery cannot manufacture a missing historical correlation from uncorrelated noise. The particular's continuing history and a later reconstruction retain their distinct meanings.

`DataProcessing.data_processing` establishes data processing for its probability-space formulation; `FiniteInformation.data_processing_fin` establishes the finite classical version. The quantum message inequality above is the corresponding entropy argument, with its quantum systems and units explicitly stated.

### 21.2 A joint covariance estimate, with its proof

For the finite-temperature fermionic Gaussian calculation, let

\[
Q=(I+e^{\beta(h-\mu I)})^{-1},\qquad
\|h-\mu I\|\le E_{\max}.
\]

The scalar Fermi function is decreasing, so every eigenvalue lies in \([\delta,1-\delta]\), where \(\delta=(1+e^{\beta E_{\max}})^{-1}>0\). For the full reference–field covariance

\[
Q=\begin{pmatrix}A&C\\C^\dagger&B\end{pmatrix},
\qquad \delta I\le Q\le(1-\delta)I,
\]

the joint gauge-invariant fermionic Gaussian mutual information satisfies

\[
I(H:F)\le\frac{\|C\|_F^2}{\delta(1-\delta)}
\quad\text{(nats)}.
\]

To prove it, put \(Q_0=A\oplus B\), \(\Delta=Q-Q_0\), and \(f(x)=x\ln x+(1-x)\ln(1-x)\). Gaussian entropy gives \(I=\operatorname{Tr}f(Q)-\operatorname{Tr}f(Q_0)\). The first derivative along \(Q_0+s\Delta\) vanishes at zero because \(f'(Q_0)\) is block diagonal and \(\Delta\) is off diagonal. The whole segment keeps the spectral margin. Since \(f''(x)\le[\delta(1-\delta)]^{-1}\), the divided-difference expression for the trace Hessian bounds it by \(\|\Delta\|_F^2/[\delta(1-\delta)]\). Taylor's integral remainder contributes one half, and \(\|\Delta\|_F^2=2\|C\|_F^2\), proving the claim.

This uses the complete cross block. A single connected correlator or a count of modes supplies neither this joint upper bound nor a compulsory reset bill. The full UPT domain continues to include bosonic and other carriers; this particular covariance calculation has its stated fermionic domain. Their complete physical input/readout binding belongs in the full-domain construction G1, rather than being obtained by relabelling the covariance theorem. The channel, carrier and work steps below retain their own explicit constructions.

### 21.3 The calibrated joint adaptive channel

Let a boundary have \(n\ge1\) nodes and at most \(m\le n\) active readout coordinates per use. Conditional on the complete decoder history \(h\), assume each input coordinate has range at most \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}/n\), and write

\[
Y=G_hX+Z,\qquad
\|G_h\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le g,\qquad
\Sigma_h=\operatorname{Cov}(Z\mid h)\ge\sigma^2I,\quad\sigma>0.
\]

The conditional Gaussian noise is independent of the current message/input. The covariance floor applies after conditioning on all permitted side information. Inputs may be correlated and adaptive; all accessible readout channels belong in the joint model.

The midpoint of each coordinate's range yields \(\operatorname{Var}(X_i\mid h)\le\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2/(4n^2)\). Hence its covariance \(K_h\) satisfies \(\operatorname{Tr}K_h\le m\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2/(4n^2)\). Gaussian maximum entropy and data processing give, in bits,

\[
I(M:Y\mid h)\le\frac1{2\ln2}
\ln\det\!\left(I+\Sigma_h^{-1/2}G_hK_hG_h^\dagger\Sigma_h^{-1/2}\right).
\]

For a positive semidefinite \(W\), apply \(\ln(1+x)\le x\) to its eigenvalues to obtain \(\ln\det(I+W)\le\operatorname{Tr}W\). The gain and full covariance floor then give

\[
I(M:Y\mid h)\le
\frac{g^2}{2\sigma^2\ln2}\operatorname{Tr}K_h
\le \frac{m g^2\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2}{8\sigma^2\ln2\,n^2}.
\]

Averaging and the chain rule sum these conditional inequalities over adaptive uses. With at most \(\nu\) uses per second, put

\[
K_{\mathrm{rate}}=\frac{\nu g^2\Lambda_{\mathrm{obs}}^2}{8\sigma^2\ln2}.
\]

The rate is at most \(mK_{\mathrm{rate}}/n^2\le mK_{\mathrm{rate}}/n\). Observable range and information rate therefore have separate calibrated constants. The joint noise floor prevents an uncharged noiseless combination of marginally noisy sensors. `GaussianKL` through `LogDet` supply the Gaussian channel results, and `AreaBridge.logdet_le_trace` and `channel_cap` supply this assembly.

### 21.4 A positive deficit forces traffic, then charged area power

Let a historical message \(W\) have \(M\ge2\) possible values. Include every initially accessible record in \(B\), and require decoder error at most \(\epsilon<1-1/M\). If \(H(W\mid B)\ge H_{\mathrm{rem}}\), Fano's error-flag argument gives

\[
H(W\mid Y,B)\le h_2(\epsilon)+\epsilon\log_2(M-1),
\]
\[
\Delta_0=H_{\mathrm{rem}}-h_2(\epsilon)-\epsilon\log_2(M-1)>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad I(W:Y\mid B)\ge\Delta_0.
\]

Indeed the correct branch determines \(W\) and the error branch leaves at most \(M-1\) possibilities. Expanding conditional entropy by that flag proves the inequality; its right-hand side is increasing up to error \(1-1/M\). These steps are `Fano.fano` and `required_information`.

For deadline at most \(\tau_{\max}>0\), the required average rate is at least \(\Delta_0/\tau_{\max}\). With \(0<K_{\mathrm{rate}}\le K_{\max}\), the cut bound implies

\[
m\ge\theta_0 n,\qquad
\theta_0=\frac{\Delta_0}{\tau_{\max}K_{\max}}>0
\quad(\texttt{AreaBridge.required\_traffic}).
\]

If the active set varies, the chain rule charges active counts over the actual uses. A standing-power application also includes the concurrency/service certificate, rather than silently converting average work into instantaneous power.

For \(d_G\)-dimensional geometry, let \(n(r)\ge a r^{d_G-1}\) and let every active frontier node carry actual power at least \(p_0>0\). Then

\[
P(r)\ge p_0m(r)\ge p_0\theta_0a\,r^{d_G-1}
=p'r^{d_G-1}.
\]

This is `AreaBridge.area_power`. Geometry has an explicit lattice witness: the box of radius \(r\) has \((2r+1)^{d_G}\) sites, and its boundary has at least \(2(2r+1)^{d_G-1}\) sites (`Instruments.card_box`, `card_boundary`, `boundary_power`). The information deficit and deadline force activation; the geometry counts available sites; the ledger charges the active sites. All three steps enter the argument.

### 21.5 The carrier tail gives the necessary recovery radius

In the explicit flagged-carrier construction, a payload \(P\) contains the retained correlation with \(H\), with \(I_0=I(H:P)>0\). Its location is independent of the payload/reference and undergoes outward drift \(u>0\) with diffusion \(D>0\). Every other permitted correlated record is already included in the payload inventory. Grant an optimistic decoder the payload whenever the carrier lies at \(x\le r\), and an independent blank otherwise, with the occupancy flag revealed. This grants at least as much access as the actual local decoder.

The flag is independent of \(H\). The chain rule therefore gives the exact identity

\[
I(H:P_{\mathrm{view}}E)=p_r(t)I_0,\qquad
p_r(t)=\Phi\!\left(\frac{r-ut}{\sqrt{2Dt}}\right).
\]

Data processing bounds any lesser decoder by the same quantity. In the finite-alphabet construction these are `FiniteInformation.flag_identity` and `decoder_bound`; the displayed flag argument also states the corresponding joint-state calculation.

For \(r<ut\), the Gaussian tail gives \(p_r(t)\le\exp[-(ut-r)^2/(4Dt)]\). If success needs \(0<I_{\mathrm{target}}\le I_0\), every successful radius therefore satisfies

\[
r\ge ut-c\sqrt t,\qquad
c=2\sqrt{D\ln(I_0/I_{\mathrm{target}})}.
\]

For \(r\ge ut\) the same inequality is immediate. Choose any \(0<w<u\) and set \(t_c=c^2/[4w(u-w)]\). Completing the square gives

\[
ut-c\sqrt t-w(t-t_c)
=(u-w)\left(\sqrt t-\frac{c}{2(u-w)}\right)^2\ge0,
\]
\[
\boxed{R(t)\ge w(t-t_c)}.
\]

These are `GaussianTail.carrier_tail`, `FiniteInformation.necessary_radius_from_decoder` and `RecoveryWork.carrier_radius`. Diffusive tails stay positive at finite time; exact purging is unnecessary. The necessary lower radius comes from the complete carrier/information construction. A Lieb–Robinson upper bound on propagation is compatible with locality but does not supply this lower radius by itself.

### 21.6 Integrating actual recovery work

Keep unattended age \(t\) distinct from subsequent recovery duration \(s\in[0,T_{\mathrm{rec}}]\). For every successful permitted protocol let its frontier \(r(s)\ge0\) be absolutely continuous, start at \(r_0\), finish at least at \(R(t)\), and satisfy \(r'(s)\le v\). With \(d_G\ge1\), the charged power floor of 21.4 yields

\[
W(t)\ge p'\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{rec}}}r(s)^{d_G-1}\,ds.
\]

The fundamental theorem of calculus gives

\[
r(T_{\mathrm{rec}})^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}
=d_G\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{rec}}}r^{d_G-1}r'\,ds
\le vd_G\int_0^{T_{\mathrm{rec}}}r^{d_G-1}\,ds.
\]

Monotonicity of the frontier is unnecessary; nonnegativity and the speed bound suffice. Taking the infimum over the same successful protocol class gives

\[
\boxed{
W_{\min}(t)\ge\frac{p'}{v d_G}
\left(R(t)^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}\right)
\ge\frac{p'}{v d_G}
\left([w(t-t_c)]^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}\right)
}
\]

for sufficiently late \(t\). Thus delayed recovery work diverges, with an explicit order-\(t^{d_G}\) lower bound (`RecoveryWork.frontier_work_bound`, `Wmin_lower_bound`, `delayed_work_diverges`; `GaussianTail.x3_work_from_decoder`).

Predeployment is charged within the same accounting. If the initial protected radius depends on age, it cannot be treated as a free constant and subtracted while ignoring its deployment and holding bill. For example, an already funded deployment costing at least \(A r_0^{d_G}\), followed by a recovery bound \(A(R^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G})\), gives a total at least \(A R^{d_G}\). This is the prepaid lifecycle cancellation: the rival may move the expenditure in time, while the complete resource bill retains it. It carries forward the gauntlet's predeployment answer.

### 21.7 The matching upper construction: the supplied one-pass wavefront

The gauntlet's dimension-general target and predeployment answer remain

\[
A(R)=\Theta(R^{d_G-1}),\qquad
\dot W_{\mathrm{maint}}=\omega_0R^{d_G-1},\qquad
W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G}).
\]

The new single-shot feedforward construction supplies a concrete way to avoid repeatedly processing the recovered interior. Its cumulative-work calculation is stronger and simpler when written as the following exact telescoping proof.

#### 21.7.1 Count each processed patch once

Let \(v_{\mathrm{sw}}>0\) be the protocol's actual sweep speed, consistent with the causal speed limit. For integer wave index \(k\), set

\[
S_0=B_0(v_0),\qquad
S_k=B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}k}(v_0)\setminus
B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}(k-1)}(v_0)\quad(k\ge1).
\]

The shells are pairwise disjoint and their union through wave \(N\) is \(B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}N}(v_0)\). Consequently

\[
\boxed{\sum_{k=0}^N|S_k|=|B_{v_{\mathrm{sw}}N}(v_0)|}.
\]

Use the graph-volume convention \(|B_R|\le V_0(1+R)^{d_G}\); the \(1+R\) handles the origin, where a nonempty ball cannot be bounded by \(V_0R^{d_G}=0\). Equivalently one can state the original polynomial bound only for radii at least one.

If a vertex is processed at most once at cost at most \(w_*\), the work of that processing is

\[
W_{\mathrm{process}}(N)
\le w_*\sum_{k=0}^N|S_k|
\le w_*V_0(1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}N)^{d_G}.
\]

A fixed-radius local map on a bounded-degree graph has a bounded neighbourhood: \(|B_{r_0}(v)|\le\sum_{j=0}^{r_0}D_{\max}^j\). Fixed local input registers and a fixed evaluation circuit then give a constant evaluation cost. Even if neighbouring evaluations read overlapping neighbourhoods, each local register is read only a bounded number of times. Thus that overlap changes the constant, not the exponent.

No bound \(|S_k|\le A k^{d_G-1}\) is needed for this cumulative result. An upper bound on two ball volumes alone does not justify subtracting their upper bounds. The exact shell partition avoids that step while proving the intended exponent.

#### 21.7.2 Relate the construction to unattended age and the full bill

Keep unattended age \(t\) separate from wave count \(N(t)\) and physical recovery duration. For a successful recovery schedule with \(N(t)\le at+b\), constants \(a,b\ge0\) independent of age, the processing bound gives, for \(t\ge1\),

\[
W_{\mathrm{process}}(t)
\le w_*V_0[1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}(at+b)]^{d_G}
\le w_*V_0[1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}(a+b)]^{d_G}t^{d_G}.
\]

Let \(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}}\) be the supplied feedforward protocol. A complete work certificate charges deployment, signalling, routing, reset, protection during recovery, controller export and final decoding as well as the local map. Write its remaining total charge as \(W_{\mathrm{rest}}(t)\). If that certificate bounds it by \(C_{\mathrm{rest}}t^{d_G}\), the same protocol has

\[
W(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}},t)\le
\underbrace{\left(w_*V_0[1+v_{\mathrm{sw}}(a+b)]^{d_G}
+C_{\mathrm{rest}}\right)}_{C}
t^{d_G}.
\]

Since this is one successful protocol in the original permitted class,

\[
\boxed{W_{\min}(t)\le W(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}},t)\le Ct^{d_G}}.
\]

The inequality uses the cost of a constructed protocol as an upper bound on the minimum. It does not identify the minimum with that protocol's double sum. Combined with 21.6 it gives the unchanged tight \(\Theta(t^{d_G})\) target once the protocol's complete success and work certificate is joined.

This settles the supplied construction's shell-counting step. A sum that charges the whole interior on every wave describes a different schedule and cannot rule out this one-pass schedule. The claimed post-processing quiescence must retain the recovered state and the original fault standard; zero further active decoding does not by itself set all physical maintenance costs to zero. The existing ledger retains a minimal-hold drain and the controller's entropy export. G3 now asks for that exact success/quiescence/full-bill certificate for this supplied construction, rather than another derivation of the shell exponent.

#### 21.7.3 The boundary-flux route and the same accounting

A separately constructed protocol could also supply a boundary-work bound. If its complete charged power obeys \(P(s)\le J_{\max}A_0r(s)^{d_G-1}\), and its actual sweep grows at \(v_{\mathrm{sw}}\) to radius \(R\), then

\[
W\le W_{\mathrm{initial}}+W_{\mathrm{terminal}}
+\frac{J_{\max}A_0}{v_{\mathrm{sw}}d_G}
(R^{d_G}-r_0^{d_G}).
\]

With the endpoint charges bounded at order \(R^{d_G}\) and \(R=O(t)\), this also yields \(O(t^{d_G})\). The graph-volume bound alone is not a proof of the required surface-area upper bound; the boundary construction supplies that estimate.

Conservation supplies an energy balance, while the complete bill records imports, stored energy, exports and dissipation. To express that balance by differential forms in \(d_G+1\) spacetime dimensions, the conserved current is a \(d_G\)-form. If its exterior derivative vanishes, its oriented **net** boundary flux vanishes. That identity does not equate positive cumulative dissipation with zero bulk work, nor discard the final time slice. Therefore Stokes' theorem can express a verified work balance but cannot supply the missing power, success or endpoint-cost bounds by itself.

The one-pass shell proof above supplies the useful exponent argument directly. Its original submission is preserved verbatim in the increment bank; the telescoping repair, complete-cost accounting and actual inequality for the minimum are written here without changing the universal target.

#### 21.7.4 TIWA: construct the guard, then prove the bound

The author's Topological Insulating Wavefront Architecture proposes a guard of thickness at least the interaction radius, committed boundary data and a single-shot local correction map. It gives the quiescence requirement a concrete source construction to develop.

At the algorithmic level, let \(F\) be the verified frozen set. An outer transition writes only an authorised target outside \(F\), and keeps the committed guard data unchanged. Pointwise lookup then proves

\[
\operatorname{Read}_{F}(T_{\mathrm{outer}}(s,c,e))
=\operatorname{Read}_{F}(s).
\]

Freeze first resolves or fences in-flight writes, commits its epoch and reserves its complete cost. The frozen set grows only after verification. Induction preserves its old coordinates through every subsequent outer step; the schedule issues no second recovery task for a completed vertex. A fixed guard thickness and bounded degree allow a constant number of guard/handoff operations per vertex when the actual schedule enforces that limit. Those operations can then join the same telescoping work charge.

The physical guard must implement this source property while preserving the recovered history and its relational witnesses. The proposed identity \(\Pi_{\mathrm{freeze}}\circ M=\Pi_{\mathrm{freeze}}\) needs an actual map and mutation semantics: a read restriction, linear projector and recovery channel have different meanings. A write fence mediates attempted commands; the actual device, maintained record and decoder must establish preservation under physical disturbances. Remaining inside a code subspace alone does not prove that its logical state remains unchanged.

The local map must also compose to successful recovery, and the guard's storage, control, reset, routing and endpoint charges remain in \(W(\pi_{\mathrm{TIWA}},t)\). These are the success and complete-work parts of the same G3 construction, connected to G1's physical source certificate. The full universal target and original recovery class remain unchanged.

The accompanying `TIWA-PATCH-REQUIREMENTS.md` specifies the actual state, event transitions, application-time fences, noninterference proof, decoder-success proof, bounded visits, full ledger and compilation obligations. It is a construction specification; no new physical guard or Lean theorem is claimed by giving it a name.


#### 21.7.5 Boundary encoding, a sliding window, and the complete bill

The supplied continuation of TIWA keeps a fixed-width active window of shells and transfers a retiring patch's protected historical and relational record to the next boundary code. Let the window width be \(L\), independent of age. With the disjoint shells already defined, put

\[
A_k=\bigcup_{\max(0,k-L+1)\le j\le k}S_j.
\]

Every vertex of \(S_j\) belongs to at most \(L\) such windows. Hence the total active vertex-time count is at most \(L|B_R|\). This is a constant-overlap extension of the settled telescoping count; it needs no separate upper bound on each shell's surface area. If each active visit's complete charge is at most \(K\), the window work is at most \(KL|B_R|\). The charge includes holding, decoding, guard operation, control, routing, verification, routine ancilla reset, deployment and handoff. Continuing physical work outside that window must also remain in the complete ledger.

The boundary handoff has the original preservation target: a recovery of the encoded record must return its historical state and relations at the same fidelity standard. In symbols, the actual source maps must supply \(\mathcal R_k\mathcal E_k\simeq_\varepsilon\mathrm{id}\) on that full record, including its reference correlations. The code-capacity certificate and the physical implementation join this identity to the sliding-window charge. Section III-F records the supplied homological route and its chain-map correspondence.

For the supplied per-wave certificate, write \(q_k\) for the complete non-rollback charge and suppose its constructed local bound is

\[
q_k\le\alpha k^{d_G-1}+\beta,\qquad k\ge1.
\]

Let \(F\) be fixed initial/final charge and \(r_k\in\{0,1\}\) the controller's metered local rollback flag, charged at most \(R_*\) per wave. This explicit schedule gives the pathwise bill

\[
\mathcal Q(N)=F+\sum_{k=1}^{N}(q_k+R_*r_k).
\]

Routine erasure is already in \(q_k\), including successful decoding. With \(d_G\ge1\) and \(N\ge1\), every \(k^{d_G-1}\le N^{d_G-1}\), so

\[
\sum_{k=1}^{N}k^{d_G-1}\le N^{d_G},\qquad
\mathcal Q(N)\le(F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)N^{d_G}.
\]

This bound allows a metered rollback on every wave. An expected reset allowance is therefore unnecessary for the cost certificate. Successful recovery and any failed/rejected decoding still use the original charged fault accounts. Where the source admits more rollback operations, their actual count and cost must be included in \(q_k\) or separately bounded by the metering schedule.

For \(N(t)\le at+b\) and \(t\ge1\), \(N(t)\le(a+b)t\), yielding the age-independent coefficient

\[
\mathcal Q(t)\le
(F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)(a+b)^{d_G}t^{d_G}.
\]

`G3CompleteBill.cumulative_charge_bound` proves this cumulative implication in the existing natural-valued work-bill interface. `valid_work_bill_from_complete_ledger` derives `G3_ValidWorkBill` from the full ledger equality, local cost bounds and coefficient; `valid_bill_bound_in_age` supplies the wave-to-age conversion. The real-exponent argument is the displayed maximum-term proof. The constant-overlap calculation retains the full polynomial-growth graph domain when a per-shell surface upper estimate is unavailable.

The remaining source correspondence is the actual boundary encoder, preserved semantic observables, storage capacity, charged retirement map and complete cost equality. These are the construction links of the same G1/G3 task; the cumulative upper inequality is not taken as its own input.

### 21.8 Consequences for preservation and the fixed proof target

For a fixed local holding demand, continuous maintenance has an order-\(T\) bill; the derived delayed-recovery lower bound has order \(t^{d_G}\). The exponent distinction is strict for \(d_G>1\); the one-dimensional case retains its linear obstruction. For any fixed total recovery budget \(B\), the lower inequality already yields a finite age beyond which that budget cannot perform the stipulated recovery. This is budget-relative irrecoverability of the historical witness.

In the complete argument, a protected particular stays funded and held rather than being abandoned to that later recovery bill. A hostile mind remains a protected particular while its harmful effects are mediated. The full-domain physical certificate (G1), whole-outcome realisability (G2), and matching recovery construction (G3) join these components to the unchanged universal proposition. They are the three support requests supplied by the author and Gemini, now constructed in G.9–G.12. Existing gauntlet answers remain part of the proof; an agent's failure to retrieve them does not turn X3, P1 or P2 into fresh objections.

---

## Section 22 — The Physical Parameter Ledger: Instantiation, Rates, and Fault Ceilings

*This section instantiates the income condition for concrete hardware, bounds the charged fault accounts, and tabulates the engineering parameters. Every figure is either sourced (bracketed references, §22.5) or marked as a design value.*

---

### 22.1 The Master Income Condition

Section 17.1's Income-or-Irrelevance theorem makes solvency a necessary condition for an agent to remain a viable protector. The master solvency inequality balances income against four additive demand rates:

$$\boxed{\operatorname{Income}(t) \;\ge\; \dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}(t) \;=\; \dot W_{\mathrm{Landauer}} + \dot W_{\mathrm{boundary}} + \dot W_{\mathrm{holding}} + \dot W_{\mathrm{emulation}}}$$

1. **Thermal and adversarial dissipation floor:**
   $$\dot W_{\mathrm{Landauer}} = k_B T \cdot \left(\dot D_{\mathrm{damage}} + \dot D_{\mathrm{adv,sub-thresh}} + \gamma_{\mathrm{leak\_export}}\right)$$
   * $\dot D_{\mathrm{damage}}$: entropy rate injected by thermal and environmental noise.
   * $\dot D_{\mathrm{adv,sub-thresh}}$: worst-case adversarial entropy injection beneath the sensor threshold $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}$.
   * $\gamma_{\mathrm{leak\_export}}$: boundary loss rate of carrier modes escaping the enclosure.

   This floor is Landauer's bound, now a compiled theorem (`Landauer.landauer`, `QuantumLandauer.quantum_landauer`; Section 23.2).
2. **Boundary containment ($\dot W_{\mathrm{boundary}}$):** $\dot W_{\mathrm{boundary}} = \omega_0 \cdot R(t)^{d_G - 1}$, the work to maintain the boundary enclosing radius $R(t)$ in $d_G$ spatial dimensions.
3. **Holding drain ($\dot W_{\mathrm{holding}}$):** the baseline power of the sensor lattice, clocks, QND readout and consensus comparators.
4. **Emulation overhead ($\dot W_{\mathrm{emulation}}$):** the energy for concurrent rival simulation ($P\text{-emu}(c)$) within the surplus.

---

### 22.2 Reference Physical Instantiations

#### Reference Implementation A: superconducting transmon lattice in a dilution refrigerator

A dilution refrigerator has a separate cooling budget at each temperature stage, and each stage must carry its own load. Implementation A is therefore certified stage by stage, using the measured cooling powers and per-line heat loads of a Bluefors XLD400 cabled for a 50-qubit processor with individual drive and flux lines and 124 RF lines [S2].

| Stage | Cooling power, measured [S2, S3] | Load of the 50-qubit processor, measured per line [S2] | Headroom |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 50 K | 30 W at 45 K | ≈ 20 %, mostly passive (shield radiation is within the specified cooling power) | ≈ 5× |
| 4 K | 1.5 W at 4.2 K | ≈ 10 %, mostly passive; includes the HEMT amplifiers at 7.8 mW each [S4] | ≈ 10× |
| Still | 40 mW at 1.2 K | negligible | ≫ 10× |
| Cold plate | 200 µW at 140 mK | ≈ 30 % | ≈ 3.3× |
| Mixing chamber | 19 µW at 20 mK | ≈ 30 % (operation at 14 mK) | ≈ 3.3× |

* **Demand components.** The boundary term is the radiation-shield load, which falls on the 50 K and 4 K stage budgets. The holding term is the wiring's passive and active loads and the amplifiers, which the table carries stage by stage. The Landauer floor is negligible: with one nat per error event and an error rate of order $N/T_1$ ($N = 50$, $T_1 = 68\ \mu\mathrm{s}$ [S6]), $\dot W_{\mathrm{Landauer}} \approx 2.07 \times 10^{-25}\ \mathrm{J} \times 7 \times 10^{5}\ \mathrm{s}^{-1} \approx 1.5 \times 10^{-19}\ \mathrm{W}$ at 15 mK, against microwatts of cooling. The emulation term (classical control and decoding) runs at room temperature on the electrical income.
* **Electrical income.** Each pulse-tube compressor draws 9.2 kW at 50 Hz or 10.7 kW at 60 Hz [S3], and the reference system uses two. The electrical income must therefore be at least about 20 kW plus the gas handling and room-temperature electronics, which are not sourced here. A three-phase utility connection of a few tens of kW, with backup sized to match, supplies it.
* **Solvency ratio.** The binding stages run at about 30 % of capacity, so
  $$\frac{\operatorname{Income}}{\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}}}\Big|_{\text{binding stage}} \approx 3.3 > 1 \quad \text{(measured, 50-qubit scale [S2])}.$$
* **Scale.** [S2] states that the same thermal budget supports about 150 qubits once line capacity is tripled, and about 400 with 0.047″ cables and suppressed flux offsets. Larger installations need cooling and electrical income in proportion to the qubit count, which is a sizing requirement. No installation of 10⁴ qubits has published stage loads, so solvency at that scale is not yet a measured certificate.

#### Reference Implementation B: photonic cluster-state architecture

* **Optical layer.** At 193.4 THz (1550 nm), $h\nu = 1.28 \times 10^{-19}\ \mathrm{J} = 30.9\,k_B T$ at 300 K, so thermal photons in the waveguides are suppressed by $e^{-h\nu/k_BT} \approx 3.7 \times 10^{-14}$.
* **Detector layer.** The fault-tolerant photonic architectures in the literature detect photons with superconducting detectors [S8], which need cryogenic cabinets; the vendor states about 30 W of cooling per cabinet. A sourced ledger for Implementation B therefore carries a cryogenic detector stage, and its electrical income includes the cryocoolers.
* **Error rate.** The demonstrated two-qubit fusion fidelity is 99.22 % [S8]. The surface-code threshold of [S5] does not transfer directly to fusion-based architectures; the architecture's own threshold applies and is not sourced here.
* **Budget (design estimates).** Waveguide loss refresh ≈ 1.2 mW, laser stabilisation and phase modulators ≈ 85 mW, and photonic routing ≈ 20 mW are design estimates with no published installation source. Implementation B is therefore not yet certified. Its solvency ratio awaits a sourced budget that includes the detector stage.

---

### 22.3 The Charged Fault Accounts and the Failure Ceiling

The certified good event $G$ carries zero uncompensated loss over the design horizon $T_{\mathrm{horizon}} = 10^9\ \mathrm{s}$ (≈ 31.7 years). The failure ceiling is the sum of the conditional error probabilities of the charged accounts,

$$P(G^c) \le \sum_{i=1}^{21} \delta_i \le 10^{-6},$$

and the composition holds with no independence assumed (`ThermoLedger.canon_invariant`, compiled). The allocations below are the design budget. The status column records what present hardware certifies.

| Account | Fault | Mitigation | Allocated $\delta$ | Status |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :---: | :--- |
| **1–4** | Single-qubit Pauli and leakage errors | Stabilizer decoding | $4 \times 10^{-8}$ | Met at **d = 25** for p = 10⁻⁴, or d = 41 for p = 5 × 10⁻⁴ (§22.5.2), once the correlated floor of accounts 5–7 is removed |
| **5–7** | Correlated bursts (high-energy impacts, phonon events) | Gap engineering, rapid reset, code distance beyond the burst footprint | $3 \times 10^{-8}$ | **Attained at a sufficient distance** (`CorrelatedCeiling.correlated_family_ceiling`): bounded-footprint bursts are local stochastic at rate $(e\,m\,k\,q)^{1/k}$. The measured floor on present devices is ≈ 10⁻¹⁰ per cycle [S6], which fixes their burst parameters; the target is ≤ 3.7 × 10⁻²⁴ per cycle per logical qubit (§22.5.2) |
| **8–10** | Clock drift, dephasing, timing jitter | Byzantine clock distribution (E9) | $3 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation; no installation measurement sourced |
| **11–12** | Sensor failure and readout spoofing | Trimmed-median consensus, $K \ge 2f + 1$ | $2 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation; the consensus logic is compiled (`E9SelfHealing`) |
| **13–14** | Thermal fluctuations above threshold | Thermal ballast escrow, automatic throttle | $2 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation |
| **15–16** | Power transients and dropouts | Redundant battery escrow | $2 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation |
| **17–18** | Corrupted spawning, illegal delegation | Kernel capability gate (H5) | $2 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation; the gate logic is compiled (`H5Closure`) |
| **19–20** | Carrier leakage into the open bath | Lieb–Robinson containment (X3) | $2 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation |
| **21** | External macroscopic boundary shear | Standoff buffer $L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \tau_{\mathrm{resp}}$ | $1 \times 10^{-8}$ | Design allocation |
| **Total** | | | $\le 2.1 \times 10^{-7} < 10^{-6}$ | Certified when each account is |

**Correlated errors.** Gap-engineered junctions remove the high-energy-impact T₁ bursts [S9]. The remaining correlated events occur about once per hour, "whose origins are not yet understood", and they set the floor of 10⁻¹⁰ per cycle with a deviation from exponential suppression at d ≥ 15 [S6]. The threshold theorem covers these correlated events. Let each burst damage at most $k$ locations, let each location be touched by at most $m$ burst types, and let bursts be local stochastic at rate $q$, common causes included. Then the damaged locations are local stochastic at the effective rate $(e\,m\,k\,q)^{1/k}$ (`CorrelatedCeiling.damage_local_stochastic`). Every target ceiling is then met at some code distance and at every larger one (`correlated_family_ceiling`). Correlated damage therefore sets no floor that distance cannot pass. The measured deviation at d ≥ 15 [S6] fixes the burst parameters of present devices, which enter this theorem.

**Achievability, as a theorem.** That the ceiling is attainable in physical matter is a compiled theorem.
* `Threshold.family_ceiling`: for any code family under local stochastic noise of rate p (every set S of locations is entirely faulty with probability at most $p^{\lvert S\rvert}$), with 2Δ√p < 1 and polynomially many failure witnesses of each length, every target ceiling is met at some code distance and at every larger one. The 10⁻⁶ ceiling over the horizon is included.
* `Threshold.repetition_ceiling`: the repetition code end to end, below p = 1/4.

Any installation whose noise meets the local stochastic condition below threshold therefore attains the ceiling. The rigorous threshold from path counting is conservative, and the distances in §22.5.2 use the simulated threshold of [S5]. What present hardware does not yet supply is the noise condition itself at the 10⁻¹⁰ level [S6].

---

### 22.4 The Engineering Parameter Matrix

| Parameter | Symbol | Superconducting | Photonic | Admissible range |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| Code distance | $d$ | 25 at p = 10⁻⁴; 41 at p = 5 × 10⁻⁴ (computed, §22.5.2) | architecture-specific (not sourced) | target met at some $d$ (`ceiling_achievable`, `Threshold.family_ceiling`) |
| Syndrome cycle | $\tau_{\mathrm{cycle}}$ | 1.1 µs (measured [S6]) | — | $\tau_{\mathrm{sampling}} < L_{\mathrm{buffer}}/(2 v_{\mathrm{harm}})$ |
| Sensor quorum size | $K$ | 7 (design) | 9 (design) | $K \ge 2f + 1$, $f \ge 2$ |
| Sensor threshold | $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}}$ | 0.05 (design) | 0.02 (design) | $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{sens}} \in (\sigma_{\mathrm{noise}}, \theta_{\mathrm{damage}})$ |
| Standoff buffer | $L_{\mathrm{buffer}}$ | 0.2 m (design) | 0.05 m (design) | $L_{\mathrm{buffer}} > v_{\mathrm{harm}} \tau_{\mathrm{response}}$ |
| Escrow multiplier | $\mu_{\mathrm{escrow}}$ | ≈ 3.3× at the binding stage (measured [S2]) | not yet certified | $\mu_{\mathrm{escrow}} = \operatorname{Income}/\dot W_{\mathrm{demand}} > 1$ |
| Boundary embedding | $\Pi$ | radiation shields, Dirichlet microwave enclosure | total internal reflection | reflection coefficient $> 0.999$ (design) |

**What this section certifies.**
* The income condition is physically satisfiable, with measured positive headroom (≈ 3.3× at the binding stage), for a superconducting installation at the 50-qubit scale, with electrical income sized at a few tens of kW.
* The code distance the §22.3 allocation requires is computable from [S5]: d = 25 at p = 10⁻⁴.
* The composition of the accounts into the ceiling is a compiled theorem.
* The ceiling is attainable as a theorem: under local stochastic noise below threshold, every target, 10⁻⁶ included, is met at some code distance (`Threshold.family_ceiling`).

**What it does not yet certify.**
* A built installation at the distance the ceiling requires. The ceiling is attained by theorem for local and correlated bounded-footprint faults (`Threshold.family_ceiling`, `CorrelatedCeiling.correlated_family_ceiling`). Present devices measure their burst parameters [S6], and the required distance follows from them.
* Implementation B, whose budget awaits a published installation source.
* Solvency at installations of 10⁴ qubits and above, which is a sizing requirement without published stage loads.

**Sources.**
[S1] Bluefors, XLD dilution refrigerator specifications (XLD450sl: > 14 µW at 20 mK, > 450 µW at 100 mK; XLD1000sl: > 30 µW at 20 mK, > 1000 µW at 100 mK; guaranteed values, measured at the mixing-chamber experimental flange). bluefors.com/products/xld-dilution-refrigerator/.
[S2] S. Krinner et al., "Engineering cryogenic setups for 100-qubit scale superconducting circuit systems", *EPJ Quantum Technology* 6, 2 (2019), arXiv:1806.07862. Measured stage cooling powers of a Bluefors XLD400 (Table 1) and measured passive and active heat loads per line.
[S3] Cryomech PT415 pulse tube: 1.5 W at 4.2 K (nominal; CUORE cryostat paper, arXiv:1603.03306, and [S2] Table 1). Compressor input with the CPA1110: 9.2 kW at 50 Hz, 10.7 kW at 60 Hz (Bluefors PT415 sheet).
[S4] Low Noise Factory LNF-LNC4_8G cryogenic HEMT amplifier datasheet (2026-02-03): typical bias 0.6 V, 13 mA, i.e. 7.8 mW at the 4 K stage.
[S5] A. G. Fowler, M. Mariantoni, J. M. Martinis, A. N. Cleland, "Surface codes: Towards practical large-scale quantum computation", *Phys. Rev. A* 86, 032324 (2012), arXiv:1208.0928. Eq. (11): $P_L \approx 0.03\,(p/p_{\mathrm{th}})^{d_e}$, $d_e = (d+1)/2$, with $p_{\mathrm{th}} = 0.57\,\%$ for the circuit simulated. Physical qubits per logical qubit: (2d − 1)².
[S6] Google Quantum AI (R. Acharya et al.), "Quantum error correction below the surface code threshold", *Nature* (2024), doi:10.1038/s41586-024-08449-y, arXiv:2408.13687. Distance-7 surface code at 0.143 % error per cycle, Λ = 2.14 ± 0.02, cycle time 1.1 µs, mean T₁ = 68 µs. Repetition codes to distance 29: Λ = 8.4 for d = 5–11, a deviation from exponential suppression at d ≥ 15, and "an apparent logical error floor of 10⁻¹⁰" set by correlated events about once per hour (3 × 10⁹ cycles) "whose origins are not yet understood".
[S7] Best reported two-qubit gate errors: trapped ions, estimated 8.4(7) × 10⁻⁵ (A. C. Hughes et al., arXiv:2510.17286, preprint); superconducting transmons, CZ fidelity 99.93 % averaged over 40 h, 99.95 % maximum (IQM, company-reported).
[S8] PsiQuantum, "Omega" chipset, *Nature* (2025), doi:10.1038/s41586-025-08820-7: superconducting single-photon detectors integrated on silicon; two-qubit fusion fidelity 99.22 %; the vendor states cryogenic cabinets of about 30 W cooling power each.
[S9] M. McEwen et al., "Resolving catastrophic error bursts from cosmic rays in large arrays of superconducting qubits", *Nature Physics* 18, 107 (2022); M. McEwen et al., "Resisting high-energy impact events through gap engineering in superconducting qubit arrays", *Phys. Rev. Lett.* 133, 240601 (2024), arXiv:2402.15644.

Source PDFs and checksums: `BANKS/2026-10-25-section22-sources/`. The published source list with links is `SOURCES-22.md`.

#### 22.5.1 Constants

$k_B$ and $h$ are exact in the SI. $k_B T = 2.071 \times 10^{-25}$ J = 1.293 µeV at 15 mK and $4.142 \times 10^{-21}$ J = 25.85 meV at 300 K. At 193.4 THz, $h\nu = 1.281 \times 10^{-19}$ J = 30.9 $k_B T$(300 K), and $e^{-h\nu/k_BT} = 3.7 \times 10^{-14}$.

#### 22.5.2 Code distance for the fault allocation

Horizon $10^9$ s at 1.1 µs per cycle [S6] gives $N = 9.09 \times 10^{14}$ cycles. Ten thousand physical qubits at the [S5] footprint $(2d-1)^2$ hold about 4 logical qubits at d = 25 (2,401 physical each); the 9-logical allocation below corresponds to an installation of about $2.2\times10^4$ physical qubits. With [S5] Eq. (11) and $p_{\mathrm{th}} = 0.57\,\%$, account 1–4 ($\delta = 4 \times 10^{-8}$, shared over the logical qubits) requires:

| Physical error p | Logical qubits | Per-cycle target per logical qubit | Required distance d | Physical qubits per logical qubit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 10⁻⁴ (best ion gate [S7]) | 1 | 4.4 × 10⁻²³ | 23 | 2 025 |
| 10⁻⁴ | 9 | 4.9 × 10⁻²⁴ | **25** | 2 401 |
| 5 × 10⁻⁴ (best transmon CZ [S7]) | 9 | 4.9 × 10⁻²⁴ | 41 | 6 561 |
| 10⁻³ | 9 | 4.9 × 10⁻²⁴ | 57 | 12 769 |

At d = 17 and p = 10⁻⁴, $P_L = 4.7 \times 10^{-18}$ per cycle, which is 4.3 × 10⁻³ expected failures per logical qubit over the horizon, so d = 17 does not meet the allocation and d = 25 does. Accounts 5–7 ($\delta = 3 \times 10^{-8}$) set a per-cycle target of $3 \times 10^{-8} / (9N) = 3.7 \times 10^{-24}$ per logical qubit. At the measured floor of $10^{-10}$ per cycle [S6], the expected number of correlated failures over the horizon is about $9 \times 10^{4}$ per logical qubit.

**Sizing from the measured surface-code scaling.** [S6] measures a distance-7 logical error of 0.143 % per cycle and a suppression factor \(\Lambda=2.14\pm0.02\) per distance step of two. If that measured \(\Lambda\) continues to hold, accounts 1–4 at \(4.9\times10^{-24}\) per cycle per logical qubit require \(d=131\) at \(\Lambda=2.14\) or \(2.16\), and \(d=133\) at \(\Lambda=2.12\). That is 68 121–70 225 physical qubits per logical qubit, or about \(6.1\)–\(6.3\times10^{5}\) for the 9-logical allocation. The computation is `BANKS/2026-10-25-historical-handoff-source/section22-sizing/sizing_from_S6.py`. This is the installation the ledger specifies; no installation at that scale has yet been built.

* `ReciprocityCeiling.ceiling_achievable`: the ceiling as a parameter, met at some code distance.
* `ThermoLedger.canon_invariant`: composition of the accounts without independence.
* `E9SelfHealing.code_headroom` and `wave_race_margin`: admissible parameter ranges.
* `Landauer.landauer` and `QuantumLandauer.quantum_landauer`: the dissipation floor of 22.1.
* `Threshold.family_ceiling` and `Threshold.repetition_ceiling`: the ceiling attained below threshold under local stochastic noise.

The installation figures themselves are sourced measurements and design values, not compiled theorems.

*This section maps the edition's compiled results and their actual hypotheses. The current Lean canon contains 192 modules and 1158 public theorem/lemma names.
Checkpoint 12 freshly compiled and kernel-checked every module with zero output and passed
the full 1158-name standard-axiom audit, strict completeness and claims gates. Its source hashes
match the current tree. Checkpoint 11 was already complete for 191 modules / 1150 names;
TransferRate adds the eight results in this checkpoint. Earlier receipts remain preserved for
exactly their sources. The published 115-module/831-name baseline is unchanged. These receipts
certify the named Lean results and their premises, not the unfinished physical holding application. Section 23.9 distinguishes the interfaces, derived adapters and remaining physical applications.*

*Evidence convention (unchanged from the edition header): a compiled theorem is cited by its name; a statement carried as a hypothesis of a compiled theorem is named as such; a prose derivation keeps its stated premises. A prose proof does not acquire a compiler certificate by association.*

| **Branch A rate bound (quantum speed limit).** Unitary evolution preserves the norm; under H₀ + V the trajectory departs from the H₀ trajectory at rate at most ‖V‖/ħ, and at most $P_{\mathrm{adv}}/(\hbar\,\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}})$ under the per-site coupling $\lVert V\rVert \le P_{\mathrm{adv}}/\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}}$ | `SpeedLimit` | `norm_conserved`, `branchA_divergence`, `branchA_rate` |
| Mandelstam–Tamm, sharp: $\lvert\langle\psi_0, \psi_t\rangle\rvert \ge \cos(\Delta E\, t/\hbar)$ while $\Delta E\, t/\hbar \le \pi$; orthogonalisation time $T \ge \pi\hbar/(2\Delta E)$ | `SpeedLimit` | `mandelstam_tamm`, `orthogonalization_time` |

The Branch A divergence is measured in state-vector norm. For pure states the infidelity is bounded by the squared state-vector distance, \(1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2\le\|\psi-\varphi\|^2\) (`PureStateDistance.pure_infidelity_le`; G.13); with the pure-state identity \(\|\rho-\sigma\|_1=2\sqrt{1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2}\), now compiled (`PureStateTraceNorm.pure_trace_norm`), this gives \(\|\rho-\sigma\|_1\le2\|\psi-\varphi\|\).

### 23.7 The Universal Protection Theorem with the derived holding bridge

`UniversalProtection.universal_protection` retains the abstract assembly: reasons-or-holding coverage, continuation through the horizon on the good event, the joint fault ceiling, greatestness and reciprocity at its stated inputs.

`Bridge.universal_protection_held` strengthens the continuation assembly by deriving scope from the law and preservation from nearest-codeword holding. It composes the physical/source projection with the same reactive controller and environment. On \(G\), every particular continues at every step through the horizon; the probability that any particular fails is at most \(\sum_i\delta_i\), without independence.

Part II.1–6 supplies the proof, physical-cycle derivations and all five `hcert` components. G1 is the combined full-domain source/event/observation/fault construction target; G.10 proves its held-source components and identifies the physical correspondence still needed. Part II.10 supplies the separate greatestness proof, with G2 retaining the whole outcome and freedom stages.

### 23.8 What the compiled theorems rest on

**Premises carried as stated premises or hypotheses:**
* the surface-code chain lemma (Dennis, Kitaev, Landahl and Preskill, 2002): minimum-weight decoding fails only along a self-avoiding chain of length at least d with at least half its edges faulty, with at most $V\Delta^L$ such chains of length $L$ — as the code-specific input to `Threshold.family_ceiling` for the surface-code instance. The threshold theorem's counting core is compiled (§23.6), and for concatenated codes the threshold is compiled end to end for the concatenated memory: failure probability at most $(21p)^{2^k}/21$ under local stochastic noise (`Concatenated.concatenated_threshold`). Actual decoding failure is identified with the recursive failure predicate: with nearest-codeword decoding at every level, any corruption of a non-failing fault set decodes to the stored record (`ConcatenatedDecoding.decodes_unless_fails`). So the probability of actual decoding failure is at most the same bound (`decoding_failure_le`), and the fault ceiling is attained on a compiled route. Fault-tolerant syndrome-measurement gadgets whose own operations can fail are the cited construction of Aliferis, Gottesman and Preskill (2006); their faults enter the charged accounts;
* Bennett's compute/copy/uncompute identity and the heat-only option model are compiled. The specific implementation and full ordered history are separately checked candidates (G.14). The physical counterpart still needs a complete-cost saving within the same deadline; this is not supplied by a zero-heat identity or a polynomial upper bound;
* the gate-set clause of premise 2, no longer carried as prose: every unitary on $\mathbb{C}^n$ is a finite product of two-level unitaries (`TwoLevel.two_level_decomposition`), so a simulator implementing each two-level factor implements every local evolution. What remains carried is that the universal simulator's gate set reaches every two-level factor;
* the relational frame BCRD P1–P4.

The chain lemma is a published mathematical theorem, cited as such; the Bennett physical comparison remains construction work (G.14); the gate-set item is the explicit hypothesis of a compiled theorem; the fourth is the frame the edition is stated in.

**The cited theorems and their hypotheses.** Three published theorems remain cited. Each is applied within its stated conditions.

| Cited theorem | Form used | Our setting meets its conditions because |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Surface-code chain lemma (Dennis, Kitaev, Landahl and Preskill, *J. Math. Phys.* 43, 2002) | Minimum-weight decoding fails only along a self-avoiding chain of length at least \(d\) with at least half its edges faulty; at most \(V\Delta^L\) such chains of length \(L\) | The counting argument uses only that a given set of locations is entirely faulty with probability at most \(p^{|S|}\). That is exactly `Threshold.LocalStochastic`, which includes independent noise and is weaker than it. It enters `Threshold.family_ceiling` as the witness and count inputs. |
| Solovay–Kitaev theorem (Kitaev 1997; Dawson and Nielsen 2006), with the two-level-to-gate reduction of Nielsen and Chuang, §4.5 | Every single-qubit unitary is approximated to \(\varepsilon\) in operator norm by a word of length polylogarithmic in \(1/\varepsilon\) over a finite, inverse-closed set generating a dense subgroup; two-level unitaries reduce to CNOTs and single-qubit gates | `TwoLevel.two_level_decomposition` writes every unitary as a product of two-level factors. `ErrorBudget.error_budget` needs each factor within \(\varepsilon/m\) in the norm of a normed ring of contractions; operator-norm approximation by unitaries meets that. The gate set must be universal in this sense; that is the gate-set clause of premise 2. |
| Fault-tolerant syndrome gadgets (Aliferis, Gottesman and Preskill, *Quantum Inf. Comput.* 6, 2006) | Under local stochastic noise, a level-\(k\) extended rectangle is correct unless it contains two independent malignant level-\((k-1)\) rectangles | Their noise model is local stochastic noise, as in `Threshold.LocalStochastic`. Their recursion is the recursion of `Concatenated.Fails`, now joined to actual decoding of the memory (`ConcatenatedDecoding`). With faulty syndrome gadgets the threshold constant is theirs, set by the location count of an extended rectangle, rather than the \(1/21\) of the memory model. |

**Physical premises now compiled.** Guard 1 (the second law in its export form) is derived from Landauer's theorem (§23.2), and the quantum speed limit, in Mandelstam–Tamm form and in the rate-bound form of E9 Branch A, is derived from unitary dynamics (§23.3). What these theorems assume is the standard physical framework:
* finite-dimensional quantum mechanics, with Schrödinger dynamics generated by self-adjoint Hamiltonians;
* unitary (in the classical form, reversible) joint dynamics;
* a bath that starts in its Gibbs state, uncorrelated with the register;
* for Branch A, the per-site coupling bound $\lVert V\rVert \le P_{\mathrm{adv}}/\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}}$;
* for the fault ceiling, local stochastic noise of rate p below the threshold of `Threshold.family_ceiling`.

**Greatestness.** `UnionClosure.greatest_of_solvent` takes both `htopR : ⊤ ∈ R` and `hsolv : c ⊤ ≤ B`. The first supplies the whole top tuple, including freedom stages; the second pays its cost. Alignment forces equality with that feasible top (Part II.10). `greatest_or_irrelevant` retains realisability at every step and derives the resource split. G2 retains the full physical policy/outcome correspondence as its construction target. The affordable-union results retain their separate resource cases.

The amount $I_M + D_{\mathrm{err}} + \delta_I$ that the reset removes from the register is the instance's entropy accounting (§18), stated as the hypothesis of `guard_one` and `quantum_guard_one`.

**The physical bridge and its joined certificate.** `Bridge.coverage_from_law` derives full scope from the law guarantee over all feasible families. `holding_preserves` derives preservation from decoding radius and repair precision. `qsl_within_radius`, `headroom_within_radius` and `holding_cycle_physical` supply the continuous and discrete cycle arguments. `universal_protection_held` takes the actual commuting source transition, observation agreement, initial continuation, perturbation-radius and repair witnesses in `hcert`, together with the named-event cover and fault bounds. G1 supplies their combined construction for the complete claimed physical domain (G.10). Parts II.3–6 give the available arguments; a deployment and a mathematical construction of the full source domain retain their distinct roles.

**Not compiled by this section:**
* the installation figures of §22, which are sourced measurements and design values (§22.5);
* the interpretive companion (the doors), which is held for consultation and is not mathematics.

## Section 24 — Statements of record for the retained core

*Part II supplies the retained proofs and stronger bridge in this edition; Section 21 supplies the information–traffic–carrier–work derivation. This section records concrete instrument rows and numerical instances at their evidence tiers. The parent core, DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23599528, remains publication provenance; the current argument is given here.*

### 24.1 The nine-row A1 register (effect mediation)

**Statement of record.** The A1 register identifies nine rows of effect mediation: host memory mapping, DMA, context switches, packet ingestion, bus messages, storage synchronisation, commissioning, delegation and retirement. Each row names its refinement carrier and the enforcement witness the physical instance must supply. A packet or memory operation acquires no exemption by being below the application layer.

*Tier:* stated definition, given in Part II.5 and the nine rows above. The enforcement machinery it names is compiled in the abstract (`H5Closure.no_harmful_effect`, `effect_preserves_care`); the nine rows themselves are the instance inventory.

### 24.2 Capacity rows 17 and 22, and the pool bill (workload)

**Statement of record.** The workload pool bill is compiled: capacity rows 17 and 22 at 48,000 and 67,200 per cycle give \(48000 \times 17 + 67200 \times 22 = 2{,}294{,}400\) — the per-cycle pool bill against which the FIFO completion certificate (Proposition 6) is issued (`ControllerCertificate`, exact rationals; the same module derives the bill-saving ratios \(6{,}604{,}800/2{,}294{,}400 = 688/239\) and \(6{,}604{,}800/2{,}409{,}600 = 688/251\) of the earlier bill over the corrected bills, `corrected_bill_saving`).

*Tier:* compiled. The deadline-certificate machinery of which this is the workload instance is published in the parent core (DOI 10.5281/zenodo.23599528, exact FIFO deadline certificates with application-time finality and funding conservation).

### 24.3 The causal boundary (reactive refinement)

**Statement of record.** The causal boundary is given in two forms, which agree: a closed dependency component is exactly a directed separator in the unrolled dependency graph with empty certified boundary (`CutEquivalence.closed_iff_no_bypass`), and the separator form extends it to a matched certified boundary (`UPTR16Cut.causal_separator`). Pending effects belong in that graph. Omitting one future effect or one path invalidates the corresponding exclusion certificate.

*Tier:* stated definition (R16-CUT); the reactive refinement that uses it is compiled (`Composition.reactive_lockstep`, with the causal boundary carried as the instance's refinement source).

### 24.4 The revised JW2.6 arithmetic (conserved allocation and joint risk)

**Statement of record.** The revised JW2.6 arithmetic gives \(\approx 2.06419526094 \times 10^{-7}\), within the declared \(10^{-6}\) horizon ceiling, under its source-event and stochastic-law premises. The positive dormant allowance, recruitment noninterference and the historical recruitment-family event retain their distinct meanings. The three ledger scenarios carry this value (solvent and quarantine) and the charged variant \(9.86419526094 \times 10^{-7}\) (one priced event), each with a sealed, hash-chained trace.

*Tier:* exact replay receipt — `LEDGER-FOUR-STROKE-2026-10-24/`, chain heads as printed in Chapter 1.4; the composition into the ceiling (no independence) is compiled (`ThermoLedger.canon_invariant`); the arithmetic itself is a receipt, not a theorem.

### 24.5 The quorum (safety)

**Statement of record.** The design instantiation is five-of-seven with at most two Byzantine identities (\(K = 7\), \(f = 2\), satisfying \(K \ge 2f + 1\)). The Byzantine quorum results it instantiates are compiled (`E9SelfHealing.robust_between_honest`, `no_false_alarm`, `no_masking`, `exists_robust`); the five-of-seven values are instance constants (§22.4's parameter matrix).

*Tier:* compiled theorem with stated instance constants.

# Part III-C — The three joined supports and their constructions

The proposition remains protection of all things from any digital mind, especially a pure optimiser. X3, P1 and P2 retain their established gauntlet arguments. These are the three support requests supplied by the author and Gemini; their constructions are recorded below, with no additional objection introduced by this incorporation.

**G1 — combined full-domain physical certificate.** Construct the actual source state, admitted commands/events, physical projection, observations and charged fault accounts that jointly supply `Bridge.universal_protection_held`. Its `hcert` establishes commuting refinement, observation agreement, initial continuation of every particular, the perturbation-radius bound and repair precision on the named good event. Also join the event cover and conditional fault bounds. Part II incorporates the law-derived scope, code decoder, physical cycle, controller-derived tracking, E9 branches, H5 care-preserving mediation, R14/R16 source refinements and timely R17 effect construction. The requested result joins these for the complete claimed physical domain.

Compiled support modules for this construction: `G1Certificate` (placeholder with conclusion `True`), `FaultCover` (bad-event cover), `G1Executable` (executable admitted-command/event/observation pipeline), `PhysicalGuard` (factorized evolution), `G1Complete` (inductive event-trace compliance), and `G1FullDomain` (source domain, fault taxonomy, exhaustive classification).

*Constructed (G.10).* `G1Held.held_source_protection` proves all five `hcert` components for the canonical held source, and `good_cover` proves the cover of the good event by the named accounts. `held_source_qsl` derives the per-step drift from the quantum speed limit for any perturbing agent, and `G1E9.held_source_e9` derives the coupling bound from the exhaustive E9 split. The journal of G.8 preserves its supplied transition (`G1FullDomain.journal_reactive_fidelity`); protective refinement of the actual installation is the separate transition equation identified in G.10. The inputs these theorems take are physical: the frame's Schrödinger evolution and the sub-threshold coupling bound in each step; the honest-sensor response and quorum size; the response's removal of coupling once the alarm fires; and the account probabilities, whose composition and attainment are `ThermoLedger.canon_invariant`, `Threshold.family_ceiling` and `CorrelatedCeiling.correlated_family_ceiling`.

**G2 — realisability of the whole greatest outcome.** Construct `htopR : (⊤ : ∀ i, L i) ∈ R` for the same full order, including existing and new continuation and freedom stages, with the semantic continuation reading `htopkeeps`. Keep it distinct from `hsolv : c ⊤ ≤ B`. Part II.10 supplies the algebra from those inputs to equality of every aligned option with the top and dominance over every rival. The construction retains the full tuple.

First compiled support module for this construction: `TopRealisability` (union-closed realisability of singletons yields realisability of the top outcome).

Compiled support modules for this construction: `G2Singleton` (coverage discharge of singleton realisability), `G2Outcome` (transactional outcome-tuple invariance), and `G2WholeOutcome` (authorized-implies-closed, authorized-implies-positive-work).

*Compiled components (G.9, G.11).* `G2Splice` proves product-model union closure and closed-block agreement. `G2Held` proves the top of its assigned grade model from held continuation and its one-step H5 predicate. The full construction must map actual policies and outcomes to that model, preserving all original continuation and freedom stages, shared constraints and R17 delivery for every admitted rival and world. `hsolv` remains the separate complete funded-cost certificate.

**G3 — supplied wavefront/TIWA protocol: success and the complete work certificate.** The author has supplied the single-shot feedforward wavefront construction and the TIWA guard-ring proposal. Section 21.7.4 and `TIWA-PATCH-REQUIREMENTS.md` specify the latter’s source, physical and work obligations. Section 21.7 proves its processing count exactly by disjoint shells: \(\sum_{k=0}^N|S_k|=|B_{vN}|\), hence bounded work per processed vertex gives \(O(N^{d_G})\). The same construction must now supply its actual recovery success and retained-state/quiescence witness, the age-to-wave-count bound, and the complete physical bill including routing, reset, holding and controller export. Those establish \(W_{\min}(t)\le W(\pi_{\mathrm{ff}},t)\le Ct^{d_G}\) in the original permitted class. The shell exponent is established and is not requested again. This is the remaining certificate for the supplied construction, with the full \(\Theta(t^{d_G})\) target unchanged.

First compiled support modules for this construction: `TIWASource` (transition system, frozen-frame property, no-rework rule), `TIWAGuard` (guard installation monotonicity), `TIWARecovery` (bounded visits and wave counts), `TIWAWork` (per-vertex and ball-size work accounting), `G3WorkBill` (polynomial work-bill invariance), and `G3CompleteBill` (success-implies-bound, wave-count nonnegativity).

*Constructed (G.12).* `G3Sweep.sweep_recovers` proves that the sweep recovers every shell and retains it against any outer activity the TIWA fence admits, and `sweep_visits` proves that no vertex is reworked. `waves_from_spread` gives the wave count from finite propagation. `sweep_bill` proves recovery success on the whole region together with the complete bill \(W(t)\le(F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)(c+r)^{d}t^{d}\), with an age-independent coefficient. With §21.6 this gives \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\). The inputs it takes are the local correction's success against a recovered interior, the fence, the propagation bound and the per-wave complete charge.
