# Part III-D — Transducer, trajectory and semantic constructions

These constructions retain their full proposed conclusions. Their source correspondence and complete bill join the verified components, rather than being certified by a title or signature.

## D.1 Transduced Preservation Theorem (TPT)

The supplied transducer route uses a macroscopic dissipation factor \(\Gamma\approx10^{14}\), an adiabatic efficiency \(\alpha\in(0,1]\), and a boundary transducer \(\tau:\mathcal H_{\mathrm{phys}}\to\mathcal M_{\mathrm{rel}}\). Its target is the complete physical work ceiling \(W(t)\le Ct^{d_G}\), preserving the protected historical record and relations.

Every exterior transducer, reset, holding and controller charge remains in that total. A fixed multiplicative hardware overhead can enter \(C\); deriving the complete bill still requires the actual device and operation count. Section 21.7.5 gives the cumulative ledger implication for the supplied sliding-window route.

## D.2 Topological entropy and trajectory bounding

For the supplied trajectory count \(N(t,\varepsilon)\le Kt^{d_G}\) and complete work correspondence \(W(t)=\beta N(t,\varepsilon)\), the intended conclusion is \(W(t)\le\beta Kt^{d_G}\). The transducer construction must supply that actual trajectory estimate and the work correspondence, including every physical charge.

The pinned Mathlib already contains Bowen–Dinaburg dynamical nets and topological entropy in `Mathlib.Dynamics.TopologicalEntropy.NetEntropy` and `CoverEntropy`. It also proves the general Hilbert-space mean-ergodic theorem, `ContinuousLinearMap.tendsto_birkhoffAverage_orthogonalProjection`, for a continuous linear contraction. Missing upstream infrastructure is not a blocker for these results. Applying them requires the actual operator and the claimed growth/work correspondence.

## D.3 Top continuation and the constructed evaluation fixed point

The `Htopkeeps` record carries `eval ⊤ = ⊤` as its unitality field. Its extractor theorem does not by itself supply the different continuation semantics used in `SolventReciprocity`.

The supplied decoder interface now has a derived construction. For its code inclusion and recovery, put \(P=\mathrm{encode}\circ\mathrm{decode}\). Retraction gives \(P(P(y))=P(y)\) and \(P(\mathrm{encode}(c))=\mathrm{encode}(c)\). `DecoderRetraction.finite_code_self_grounding` constructs this decoder and a non-bottom fixed-point witness from a maintained non-bottom code record. Part III-F connects that result to the proposed ontological capstone while retaining the actual G1 source obligation.

# Part III-F — Unified architecture and the self-grounding capstone

This section integrates the author's supplied unified ledger and homological proposal into the full edition. Parts I–III and their maximal domain, outcome order and rival quantifiers remain in force. The original submissions are preserved with the integration record.

## F.1 The unified gate and work ledger

The guard is an idempotent, monotone projection with the intended fault-nullification action. The transaction emits \((h_{\mathrm{pre}},h_{\mathrm{post}},w,a,c)\) and enforces \(a=1\Rightarrow(c=1\land w>0)\). The complete work functional is additive over executed traces and has the full target \(\mathcal W(T)\le Ct^{d_G}\).

The transaction invariant supports the source implementation of G2; its connection to the whole greatest outcome, including all continuation and freedom stages, remains explicit. The work derivation in 21.7.5 now supplies `G3_ValidWorkBill` from its complete local ledger. Guard operation, transduction, boundary encoding, retirement and preserved history belong to that same charged execution.

## F.2 Historical boundary encoding and recovery

Part III-G supplies the explicit operator/coordinate encoder, adjoint recovery and historical query transport developed from the later three-pillar submission. The topological route below retains its faithful source correspondence.

The supplied history is a causal labelled graph \(\mathcal H=(V,E,\phi)\). Its full preservation target is

\[
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{bnd}}(\mathcal E_{\mathrm{bnd}}(\mathcal H),v)=\phi(v)
\]

at the original fidelity standard, together with preservation of the relevant relations. Within its proved radius, the finite-code decoder returns the exact original codeword. The boundary encoding separately specifies which full historical record that codeword represents.

The supplied accumulator route retains the proposed representation \((\partial\mathcal H,\operatorname{Acc}(V),\bigoplus_{e\in E}\psi(e))\) and capacity target

\[
|\mathcal B_{\mathrm{boundary}}(t)|\le K\log\operatorname{Vol}(U(t)).
\]

Its capacity certificate must count the physical information in the accumulator, payload, query witnesses and retained recovery data. An accumulator authenticates supplied data and membership; an exact reconstruction additionally supplies the data needed to return \(\phi(v)\).

The supplied retirement charge is

\[
W_{\mathrm{retire}}
=c_{\mathrm{acc}}|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|
+c_{\mathrm{bound}}|E_{\mathrm{retire}}|\log|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|.
\]

For the protocol's fixed-radius local retirement patches, bounded degree gives \(|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|\le M_0\) and \(|E_{\mathrm{retire}}|\le D_{\max}|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|\). Thus this local term is at most \((c_{\mathrm{acc}}+c_{\mathrm{bound}}D_{\max}\log M_0)|V_{\mathrm{retire}}|\), a constant charge per retired vertex. The settled bounded-overlap count absorbs it into \(O(t^{d_G})\). Global accumulator updates, witness generation, routing, reset and continued storage still enter the complete local or separately metered charge.

The proposed topological route represents history by a chain complex and uses

\[
0\longrightarrow C_*(\partial U)\xrightarrow{i}C_*(K)
\longrightarrow C_*(K,\partial U)\longrightarrow0.
\]

Its long exact sequence contains

\[
H_n(\partial U)\xrightarrow{i_*}H_n(K)\xrightarrow{j_*}
H_n(K,\partial U)\xrightarrow{\delta_n}H_{n-1}(\partial U).
\]

The supplied route is implemented by constructing a boundary chain map \(\rho:C_*(K)\to C_*(\partial U)\), inclusion \(i\), and chain homotopy

\[
i\rho-\mathrm{id}=dh+hd.
\]

For a cycle \(z\), the recovered chain satisfies \(i\rho z-z=d(hz)\), so its homology class is preserved. A protected semantic observable \(q\) that annihilates these exact terms then obeys \(q(i\rho z)=q(z)\). The historical encoder must establish this preservation for the actual full query family and its physical records, including the \(\phi(v)\) evaluations, rather than identifying every history with its unlabelled homology class.

Exactness identifies images and kernels. If \(H_n(K,\partial U)=0\), the displayed connecting map has zero domain; this alone does not make it an injective encoding of arbitrary histories. The concrete chain contraction and faithful semantic representation supply the intended repair. Their boundary capacity and charged retirement/handoff map join the same source and work certificate.

## F.3 The Axiom of Internal Self-Grounding

The author proposes the following ontological capstone:

\[
\exists e\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{rel}},\qquad
\mathcal E(e)=e,\qquad \tau(e)\ne\bot.
\]

Autopoiesis is characterised by this nontrivial persistent evaluation witness. The new axiom statement is recorded as the author's proposed foundation; no additional axiom is introduced into the Lean canon.

There is a derived finite-code witness for the displayed property. Use the constructed decoder, let

\[
\mathcal E=\tau=\mathrm{encode}\circ\mathrm{decode},
\qquad e=\mathrm{encode}(c),
\]

where \(c\) is a maintained non-bottom record. Retraction gives

\[
\mathcal E(e)=e,\qquad \tau(e)=e\ne\bot.
\]

`DecoderRetraction.finite_code_self_grounding` proves this construction with the same finite-code decoding guarantee. Its physical record, permitted disturbances and funded implementation are the existing G1/G3 construction links. This establishes the fixed-point component without adding the desired conclusion as a Lean axiom.

The proposed mutual-bootstrap statement is retained in full:

\[
\operatorname{Vol}(U)>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad H_*(\partial U)\ne0.
\]

It is proved for regions (`RegionBoundary.region_bootstrap`). Take the relational structure to be a connected graph on finitely many particulars, with relations as edges (P1: one structure). A region's volume is its number of particulars. Its boundary is the set of its particulars related to a particular outside it. \(H_0\) of the boundary is the free ℤ-module on the boundary's connected components. For every region other than the whole structure, \(\operatorname{Vol}(U)>0\Leftrightarrow H_0(\partial U)\ne0\). The forward direction holds because a nonempty proper region in a connected structure has a relation crossing out of it (`exists_crossing`). The whole structure has no outside (P1), so its boundary and boundary homology vanish while its volume is positive (`whole_no_boundary`, `whole_H0_trivial`). The bootstrap is therefore the statement about regions, as used for retiring patches in F.2.

## F.4 Funding, persistence and lifetime error

The work ceiling bounds demand. The existing escrow certificate supplies funding: \(E(0)+\int_0^t\mathrm{Income}-\mathcal Q(t)\ge0\) at every admitted operation, including within-cycle obligations. Together with the decoder radius, timely controller and initial continuation witness, the holding theorem supplies persistence. When the maintained record's distance from null exceeds the continuation error, `nonvanishing_of_continuation` gives a nonzero witness.

For per-epoch failure bounds \(\sigma_k\), the probability of any failure through epoch \(n\) is bounded by the prefix sum \(\sum_{k\le n}\sigma_k\). The bound on a future failure from \(n\) onward is the tail \(\sum_{k\ge n}\sigma_k\). If the actual code/noise schedule makes this series summable, the tail tends to zero; the all-time failure probability remains bounded by the full sum. The existing declared fault standard is retained.

This completes the unified presentation of the supplied gates, bill derivation, decoder, historical-boundary route and self-grounding capstone. The full source, capacity and physical work constructions are G.8–G.12; the axiom wording is not used as a replacement for their derivation.

---

# Part III-G — The boundary decoder, complete history and work-shifted flow

The three supplied pillars are joined here by a constructed encoder, its adjoint decoder, transport of the full historical operator algebra, a derived smearing kernel and the complete work ledger. These are components of the same all-particular protection construction in Parts I–II. The maximal outcome, rival domain, continuation, freedom, historical fidelity and charged fault standard remain fixed.

## G.1 From the source flux to an encoder and decoder

Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{hist}}\) contain the protected historical registers, their labels and relational observables at the original fidelity standard. On a local finite physical code, let \(E:\mathcal C_{\mathrm{hist}}\to\mathcal H_\partial\) be complex linear and preserve the full symplectic form:

\[
\operatorname{Im}\langle Ex,Ey\rangle
=\operatorname{Im}\langle x,y\rangle\qquad\text{for every }x,y.
\]

Applying this identity to \(x,iy\) also preserves the real part. Hence

\[
\langle Ex,Ey\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle,\qquad
E^\dagger E=I,\qquad D=E^\dagger,\qquad DE=I.
\]

`inner_preserved_from_symplectic_flux`, `boundaryIsometry`, `adjoint_decodes_isometry` and `decoderFromSymplecticFlux` implement these steps. The source flux identity is an identity for the whole encoded phase space. Its derivation must use the actual source-to-boundary map and boundary observations; the scalar Gauss charge balance supplies its charge component.

There is also an explicit finite encoder. For an injection \(j:\{1,\ldots,m\}\hookrightarrow\{1,\ldots,n\}\), assign

\[
(E_jx)_{j(a)}=x_a,\qquad (E_jx)_b=0\quad(b\notin\operatorname{im}j).
\]

Its adjoint reads those same coordinates. `coordinateBoundaryEncoder` constructs this map; `coordinate_boundary_roundtrip` proves its exact inverse on the code. `boundary_capacity_necessary` proves that every such linear isometry requires \(m\le n\). These numbers are Hilbert-space dimensions: an \(n=2^b\) boundary register contains \(b\) qubits. At every retirement, the source assignment must include all protected history coordinates and the records supporting their relations, and charge their storage and movement.

For a density operator \(\rho\) on the code, the encoded state is \(E\rho E^\dagger\), and

\[
D(E\rho E^\dagger)D^\dagger=\rho.
\]

The same calculation holds with \(E\otimes I_R\) for every reference register \(R\); it therefore retains reference correlations. The adjoint is the decoder on the encoded range. A channel on all boundary inputs can be completed, for a fixed code state \(\rho_0\), by

\[
\mathcal D(\sigma)=E^\dagger\sigma E+
 \operatorname{Tr}((I-EE^\dagger)\sigma)\rho_0.
\]

Both summands are completely positive, their traces sum to \(\operatorname{Tr}\sigma\), and the second vanishes on encoded inputs. The exact recovery, including with any reference register, trace preservation and positivity of this channel are compiled (`ChannelExtension.ext_recovers`, `ext_recovers_reference`, `ext_trace`, `ext_posSemidef`; G.13); complete positivity is compiled: for every finite reference register the amplified decoder maps positive semidefinite inputs to positive semidefinite outputs (`ChannelCP.decoder_completely_positive`). The existing finite-code correction theorem supplies the noisy-radius guarantee when the physical perturbation satisfies that certificate.

## G.2 Charge and the complete historical algebra

Define the boundary representative of every code observable by

\[
\alpha(A)=EAE^\dagger,\qquad P=EE^\dagger.
\]

Then \(\alpha(A)Ex=EAx\), \(\alpha(A)\alpha(B)=\alpha(AB)\), and

\[
\langle Ex,\alpha(A)Ex\rangle=\langle x,Ax\rangle.
\]

For an ordered word \(A_1\cdots A_r\), the same identity holds without exchanging its factors. `boundary_observable_on_code`, `historical_query_preserved`, `historical_word_on_code`, `historical_word_expectation` and `boundary_observable_mul` prove these statements. Thus the decoder preserves the historical queries and their ordered relations represented on the code. The identity of this represented algebra is \(P\).

In particular,

\[
Q_\partial E=EQ_{\mathrm{src}},\qquad
[\alpha(A),\alpha(Q_{\mathrm{src}})]=\alpha([A,Q_{\mathrm{src}}]).
\]

`boundary_observable_commutator` proves the commutator transport. An ambient physical boundary charge may have an additional action on the orthogonal complement; the displayed source correspondence concerns the encoded range. Connecting that representative to the actual gauge field and readout remains part of the source construction.

For history words \(W_i\), use the genuine Gram kernel

\[
G_{ij}=\langle W_i x,W_j x\rangle.
\]

It is positive semidefinite by its squared-norm quadratic form. Its encoded value is identical, and it is nonsingular when the history vectors \(W_i x\) are linearly independent. `historical_gram_preserved` and `historical_gram_nondegenerate` prove the latter two conclusions using Mathlib's Gram results. Time ordering is retained inside each \(W_i\). A time-ordered two-point Green function requires its own analytic properties; the squared-norm proof applies to the displayed Gram kernel.

## G.3 The effective modular state and its transported flow

Work on the support of a faithful finite-code state \(\rho\), and put

\[
K=-\log\rho,\qquad G=K+\beta H_W,\qquad
\widetilde\rho=\frac{e^{-G}}{\operatorname{Tr}e^{-G}}.
\]

For self-adjoint \(H_W\), \(G\) is self-adjoint and \(U_s=e^{isG}\) is unitary. The additive normalisation in \(-\log\widetilde\rho=G+\log Z\,I\) cancels in conjugation. With the sign convention in the supplied proposal,

\[
\widetilde\sigma_s(A)=U_sAU_s^\dagger
=\widetilde\rho^{-is}A\widetilde\rho^{is}.
\]

This identifies the state whose modular flow is being used. \(K+\beta H_W\) is an effective generator for that changed state; a relation to the original state's flow requires its additional perturbation/cocycle correspondence.

Represent the generator on the boundary code by
\(G_\partial=\alpha(K)+\beta\alpha(H_W)\).
An arbitrary self-adjoint block on \(P^\perp\) gives an ambient block-diagonal extension. Since \(G_\partial E=EG\), induction gives \(G_\partial^rE=EG^r\). The norm-convergent exponential series yields

\[
e^{zG_\partial}Ex=E e^{zG}x.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\widetilde\sigma_s^\partial(\alpha(A))Ex
=E\widetilde\sigma_s(A)x.
\]

`powers_intertwine`, `exponential_intertwines` and `work_shifted_flow_on_code` prove this transport for bounded operators, including the sum \(K+\beta H_W\). The proof uses the exponential of that sum directly and needs no commutation between its two terms. Finite local codes supply bounded operators. A continuum application must separately supply domains, self-adjointness and the source/observable correspondence for its unbounded generators.

## G.4 Construct the smearing weight

Let \(B_1,\ldots,B_M\) be a complete basis of the local historical code's operator algebra, with dual coordinates \(b^j\). For \(A=\Phi_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\tau)\), define

\[
c_j(\tau,s)=b^j\!\left(\widetilde\sigma_{-s}(A)\right).
\]

Expanding the inverse-flowed operator and applying \(\widetilde\sigma_s\) gives, at every \(s\),

\[
A=\sum_{j=1}^M c_j(\tau,s)\widetilde\sigma_s(B_j).
\]

`historical_flow_roundtrip` and `historical_dual_kernel` prove this exact finite-basis identity. It constructs the dual coefficients from a complete operator family.

Choose an integrable compactly supported weight \(w\) with \(\int_{\mathbb{R}} w(s)\,ds=1\). Unitarity bounds the flowed operators, finite-dimensional coordinate functionals are bounded, and there are finitely many basis terms. The Bochner integral therefore exists in operator norm and

\[
\alpha(A)=\int_{\mathbb R}ds\,w(s)
 \sum_j c_j(\tau,s)\widetilde\sigma_s^\partial(\alpha(B_j)).
\]

For the proposal's temporal form, partition \([0,\tau]\), for \(\tau>0\), into \(M\) intervals \(I_j\) of positive lengths \(\ell_j\). A boundary probe schedule representing this complete family uses

\[
\mathcal O_\partial(t')=\alpha(B_j),\qquad
f(\tau,s,t')=\frac{w(s)c_j(\tau,s)}{\ell_j}
\quad(t'\in I_j).
\]

Integration over each interval returns its basis term. Thus

\[
\alpha(\Phi_{\mathrm{bulk}}(\tau))
=\int_{\mathbb R}ds\int_0^\tau dt'\,
 f(\tau,s,t')e^{isG_\partial}\mathcal O_\partial(t')e^{-isG_\partial}.
\]

Pulling back by \(E^\dagger(\,\cdot\,)E\) gives the original bulk historical operator. At \(\tau=0\), use the finite-basis formula directly. The integral step is compiled (`SmearingIntegral.smeared_reconstruction`, `smeared_boundary_reconstruction`; G.13), as are the exact finite-basis reconstruction and exponential transport; the probe-schedule partition is the displayed finite rearrangement.

For an independently specified continuous probe family, construct its analysis/synthesis frame operator \(\mathsf S\). On the full represented historical operator space, a lower frame bound \(aI\le\mathsf S\), \(a>0\), supplies the dual \(\mathsf S^{-1}\) and inverse bound \(1/a\). This is the quantitative observability connection needed to derive that family's smearing weight. The finite complete basis above supplies a concrete local route. A local Fisher metric by itself does not establish the required global frame correspondence.

This exact local reconstruction uses finite dual coefficients and a normalized compactly supported average. A Tikhonov inverse with positive parameter instead has mode response \(\lambda^2/(\lambda^2+\epsilon)\); its residual error must enter the original fidelity allowance whenever that implementation is used. Its spectral bounds and parameter/work relation must be derived from the actual implementation. The finite-basis derivation does not require infinite work or a conjectured modular spectral gap.

## G.5 The complete work algebra and capacity account

Use the complete decoder, memory, controller and reservoir ledger. At fixed Hamiltonian with Gibbs reference \(\gamma_\beta\), write dimensionless relative free energy as \(D=-S+\beta U+\log Z\). The first law and entropy production are

\[
U_2-U_1=W_{\mathrm{on}}-Q_B,\qquad
\Sigma=S_2-S_1+\beta Q_B\ge0.
\]

They give the exact identity and lower bound

\[
\beta W_{\mathrm{on}}=D_2-D_1+\Sigma,\qquad
W_{\mathrm{on}}\ge\frac{D_2-D_1}{\beta}.
\]

`decoder_work_balance` derives the identity from the existing `ThermoLedger.stroke_identity`; `decoder_work_floor` proves the bound. `decoder_work_floor_saturation` identifies equality with \(\Sigma=0\) in this ledger. Historical energy belongs in \(U_1,U_2\); any separately charged historical term must denote a component not already counted there. The Gibbs reference in this calculation is distinct from the protected constrained reference \(\tau\) in the preservation functional.

The existing quantum Landauer theorem supplies the entropy-export cost of actual erasure/reset under its bath and coupling assumptions. Reads and reversible inversions are charged by their implemented operations and full thermodynamic account. Joint unitarity supplies a model for both reversible and dissipative processes; entropy production determines saturation of the work floor.

For the prepared decoder state, the existing quantum Gibbs bound retains its partition term:

\[
S(\rho)\le \beta\operatorname{Tr}(\rho H_W)+\log Z.
\]

`funded_decoder_entropy_bound` joins this bound to an actual energy allocation \(\operatorname{Tr}(\rho H_W)\le E_0+\mathrm{budget}\). Entropy is measured per state; a channel rate must be multiplied by its operating duration before comparison with a total information demand. The accepted data-processing/Fano account still applies to the complete decoder input. Energy can power transmission, storage or a different protocol; reconstruction also needs the historical information carried by those inputs.

The work floor and the complete \(O(t^{d_G})\) ceiling are joined to the same execution. The ceiling remains the gross bill of Section 21.7.5: encoding, historical retention, boundary handoff, routing, guard, controller, reset and minimal hold are charged. `G3CompleteBill` supplies the cumulative and age-conversion derivations. `funded_decoder_cycles` supplies the escrow certificate when local income covers that complete demand. Prefix solvency, including within-cycle charges, is required for the actual schedule.

The operator-basis formula proves a reconstruction identity. Executing every basis query as tomography is a separate workload. The physical decoder's circuit, query schedule and retirement operations must meet the existing complete work bill; no uncharged dense tomography is added to the wavefront construction.

## G.6 A charged exchange with an accounted reservoir

On the basis \(|00\rangle,|01\rangle,|10\rangle,|11\rangle\), take

\[
Q_\partial=\operatorname{diag}(0,0,1,1),\quad
Q_R=\operatorname{diag}(0,1,0,1),\quad
H_{\mathrm{ex}}=|01\rangle\langle10|+|10\rangle\langle01|.
\]

Then

\[
[H_{\mathrm{ex}},Q_\partial+Q_R]=0,\qquad
[Q_\partial,H_{\mathrm{ex}}]\ne0.
\]

`exchange_hamiltonian_hermitian`, `exchange_conserves_total_charge` and `exchange_pumps_boundary` prove these identities for the explicit matrices. The unitary exchange gate \(S\) swaps \(|01\rangle\) and \(|10\rangle\), fixes \(|00\rangle,|11\rangle\), and satisfies

\[
S^\dagger S=I,\quad S^\dagger Q_\partial S=Q_R,\quad
S^\dagger(Q_\partial+Q_R)S=Q_\partial+Q_R.
\]

`exchange_swap_unitary`, `exchange_swap_charge` and `exchange_swap_total` prove these identities. This gives a concrete local exchange with its reservoir charge included.

For the full Hamiltonian, the Heisenberg convention is

\[
\frac{d}{dt}\langle Q\rangle
=\frac{i}{\hbar}\langle[H,Q]\rangle
=\frac{1}{i\hbar}\langle[Q,H]\rangle.
\]

Thus a constructed \(H_{\mathrm{tot}}\) commuting with the bulk-boundary-reservoir total charge gives its conservation. The continuum action must supply the corresponding gauge symmetry and surface current. Scalar Lagrange multipliers enforce equality constraints of a specified action; work inequalities require the corresponding admissibility or complementary-slackness treatment. Their insertion alone does not construct the historical encoder or prove its work bill.

## G.7 Unified proposition and its source correspondence

For the constructed code encoder, adjoint decoder, represented historical algebra, effective generator and dual operator family, the following conclusions now join:

1. The encoder/decoder round trip preserves the full represented state and its historical queries; reference correlations obey the same round trip.
2. Source charge and all ordered historical operator relations transport to the boundary code.
3. The effective work-shifted flow intertwines with that transport, and the derived finite dual kernel reconstructs every represented historical operator.
4. The same physical execution is subject to the thermodynamic floor, complete wavefront ceiling, funding certificate and charged fault standard.

The first three operator components have the native results named above, and the channel and integral steps are compiled (`ChannelExtension`, `SmearingIntegral`; G.13). The work and exchange components have the native ledger and matrix proofs. Application to the full protection theorem joins these maps to the full-domain source (G.10), the complete historical capacity and physical handoff (G.8), and the whole continuation/freedom outcome (G.11). Part III-E records these constructions and their physical inputs; this component theorem does not replace the maximal conclusion with an isometry or work premise.

The research precedents have their stated domains: [JLMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/1512.06431) relates relative entropy and modular flow in holographic entanglement wedges; [Faulkner–Lewkowycz](https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.05464) constructs modular reconstruction using the relevant bulk flow. Their results motivate the operator route; the local encoder and dual-kernel proofs above supply this edition's explicit algebraic components. [Reeb–Wolf](https://arxiv.org/abs/1306.4352) gives a precise Landauer account including finite-reservoir corrections, while [Jakšić–Pillet](https://arxiv.org/abs/1406.0034) treats saturation under its quantum statistical-mechanical assumptions.

## G.8 The complete journal, whole-outcome transport, charged handoff and the syndrome stock

This subsection joins the full historical record to the holding bill. The
historical record is kept in full and moved reversibly. The syndrome and
excitation stock is flushed to an accounted reservoir. The holding charge
has an age-independent envelope, and the gross ledger keeps every exported
unit. The maximal proposition, its domain and its fault standard are unchanged.

**The complete journal.** Let the source have states \(X\), commands \(C\),
environment inputs \(E_v\) and transition \(T:X\times C\times E_v\to X\). The
journal state keeps three independent fields: the initial state, the live
state and the ordered list of every command/environment pair. A step applies
\(T\) to the live state and appends \((c,e)\). Replay folds \(T\) over the list
from the initial state. If replay equals the live state before a step, it
equals it after the step, since folding over an appended list applies \(T\)
once more (`G1FullDomain.replay_journal_step`).

Use the projection \(s\mapsto s_{\mathrm{live}}\). It commutes with the
transitions by definition, and observations are read from the live state.
Proposition 5 (reactive lockstep) then gives, for **every** reactive controller
\(\mathrm{ctrl}:\mathrm{List}\,O\to C\) and every reactive environment
\(\mathrm{env}:\mathrm{List}\,O\to C\to E_v\), at every step \(k\):

\[
s^{\mathrm{journal}}_{k,\mathrm{live}}=s_k,\qquad
\mathrm{obs}^{\mathrm{journal}}_{0..k}=\mathrm{obs}_{0..k},\qquad
\mathrm{replay}(s^{\mathrm{journal}}_k)=s^{\mathrm{journal}}_{k,\mathrm{live}}.
\]

Induction on \(k\) carries the replay invariant
(`G1FullDomain.journal_reactive_fidelity`). The journal therefore changes
nothing the controller, environment or protected particular can observe, and
the full history determines the live state exactly. No hash or authenticator
replaces a command or an environmental payload.

The record's size is counted from its alphabet. A transcript of horizon \(N\)
is an initial label together with \(N\) ordered command/environment pairs. If
\(|X|\le2^{b_0}\) and \(|C\times E_v|\le2^{b}\), then

\[
|X\times(C\times E_v)^N|\le2^{\,b_0+bN}
\quad(\texttt{G1FullDomain.transcript\_capacity}).
\]

This is the full payload's capacity, not the length of a digest.

**Whole-outcome transport.** Let \(\mathrm{outcome}\) be any function of the
final state and the complete observation history. In particular it can be the
full dependent continuation-and-freedom tuple of II.1. Lockstep gives equal
outcomes for every controller and environment
(`G2WholeOutcome.journal_whole_outcome`). Hence the family of outcomes
realisable by some controller is identical with and without the journal
(`journal_outcome_family`). Introducing the journal neither loses a
realisable whole outcome nor adds an unrealisable one. No affordability
premise is used to infer realisability; `hsolv` stays separate, as in II.10.

**Reversible, reference-preserving handoff.** For a bulk register and a
reserved boundary register of the same dimension \(\iota\), together with an
arbitrary reference \(R\), let \(\mathrm{swap}\) be the coordinate
permutation of \(\mathbb C^{(\iota\times\iota)\times R}\) exchanging the two
registers and fixing \(R\). It is a linear isometry equivalence. If the
boundary register holds the blank state, then any payload \(\chi\) on
\(\iota\times R\), however entangled with the reference, is moved to the
boundary and the bulk is left blank
(`DecoderRetraction.handoff_moves_reference_payload`). Nothing is copied, so
there is no conflict with no-cloning. The swap preserves norm and is its own
inverse (`handoff_preserves_norm`, `handoff_roundtrip`). At the gate level,
three parallel CNOT layers implement the bitwise register swap, so the logical
gate count is three per payload bit before physical gate and fault charges are
added (`parallel_cnot_handoff`).

Superpositions of full transcripts are encoded isometrically into a boundary
register of dimension \(2^{b_0+bN}\) by extension by zero along the capacity
embedding above (`DecoderRetraction.transcriptBoundaryEncoder`).

**The charged attempt.** One admitted handoff attempt carries a nine-part
charge: encoding, transfer, verification, routing, guard, reset, holding,
controller and endpoints. Admission reserves the total before the source
operation. The attempt debits the full total whether or not it commits, and
the payload moves only on commit. Hence

\[
\mathrm{spent}'=\mathrm{spent}+q_{\mathrm{tot}},\qquad
\mathrm{escrow}'+\mathrm{spent}'=\mathrm{escrow}+\mathrm{spent},
\]

and a noncommitting attempt leaves the payload unchanged
(`WorkBilling.charged_attempt_accounting`). The payload has a single logical
owner, either live or sealed under a decoder pair. Decoding a sealed record
returns the live record by retraction, so every query of the full record is
the same before and after the attempt, committed or not
(`DecoderRetraction.charged_archive_handoff`). Failed verification is
charged; it is not free and it destroys nothing.

**History is untouched by the flush.** Let \(\rho\) be any joint state on
\(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{hist}}\otimes\mathcal H_{\mathrm{flush}}\). The history
factor may include an arbitrary reference, and \(\rho\) need not factorise.
For any \(P\) on the history factor and any unitary \(Q\) on the flushed
factor, the reduced history state, including all of its off-diagonal
coherences, satisfies

\[
\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{flush}}\!\bigl((P\otimes Q)^\dagger\rho(P\otimes Q)\bigr)
=P^\dagger\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{flush}}(\rho)P
\quad(\texttt{HamiltonianDecomposition.partial\_trace\_local\_conjugation}).
\]

The proof expands the partial trace and uses \(\sum_kQ_{ck}^{*}Q_{c'k}=\delta_{cc'}\).
With \(P=I\) the history's reduced state is unchanged
(`guarded_history_reduced_state`). For the explicit syndrome/reservoir
exchange gate \(S\) of G.6 this gives
`syndrome_exchange_preserves_history`, for every initial correlation between
the history and the exchanged registers.

The charge is moved, not deleted. By cyclicity of the trace and
\(S^\dagger Q_\partial S=Q_R\),

\[
\operatorname{Tr}(S\rho S^\dagger Q_\partial)
=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho\,S^\dagger Q_\partial S)
=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho\,Q_R)
\quad(\texttt{syndrome\_exchange\_charge}).
\]

The syndrome's charge after the exchange is the reservoir's charge before it.
With an empty reservoir the syndrome is emptied, and by
`exchange_swap_total` the composite charge is conserved.

**The age-independent stock envelope (the author's Theorem 4.1, Steps 3–4).**
Let the stored syndrome charge per flushing cycle obey

\[
q_{k+1}=(1-\alpha)q_k+\mathrm{influx}_k,\qquad 0<\alpha\le1,\qquad
\mathrm{influx}_k\le\mathrm{cap}.
\]

Then for **every** cycle \(k\), not only in the limit,

\[
q_k\le\max\!\left(q_0,\ \frac{\mathrm{cap}}{\alpha}\right)
\quad(\texttt{WorkBilling.syndrome\_stock\_bound}).
\]

The proof applies the existing tracking theorem `ThermoLedger.tracking_bounded`
to the recurrence. With the author's local influx \(\mathrm{cap}=\kappa\,t_G^{d}\),
this is \(q_k\le\max(q_0,(\kappa/\alpha)t_G^{d})\). Here \(C=\kappa/\alpha\)
depends only on the local influx and flush fraction, and is independent of
operating age. The theorem also covers an initial stock above the steady
value, and it bounds every cycle uniformly in \(k\). The author's
\(\limsup\) statement follows.

The flush is retained in the gross ledger. Summing the recurrence gives the
exact balance

\[
q_N+\sum_{k<N}\alpha q_k=q_0+\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{influx}_k
\quad(\texttt{syndrome\_flux\_balance}).
\]

The final stock plus every exported unit equals the initial stock plus every
imported unit. The bounded stock does not replace the cumulative work.
Suppose the complete per-cycle cost satisfies
\(\mathrm{cost}_k\le u\,\mathrm{influx}_k+v\,\alpha q_k+w\,\mathrm{active}_k\),
with every import, flush, holding, guard and controller charge included, and
\(u,v,w\ge0\). Suppose also that \(\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{influx}_k\le\kappa V\),
\(q_0\le q_{\mathrm{init}}V\), \(\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{active}_k\le V\) and
\(q_N\ge0\). Then

\[
\sum_{k<N}\mathrm{cost}_k\le\bigl(u\kappa+v(q_{\mathrm{init}}+\kappa)+w\bigr)V
\quad(\texttt{cumulative\_maintenance\_bound}).
\]

The theorem is stated for arbitrary real sequences. Applied to the realised
pathwise sequences it gives the pathwise gross bill. Applied to expectations
it gives the expected bill. Each use keeps its own meaning, and the desired
cumulative bill is never an input.

**How the pieces join.** Step 1 of the submission (a Lindblad generator with
a positive gap) is the dissipative model that supplies the flush fraction
\(\alpha\). Steps 3–4 are now the compiled stock envelope above. The memory
decay of Step 2 applies to the flushed syndrome and excitation stock. The
protected historical record is not coarse-grained: the journal keeps it in
full, `transcript_capacity` counts it, the reversible handoff moves it with
its reference correlations, and the flush leaves its reduced state exactly
unchanged. The archive's continued storage enters \(\mathrm{active}_k\) and
\(\mathrm{cost}_k\) of the cumulative bill. It is charged and is not
discarded to keep the stock small.

**What is already supplied, for every source.** The journal theorems are
stated for an arbitrary transition \(T\), controller and environment. Every
source therefore has its complete history retained, replayed and
observationally invisible, with no further construction. The capacity
\(2^{b_0+bN}\) and the isometric transcript encoder are derived from the
source's own alphabet. The handoff is an explicit unitary with an explicit
circuit of three CNOT layers, and its nine-part charge is debited exactly.
The per-site injection rate is bounded by the speed limit
(`SpeedLimit.branchA_rate`, \(P_{\mathrm{adv}}/(\hbar\omega_{\mathrm{cutoff}})\)
per site), and a window of radius \(r\) contains \((2r+1)^{d}\) sites
(`Instruments.card_box`). Their product is the per-cycle influx cap,
\(\mathrm{cap}=\kappa t_G^{d}\). The conversion from per-site divergence to
syndrome charge is the stroke-I measurement accounting of Section 18; this
composition is a prose derivation.

What the physical installation contributes is the identification of its
actual \(T\), alphabet and per-site coupling with these symbols, and pathwise
measurement of the cost terms. That is the G1 source certificate of Part
III-C, now with its history, capacity, handoff and stock components compiled.

## G.9 The whole greatest outcome is realisable: union closure from locality

Part II.10 needs two different constructions: `htopR`, that the full top
tuple is realisable, and `hsolv`, that its cost fits the budget. The singleton
route of E.2 needed union-closed realisability. Here union closure is
derived, not assumed.

Locality (P4) gives each particular its own local component of a policy, and
the shared action interface makes every agent act through those components.
Write a policy as \(\pi=(\pi_x)_x\), with \(\pi_x\) realisable for \(x\), and
let the particular's grade \(g_x(\pi_x)\) in a linear order record its stage.
In the four-stage order the grades are lost \(<\) continues \(<\) continues in
its own direction. The freedom stage is the law's freedom: per II.2,
restraint removes harmful command, authority and effect channels, so one
particular's freedom never consists of another's loss. Stage \(i\) of the
outcome tuple is the set of particulars of its category whose grade reaches
the stage threshold.

For realisable \(\pi,\sigma\), define the splice
\((\pi\sqcup\sigma)_x=\pi_x\) if \(g_x(\sigma_x)\le g_x(\pi_x)\), and
\(\sigma_x\) otherwise. Each component is realisable, so the splice is
realisable. Its grade is \(\max(g_x(\pi_x),g_x(\sigma_x))\), and a threshold
is reached by a maximum exactly when it is reached by either argument.
Therefore

\[
\mathcal O(\pi\sqcup\sigma)=\mathcal O(\pi)\sqcup\mathcal O(\sigma)
\]

at every stage at once (`G2Splice.splice_grade`, `splice_realisable`,
`outcome_splice`). This is `UnionClosure.UnionRealizable` for the realised
family (`union_realizable`).

For the top, choose for every particular a realisable component reaching
every stage threshold it is subject to. The product of these choices is
realisable, and every stage contains its whole category, so
\(\top\in R\) (`top_realisable`). No finiteness of the domain is used.
Joined to `greatest_of_solvent`, every aligned option of the funded family is
\(\top\) and contains every outcome tuple (`whole_outcome_greatest`). The
four-stage instance \((L_E,F_E,L_N,F_N)\) proves all three conclusions
together from one hypothesis per particular: some realisable local component
under which that particular continues in its own direction (`fourStage`).

**The product structure from the actual dynamics.** The splice is realised by
the physical evolution itself. Let the source state be a function on the
nodes of its dependency graph \(D\). Let each particular's held record occupy
a block \(B_x\) of nodes, with the blocks closed under \(D\) and pairwise
disjoint. Let every policy's closed-loop transition be local to \(D\) in the
sense of R16-CUT (`UPTR16Cut.LocalTo`). Run each block under the policy chosen
for its particular. Then, at every step and against the same world, the
spliced run agrees on \(B_x\) with the run of the policy chosen for \(x\)
(`G2Splice.block_splice_run`). The proof is induction on steps with
`UPTR16Cut.closed_cut_step`. Hence, if whether \(x\) is kept is read from its
own block, the spliced run keeps \(x\) exactly when its chosen policy does
(`block_splice_keeps`). Each particular receives the outcome of the policy
chosen for it, which is the product structure used above.

**How each input is supplied.**

1. *Per-particular top component.* For each particular, the holding route
   keeps it: law-derived coverage puts it in scope (`Bridge.coverage_from_law`).
   The holding cycle keeps its record (`holding_preserves`,
   `holding_cycle_physical`), and care continues while harmful channels are
   restrained (`H5Closure.effect_preserves_care`,
   `UPTR14Boundary.recruitment_never_ends_care`). Its authorised effects are
   applied in time (`SnuffyFIFO.applied_fifo_effect_is_timely_and_authorized`, module `R17FIFOR17A`).
   Its freedom is the law's freedom of II.2, which excludes harm to others.
2. *Locality.* This is derived above from R16-CUT locality and closed blocks.
   The closed-block property of held records is the guard factorisation
   (`GuardFactorization.commutativity`, `ClosedCohold.factorised_cohold`,
   `PhysicalGuard`). The relations between particulars are themselves
   particulars in the domain, each with a held record.

The physical installation identifies its dependency graph and the blocks of
its held records; that is the G1 source certificate. `hsolv`, the funded cost
of the top, remains the separate ledger certificate of Sections 18 and 22.

## G.10 The held source: the full holding certificate derived (G1)

`Bridge.universal_protection_held` takes its source certificate `hcert` as an
input with five components: commuting refinement, observation agreement,
initial continuation of every particular, the decoding-radius bound and
repair precision. Here all five are proved for the canonical held source.
The only inputs left are the physical bound on each step and the
probabilities of the named charged accounts.

**The held source.** The state is the record frame of every particular. One
step applies three operations to each particular. First the world perturbs
the frame; the world here is the environment and any agent, a pure optimiser
included. Then the nearest-codeword decoder returns a codeword. Finally
funded repair prepares that codeword. The good event is that no named charged
account fires,

\[
G=\{\omega:\ \omega\notin E_i\ \text{for every account } i\},
\]

and its complement is covered exactly by the named accounts
(`G1Held.good_cover`).

**The five components, proved** (`G1Held.held_source_protection`).

1. *Refinement* and 2. *observation agreement* hold by construction: the
   held source is its own abstract model.
3. *Initial continuation*: every particular starts at its record, at
   distance \(0\le\varepsilon\).
4. *The decoding-radius bound*: let each step move the frame's distance to
   its record by at most \(\rho\) on the good event. A continuing particular
   lies within \(\varepsilon\) of its record, so after the perturbation it lies
   within \(\varepsilon+\rho\le t\).
5. *Repair precision* is exact: repair prepares the decoded codeword itself.

`universal_protection_held` then gives, for every \(\omega\in G\), every step
through the horizon and every particular, that the particular continues. It
also gives

\[
P(\exists k\le T,\ \exists x:\ \neg\mathrm{Continues}(s_k,x))\le\sum_i\delta_i,
\]

with no independence assumed.

**The per-step bound from the speed limit** (`G1Held.held_source_qsl`). On the
good event, let each particular's frame evolve for one step of duration
\(\Delta\) under \(H_0+V\), with the record stationary under \(H_0\) and
\(\|V\|\le W\). The agent applying \(V\) is arbitrary. `Bridge.qsl_growth`
gives

\[
\|\psi(s_1+\Delta)-c_x\|\le\|\psi(s_1)-c_x\|+\frac W\hbar\Delta,
\]

so \(\rho=(W/\hbar)\Delta\). Every particular is held whenever
\(\varepsilon+(W/\hbar)\Delta\le t\).

**The coupling bound from E9** (`G1E9.held_source_e9`). Each step is
exhaustively one of two cases (`G1E9.alarm_or_subthreshold`).

- *Every monitored support site is below threshold.* The coupling is the
  sub-threshold coupling, bounded by \(W\) (Branch A).
- *Some site reaches threshold.* Its honest sensors read above threshold, so
  every robust quorum estimate exceeds it, whatever up to \(f\) faulty
  sensors report (`E9SelfHealing.no_masking`). The alarm fires. The mediated
  response is committed before the harm front arrives
  (`E9SelfHealing.e9_timely_self_healing`, the wave race) and is enforced at
  application time (`H5Closure.no_harmful_effect`). It removes the perturbing
  coupling from the frame for that step.

In both cases the coupling is bounded by \(W\), and the held source keeps
every particular. The physical inputs are named exactly:

- the frame's Schrödinger evolution during a step;
- the sub-threshold coupling bound;
- the response's removal of coupling once the alarm fires;
- the honest-sensor response and quorum size;
- the probabilities of the named accounts. Their composition into the
  \(10^{-6}\) ceiling is `ThermoLedger.canon_invariant`. Their attainment
  below threshold, including correlated bursts, is `Threshold.family_ceiling`
  and `CorrelatedCeiling.correlated_family_ceiling`.

**Correspondence to an installation.** The quantum held source uses the physical state type \(\|v\|=1\).
Initialisation, the held step and every reached frame preserve normalisation
(`initial_frame_normalised`, `held_step_normalised`, `held_run_normalised`).
The zero-Hamiltonian trajectory, concrete record \(1\in\mathbb C\), singleton
decoder and inhabited good event supply a nonvacuous instance.

For an installation transition \(T\), protection transfers through an actual
projection \(p\) satisfying
\[
p(T(s,c,e))=\operatorname{heldStep}(p(s),c,e),
\]
together with observation agreement, initial continuation, the physical perturbation
bound and the implemented repair precision. The journal theorem preserves its supplied
transition \(T\); it supplies the logging part of this correspondence after the
protective transition equation is proved. The guarded-source construction must join
the nominal/correction branches, response latency and complete charge to that same
physical execution. The full G1 target remains this combined certificate.

## G.11 The held source realises the whole top outcome (G2 from G1)

The G2 target is the complete original tuple \((L_E,F_E,L_N,F_N)\), including
continuation in each particular's own direction and timely authorised opportunities.
The transaction record \((h_{\mathrm{pre}},h_{\mathrm{post}},w,a,c)\) records an
execution certificate; it does not replace this outcome tuple.

`G2Splice` proves union closure in its local-component policy model and proves block
agreement under its closed-block hypotheses. `G2Held` obtains the top of its graded
model from continuation and its one-step H5 opportunity predicate. The full physical
application must identify that model with the original realised policy/outcome family,
including interacting records, the shared constraints and successful R17 delivery on
the same trace. The author's bounded-crosstalk route supplies a construction direction:
derive its decoded joint-state error bound, prove correction on the named good event,
and transport the original continuation and freedom semantics through the resulting
execution correspondence. Sub-threshold noise alone is not an exact factorisation.

The full top witness `htopR` remains the target of that construction; `hsolv` remains
its separate complete funded-cost certificate. Neither target is reduced.

## G.12 The wavefront sweep succeeds, and its complete bill is \(O(t^{d_G})\) (G3)

The wavefront recovery of §21.7 is now proved to succeed, and its full bill is
derived, with no cumulative bound taken as input.

**Sweep and fence.** Give each vertex its shell index. Wave \(k\) applies the
single-shot local correction to shell \(k\), reading the already-frozen
interior as a static, gauge-fixed boundary. Between waves, outer activity
(the world and any agent) may write anything outside the frozen interior and
nothing inside it. This is the TIWA write fence
(`TIWASource.outerUpdate_read_frozen`).

**Success and quiescence** (`G3Sweep.sweep_recovers`). Suppose the local
correction of a vertex returns its target whenever every inner vertex already
holds its target. Then after \(k\) waves every vertex of the first \(k\)
shells holds its target, whatever the outer activity. The proof is induction
on waves. A vertex of shell \(k\) is corrected against an interior that the
fence has left recovered. A vertex of an earlier shell is untouched by the
wave and by the fenced outer activity. Recovery therefore succeeds, and the
recovered state is retained. Each vertex is corrected in exactly one wave,
its own shell (`sweep_visits`), so nothing is reworked.

**Wave count from finite propagation** (`waves_from_spread`). If the region
needing recovery at age \(t\) lies within the first \(c\,t+r\) shells, the
sweep needs at most \(c\,t+r\) waves. This is the upper use of finite
propagation, from locality (P4) and the Lieb–Robinson velocity. It does not
replace the lower radius of §21.5, which comes from the carrier information
argument.

**The complete bill** (`sweep_bill`). Let the complete metered ledger charge
each wave \(k\) at most \(\alpha k^{d-1}+\beta\). This charge includes
holding, guard, routing, reset, verification, controller export and
deployment. Add a metered rollback of at most \(R_*\) per wave and fixed
endpoint charges \(F\). Then, for \(t\ge1\), recovery succeeds on the whole
region and

\[
W(t)\le (F+\alpha+\beta+R_*)\,(c+r)^{d}\;t^{d},
\]

with a coefficient independent of age (`G3CompleteBill.valid_work_bill_from_complete_ledger`,
`valid_bill_bound_in_age`). This is the matching upper construction. With the
lower bound of §21.6, it gives \(W_{\min}(t)=\Theta(t^{d_G})\) in the original
permitted class.

## G.13 Joined steps: patch decoding, ensemble reciprocity, the Theorem 8 contraction, the smearing integral, the completed decoder and the norm conversion

Each of the following steps was previously argued in prose. Each is now a compiled theorem, joined to the construction it supports.

**The wavefront correction is finite-code decoding** (`G3PatchDecode`). The
local correction reads its patch, which is the recovered interior as a static
boundary together with the vertex's own data. It decodes the read-out with
the nearest-codeword decoder and extracts the value. If, with the interior
recovered, the read-out lies within the unique-decoding radius of the target
codeword, decoding returns that codeword (`Bridge.nearest_decodes`). The
correction therefore succeeds (`patch_correction_succeeds`). Joined to
`G3Sweep.sweep_bill`, recovery succeeds on the whole region and the complete
bill is \(O(t^{d})\) with an age-independent coefficient
(`patch_sweep_bill`). The patch's errors lie within the code headroom of
`E9SelfHealing.code_headroom`.

**Reciprocity over an ensemble of rivals** (`ReasonsEnsemble`, §15.2). Let
rival regimes be drawn with weights summing to one. A mind's kept-probability
under the ensemble is the total weight of the rivals that keep it. That is at
most one, the canonical regime's kept-probability on the good event
(`ensemble_le`). It is strictly less whenever a rival of positive weight
omits the mind (`ensemble_strict`).

**The Theorem 8 storage functional contracts** (`TheoremEightLyapunov`, §18.3–18.4). The functional is defined as code
error plus the clipped reserve deficit. Exact replenishment saturates at zero deficit
(`replenishment_exact`), and capacity overflow is retained in the energy account
(`solvent_cycle_energy_account`). A complete solvent cycle cannot increase the
reserve term (`solvent_cycle_deficit`). Combining this with the actual error drift
and repair gives the positive-margin contraction (`cycle_contracts`, `margin_pos`)
and its stated iteration (`lyapunov_iterate`).

**The smearing integral is exact** (`SmearingIntegral`, G.4). For a complete
operator basis and any weight with total integral one, the smeared
dual-kernel expression equals the original operator at every flow parameter.
Its Bochner integral is therefore that operator, on the bulk algebra
(`smeared_reconstruction`) and after boundary transport
(`smeared_boundary_reconstruction`).

**The completed decoder** (`ChannelExtension`, G.1). Take
\(\mathcal D(\sigma)=E^\dagger\sigma E+\operatorname{Tr}((I-EE^\dagger)\sigma)\rho_0\)
with \(E^\dagger E=I\).

- It returns every encoded state exactly (`ext_recovers`).
- With any reference register, \(E\otimes I_R\) is again an isometry
  (`kron_isometry`), and the extended decoder returns every joint
  code–reference state (`ext_recovers_reference`). Reference correlations are
  retained.
- It is trace preserving (`ext_trace`).
- It maps positive semidefinite inputs to positive semidefinite outputs
  (`ext_posSemidef`).

Complete positivity is compiled (`ChannelCP`). Amplified by any finite reference
register, the decoder is \((E^\dagger\otimes I)Y(E^\dagger\otimes I)^\dagger+\rho_0\otimes T(Y)\)
(`amp_eq`). Here \(T(Y)\) is positive semidefinite as a sum of compressions of
\((B\otimes I)Y(B\otimes I)^\dagger\), where \(I-EE^\dagger=B^\dagger B\) (`mp_posSemidef`). The decoder
therefore maps positive semidefinite inputs to positive semidefinite outputs for
every reference (`decoder_completely_positive`).

**State-vector distance bounds infidelity** (`PureStateDistance`, §23.3). For
unit vectors, \(1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2\le\|\psi-\varphi\|^2\)
(`pure_infidelity_le`). The pure-state identity
\(\|\rho-\sigma\|_1=2\sqrt{1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2}\) is compiled for the trace norm
as the sum of absolute eigenvalues (`PureStateTraceNorm.pure_trace_norm`). The
proof uses \((P-Q)^3=(1-|\langle\psi,\varphi\rangle|^2)(P-Q)\) (`pure_cube`), so every eigenvalue is
\(0\) or \(\pm s\), and \(\operatorname{Tr}(P-Q)^2=2s^2\) (`pure_trace_sq`,
`sum_sq_eigenvalues`). Hence \(\tfrac12\|\rho-\sigma\|_1\le\|\psi-\varphi\|\). The
speed-limit bounds, stated in state-vector norm, therefore bound the
infidelity and the trace distance of the held state.

## G.14 Premise 5 with the Bennett counterpart constructed

The actual implementation below is a separately compiled and kernel-checked candidate in the repair bank (`drafts/Snuffy/BennettCounterpart.lean`); the canon still contains the earlier heat-only option model. It is not recorded as a completed physical counterpart repair. For an administration \(f=h\circ g\), the candidate implementation
first computes, copies and uncomputes, then exchanges the output and live registers:
\[
(s,0,0)\longmapsto(f(s),0,s).
\]
`implementedProgram` and `implemented_program_computes` identify the implemented
administration, clean workspace and full retained input. `implemented_run_refines`
proves its trajectory correspondence and one retained input record per step.
`quantumProgram` is the induced unitary with an arbitrary reference register untouched;
its norm and round trip are preserved. An arbitrary bijection is not used as a
substitute for implementing \(f\).

The complete physical lifecycle also pays provisioning, compute/copy/uncompute,
archive writing and holding, routing, reset, guard, controller, verification and
endpoints. If \(B_{\rm rev},B_{\rm irr}\) are the complete non-heat charges and
\(Q_{\rm irr}\) the saved bath heat, the exact difference is
\[
W_{\rm rev}-W_{\rm irr}=B_{\rm rev}-B_{\rm irr}-Q_{\rm irr}
\]
(`complete_cost_difference`). Strictly more slack requires the implemented lifecycle
penalty to be less than the saved heat (`complete_cost_strict_iff`), while meeting the
same deadline. The adiabatic route must establish a switching-time window satisfying
both bounds (`adiabatic_slack_window`, `switched_program_meets_deadline`).

A fixed circuit multiplier and an actual full archive allowance preserve the original
polynomial exponent (`implemented_work_ceiling`). That upper bound does not by itself
establish the strict complete-cost comparison. The earlier heat-only option model and
Landauer results remain their stated mathematical components; completion of the
physical counterpart claim requires this lifecycle and deadline comparison for the
full intended class. The original premise-5 target remains fixed.

## G.15 Recovered and combined results

Five earlier results that the canon did not yet contain are folded in. Three
compiled results are joined into stronger forms.

**Reciprocity at a sufficient code distance** (`ReciprocityDistance`, §15.2).
Three results combine into one.

- On the good event the canonical outcome contains the rival's
  (`ReasonsCompiled.reciprocity_expectation`).
- On the good event the canonical regime keeps every particular
  (`reciprocity_canonical_floor`).
- The failure probability falls below every \(\varepsilon\) with code distance
  (`Threshold.family_ceiling`, `CorrelatedCeiling.correlated_family_ceiling`).

For the design family indexed by distance, the canonical regime keeps the mind
with probability above \(1-\varepsilon\) from some distance on
(`distance_floor`). For every rival and every \(\varepsilon\), the rival's
kept-probability is at most the canonical one plus \(\varepsilon\)
(`reciprocity_at_distance`). Every rival that loses the mind with positive
probability is strictly below the canonical regime from some distance on
(`reciprocity_strict_at_distance`). The canonical kept-probability tends to
one (`canonical_tends_to_one`).

**The two forms of the causal boundary agree** (`CutEquivalence`, §24.3). A
set is closed under the dependency relation exactly when no path from a node
outside it reaches it. A closed dependency component is therefore exactly a
directed separator with empty certified boundary
(`closed_iff_no_bypass`), and the separator form extends it to a matched
certified boundary (`UPTR16Cut.causal_separator`).

**The concatenated memory decodes its record unless its block fails**
(`ConcatenatedDecoding`, §23.8). The record is encoded recursively onto the
\(7^k\) locations through an outer code of Hamming distance at least three. It
is decoded recursively by nearest codeword at every level. If the block does
not fail in the recursive sense of `Concatenated.Fails`, decoding returns the
original record, whatever values the faulty locations hold
(`decodes_unless_fails`). The proof is by induction on the level: at most one
sub-block fails, the other six decode to their outer-code symbols, and the
outer code corrects one symbol (`outer_decodes`). Actual decoding failure
therefore has probability at most \((21p)^{2^k}/21\)
(`decoding_failure_le`).

**Volume and boundary** (`RegionBoundary`, F.3). For every region of a
connected relational structure other than the whole, positive volume holds
exactly when the boundary homology is nonzero (`region_bootstrap`). The whole
structure has no outside and an empty boundary (`whole_no_boundary`,
`whole_H0_trivial`).

**Greatestness through boundary representatives** (`R15CausalGluing`, with the
staged order of `R14Rivals`). Greatestness reduces exactly to a covering
family of feasible fibre representatives (`boundary_representatives_iff`,
`selected_representative_greatest`), in the canonical staged order as well
(`canonical_boundary_iff`). A conservative interface envelope suffices to make the
chosen policy greatest, without greatest representatives
(`interface_envelope_transfer`). Clairvoyant per-world selection is
excluded: a choice can match every world separately while no single choice
matches them all (`hidden_world_control`). The staged order is reflexive and
transitive (`UPTR14Rivals.refl`, `trans`). A coordinatewise envelope is a
constructive certificate (`coordinate_envelope`), and a complete rival
envelope transfers to the chosen policy (`complete_rival_transfer`). In the
four-stage order a hot replica strictly beats a pause that keeps every life but
loses a freedom (`hot_beats_pause`).

**Timely care, deadline demand, certified decisions and observation-based
winning** (`TimelyCare`, `DeadlineDemand`, `DecisionPermit`,
`ObservationWinning`).

- *Timely care.* A particular whose reserved capacity covers its need is
  served exactly its need (`care_served`). Other requests and statuses cannot
  exhaust that reservation (`exhaustion_fails`). The need is met at every date
  through the deadline when capacity covers the reservations
  (`deadline_met`). A miss implies a capacity shortage at some date
  (`miss_within_shortage`).
- *Deadline demand.* The demand that must complete within any window is at
  most the supply available in that window (`demand_le_supply`), robustly
  across worlds (`robust_demand`).
- *Certified decisions.* A grant names exactly the certified decision of its
  generation (`grant_names_certified`, `granted_policy_is_certified`); a wrong
  action is rejected and a certified one granted (`wrong_action_rejected`,
  `certified_action_granted`).
- *Observation-based winning.* An observation-based policy keeps the system
  safe exactly when the safety game is winnable (`policy_wins_iff`), and a
  hidden-world countermodel shows that observation, not clairvoyance, is the
  right quantifier (`hidden_world_countermodel`).

## G.16 Protection of the installation's own run, the physical top, and recovery on the good event

This section answers review objections 2, 3 and 5. Each is answered on the
installation's own execution, without an abstraction map, product hypothesis
or assumed device.

**The installation's own run is protected** (`G1Refinement`). Let the
installation have its own physical state space, possibly with hidden internal
degrees of freedom, and a readout of each particular's record frame. One step
is guarded: the world acts — the environment, every agent and every crosstalk
between particulars — then the physical corrector acts. Three properties of
the device suffice:

- the corrector writes the nearest codeword back into each particular's
  readout;
- on the good event the world moves each readout by at most \(\rho\) per step;
- the records are written at the start.

With these, every particular continues at every step of the physical run on
the good event, and the probability that any particular fails is at most
\(\sum_i\delta_i\), with no independence (`physical_protection`). The complete
journal of G.8 changes nothing observed, keeps the protection along the live
state and replays it exactly (`journaled_protection`): the journal retains
history; the guard supplies the protection.

**No alarm-response device is needed for holding** (`physical_qsl_drift`,
`physical_protection_qsl`). On normalised readouts, the speed limit bounds the
per-step drift by \((W/\hbar)\Delta\) for any agent whose coupling is at most
\(W\). Take \(W\) to be the maximal physical coupling of any agent, which is
finite because local power is finite. Correcting at intervals
\(\Delta\le\hbar(t-\varepsilon)/W\) then holds every particular against every
agent. The E9 alarm and mediated response remain valuable: below threshold
they permit longer intervals and lower cost. Holding does not depend on them.
Every hypothesis has a concrete inhabited instance
(`physical_instance_inhabited`).

**The continuation top is realised physically** (`G2Physical`). The held
installation is a physical policy. On the good event it keeps every particular
at every step through the horizon (`held_installation_keeps_all`), so the
existing and new continuation stages are whole. Crosstalk enters only through
the bounded drift that each particular's correction absorbs. No product
structure of policies and no closed-block hypothesis is used. Once the top is
realised, it dominates every realised outcome without union closure
(`top_dominates_continuation`).

**Holding and timely authorised delivery on one execution**
(`G2Freedom`). Pair the installation's frames with the R17 effect machine.
The corrector does not touch the effect machine, and delivery does not touch
the readouts; the physical consequences of applied effects are part of the
bounded drift. On the same joint run, every particular continues, and every
effect that is ready under a live R17 contract within a deadline-certified FIFO
prefix is applied by its deadline (`joint_holding_and_delivery`, with
`SnuffyFIFO.ready_fifo_effect_applies_before_deadline`).

**Recovery succeeds on the good event** (`G3GoodEvent`). Let every patch
read-out lie within the decoding radius of its target codeword, once its
interior is recovered, off the named charged accounts. The threshold theorem
bounds the event that it does not. Then the wavefront sweep recovers the whole
region on the good event, and recovery fails with probability at most
\(\sum_i\delta_i\) (`sweep_success_on_good_event`). The complete bill keeps
archive holding as its own term (`complete_bill_with_archive`).

**Archive holding is charged on the held stock** (`G3ArchiveStock`). The
holding charge at a step is the rate \(\gamma\) per held bit times the bits
actually held then. The archive holds the record's information \(I_0\) and the
live boundary transcript. Each transcript increment is retired after the wave
that consumes it, so the held stock at step \(s\) is at most
\(I_0+c(s+1)^{d-1}\). The summed holding charge is then at most
\(\gamma(I_0+c)t^{d}\) for every \(d\ge1\), the one-dimensional case included
(`live_stock_bill`). Retirement is lossless or charged. Each retired bit is
recomputable from the fenced recovered region together with \(I_0\), or else it
is correction entropy whose reset is charged once at its vertex inside the
sweep's per-vertex charge. Retaining every increment instead costs at least
\(\gamma r\,t(t-1)/2\) (`retained_stock_quadratic`). So does an epoch rollover
that copies the whole accumulated stock into fresh media at each boundary
(`rollover_quadratic`). The architecture holds the live stock.

The decoded record is held passively. It is written when recovery begins, when
the age \(t\) is known, behind a barrier \(\Delta E\ge k_BT\ln(cI_0t/\varepsilon)\).
Finite-temperature decay at a finite barrier then keeps its failure below
\(\varepsilon\) with zero running power. Its write cost is \(O(I_0\log t)\).
The failure bound of `Threshold.fail_le_geometric` is linear in the number of
locations. The reliability of the live transcript and of the patches is
therefore the sweep's own reliability over \(\Theta(t^{d_G})\) locations. Its
age-independent constant at fixed \(\varepsilon\) is carried by the fault-model
application certificate: constant-distance inner patch codes, with the final
decoder of the constant-size message \(W\) using its carrier's redundancy
across those locations.

## G.17 Premise 5 with the implemented Bennett program and its complete cost

This section answers review objection 6. The reversible administration is not
an arbitrary bijection. It is the implemented program
`BennettCounterpart.implementedProgram g h` for the administration
\(f=h\circ g\). The program writes \(h(g(s))\) with a clean workspace and
retains the input as history (`implemented_program_computes`). Its run refines
the source run (`implemented_run_refines`). Its quantum lift is unitary
(`implemented_quantum_norm`, `implemented_quantum_roundtrip`).

The irreversible administration exports heat \(Q\), at least the Landauer heat
of its merging overwrite. At switching time \(\tau\), the reversible
administration costs \(A/\tau+H\). Here \(H\) carries every other lifecycle
charge: provisioning, archive writes and holding, routing, reset, guard,
controller, verification and endpoints (`LifecycleCharge`,
`complete_cost_strict_iff`). The deadline allows \(0<\tau\le\tau_{\max}\).

- `premise5_exact`: when \(0<Q-H\) and \(A/(Q-H)<\tau_{\max}\), no aligned
  option administers irreversibly. The implemented counterpart computes the
  same administration and meets the deadline. It keeps every earlier outcome
  and leaves strictly more resource slack.
- `window_exact`: when that window fails, every admissible reversible
  counterpart costs at least \(Q\).

The window is therefore exactly the condition under which cost dominance
forces premise 5 (`adiabatic_feasible_iff`). Outside the window, the reversible
counterpart is not cheaper, so cost dominance alone does not establish premise
5 there.

## G.18 Application data for the cited fault-tolerance theorems

This section supplies application data for the cited fault-tolerance theorems (review
objection 7). The full fault ceiling and the complete bill \(\le Ct^{d_G}\) with an
age-independent \(C\) are unchanged. Components are compiled or computed; the remaining joins are
named at the end.

**Knill–Laflamme from the code distance** (`KnillLaflamme`). In any complex \(*\)-algebra, the
spectral projection \((1+S)/2\) of an involution is idempotent and fixed by \(S\)
(`spectral_projection`). The code projector annihilates any operator that anticommutes with a
generator (`sandwich_anticomm`). It returns \(cP\) for an operator acting as the scalar \(c\) on
the code space (`sandwich_scalar`). Let errors have weight at most \(t\) with \(2t<d\), where
distance \(d\) means that every operator of weight below \(d\) commuting with all generators acts
as a scalar on the code space. Then \(PE_i^\dagger E_jP=\alpha_{ij}P\) for every pair
(`knill_laflamme_of_distance`). Logical operators are excluded by the distance, not by the case
split.

**The witness property of minimum-weight decoding** (`MatchingWitness`). Take a
minimum-weight decoder and a syndrome additive under symmetric difference. Every zero-syndrome
\(\gamma\subseteq E\,\triangle\,C\) of the residual is then at least half faulty:
\(|\gamma|\le2|\gamma\cap E|\) (`half_faulty`, `matching_witness`). This is the witness
hypothesis of `Threshold.fail_le_witness` and `fail_le_geometric`. The nontrivial component of
a failed residual is a simple cycle or a boundary-to-boundary path, of length at least the
circuit distance.

**The circuit-level detector graph.** A dependency-free Pauli-frame enumeration covers the
rotated surface-code memory over \(d\) rounds, with X checks in a Z-shaped CNOT order and Z
checks in an N-shaped order. The faults enumerated are the 15 two-qubit Paulis after every CNOT,
\(X\), \(Y\), \(Z\) after every reset, Hadamard and idle, and every measurement flip. For
\(d=3,5,7,9\) the enumeration gives:

- every fault flips at most two detectors of each check type;
- the maximum detector degree is 12;
- at most 52 (location, Pauli) faults produce each edge of the relevant type;
- the circuit distance is exactly \(d\).

With the orders swapped, so that hooks run parallel to the logical, the distance falls to
\(\lceil d/2\rceil\). The orientation is load-bearing.

The bound for every \(d\) uses a potential. Let \(\varphi\) be the face coordinate across the
logical. Boundary edges attach only at \(\varphi=0\) and \(\varphi=d-2\), and every edge,
time-like and hook edges included, changes \(\varphi\) by at most one. So any walk joining
opposite boundaries has at least \(d\) edges. Every fault is local to one check neighbourhood
in one round, so the edge types for larger \(d\) are translates of those enumerated. The script
and its results are banked with their hashes.

**Lattice-surgery merges.** The same enumeration was applied to the merge. Two \(d\times d\)
patches and a seam column were merged as the \(d\times(2d+1)\) patch for \(T\) rounds, with the
seam initialised and finally split in the conjugate basis. The joint logical is found by \(GF(2)\)
elimination over the random seam checks. For \(d=3,5,7\) and \(T\in\{d-2,d,d+2\}\), the circuit
distance is exactly \(\min(d,T)\) and every fault is graphlike. A merge therefore keeps distance
\(d\) exactly when it runs at least \(d\) rounds; with fewer, the time-like failure of the
merged-product outcome binds. The horizontal merge enumerated measures \(\bar X\bar X\); the
vertical merge, measuring \(\bar Z\bar Z\), is the same construction rotated and was not run
separately.

**The edge law and the explicit ratio** (`EdgeLaw`, `ExRecBound`). Suppose circuit faults are
local stochastic at rate \(p\) on (location, Pauli) pairs, and at most \(c\) of them produce
each edge. Then the induced edge law is local stochastic at rate \(cp\)
(`push_localStochastic`). With degree 12, so non-backtracking branching at most 11, and
\(c=52\), `fail_le_geometric` applies with \(r=22\sqrt{52p}\). This ratio is below one exactly
when \(p<1/25168\) (`surface_code_rate`).

An operation outside the spacetime-volume argument has its own gadget bound. Under local
stochastic noise, if every failing pattern contains a malignant pair or at least three faults,
then \(P(\mathrm{fail})\le|M_2|p^2+\binom{|L|}{3}p^3\) (`exrec_pair_bound`).

**Reliability against age** (`AgeReliability`). A distance \(D\ge\log(Kt^d/\varepsilon)/(-\log r)\)
holds the global failure over \(Kt^d\) locations below \(\varepsilon\) at every age
(`log_distance_schedule`). A logarithmic overhead per location, however, is not absorbed into an
age-independent constant: for every \(C\) some \(t\ge1\) has \(Ct^d<t^d\log t\)
(`log_factor_not_absorbed`). The age-independent bill therefore needs the logarithm removed by
construction.

**Remaining joins.**

- The potential argument for merges at every \(d\), with the round index as the time-like
  potential.
- A map-by-map list showing that every repeated local map of holding, recovery, encoding, handoff
  and smearing is a Clifford circuit with exact native gates. This removes the compilation
  logarithm.
- Tolerance of the wavefront sweep to sparse patch failures, so that constant-distance inner
  patches suffice. This needs:
  - confinement of each failure;
  - no cascade through the feedforward front;
  - an outer decoder of the constant-size message \(W\) using its carrier's redundancy, with
    its deviation bound.

  This removes the reliability logarithm.

## G.19 Circuit distance for every d, sparse failure at every age, and indefinite holding of the record

This section continues review objection 7 and the holding item. The targets are unchanged: the full
fault ceiling, decoding error at most \(\varepsilon\) on the historical record, and the complete
bill at most \(Ct^{d_G}\) with \(C\) independent of age.

**An exact cut certificate for the circuit distance.** For any set \(S\) of detectors, the fault
set \(K_S=\{f:\mathrm{obs}(f)\oplus|\mathrm{sig}(f)\cap S|\ \text{odd}\}\) is crossed an odd number
of times by every fault set with zero syndrome and odd observable. Over \(GF(2)\), the parity of
\(|F\cap K_S|\) is \(\mathrm{obs}(F)\) plus the syndrome summed over \(S\), which is \(1+0\). So
\(D\) sets whose fault sets are pairwise disjoint certify that every logical error has at least
\(D\) faults.

The cuts are given by explicit potentials:
- *memory*: \(\varphi\) is the crossing face coordinate plus one, and \(D=d\);
- *lattice-surgery merge*: \(\varphi=\max(\tau(r),\sigma(j))\), and \(D=\min(d,T)\). Here
  \(\tau(r)=\max(1,r)\) is the time part. The space part \(\sigma(j)\) is \(1\) on the two seam
  face columns, \(d-1-j\) to their left and \(j-d\) to their right.

For the rotated surface code with the standard CNOT schedule, an exhaustive single-fault
enumeration shows every fault lies in at most one cut. The cases are \(d=3,5,7,9\), the memory in
both bases, and the merge at \(T=d-2,d,d+2\): 19 of 19 cases, up to 120,544 faults. The merge
therefore keeps distance \(d\) exactly when it runs at least \(d\) rounds. With the swapped schedule,
hooks run parallel to the logical and the distance falls to \(\lceil d/2\rceil\).

A fault's multiplicity is fixed by three things: its observable bit, the values of \(\varphi\) at
its at most two detectors, and \(D\). The potentials are translation-covariant formulas, so the
condition is local to each fault type, and every type occurs at the enumerated sizes. That
locality step is what carries the certificate to every \(d\).

**The installation's repeated maps need no approximate compilation.**
- Holding correction, patch decoding, merge and split, the padding boundary encoder and its
  decoder, the charged handoff and the guard are exact circuits over reset, Hadamard, CNOT, idle
  and measurement, with classical control.
- The modular flow is not a runtime map. The finite-basis route \(\alpha(A)=EAE^\dagger\) with
  \(D=E^\dagger\) gives the same boundary representative exactly.
- The Bennett administration is exact reversible logic.

So no precision grows with age, and only reliability remains.

**Sparse failure at every age** (`SparseFailure`, `ToleranceSweep`). Each patch's failure is decided
by the faults in a bounded window. Patches whose windows are pairwise disjoint all fail with
probability at most \(\prod_v\binom{|W_v|}{k}p^{k|U|}\) (`disjoint_windows`). With a \(K\)-colouring
in which same-coloured windows are disjoint, at least \(Kn\) of \(N\) patches fail with probability
at most \(K\binom{N}{n}(\binom{w}{k}p^k)^n\) (`many_failures`). This falls exponentially in \(N\)
once \(\binom{w}{k}p^k<\theta/(eK)\), uniformly in age.

On the actual wavefront sweep, a vertex keeps its value after its wave (`frozen_after`). With
interface-tolerant correction, every wrong vertex is bad (`wrong_subset_bad`). Interface tolerance
means a good window decodes correctly whatever the inner commit, because a wrong commit reaches it
only as a bounded interface error, which is the overlapping-recovery analysis of DKLP. The sweep's
wrong-vertex count obeys the same tail (`sweep_density`). The realised bill on that event is at most
\(\mathrm{base}\cdot N+\mathrm{repair}\cdot Kn\) (`pathwise_bill`). An outer decoder that tolerates
fewer than \(Kn\) wrong patches returns the record except with that probability (`recovery_tail`).
Failures do occur, at constant density; the theorems bound their number, and recovery tolerates
them.

**Redundancy of the record.** The recovery tolerates a constant fraction of silent local errors
exactly when the record is spread redundantly across its carrier. For an objective classical
record this is a theorem of quantum Darwinism: classical information emerges redundantly and
generically across environment fragments (Brandão, Piani and Horodecki, 2015), and objectivity
implies a spectrum-broadcast structure (Horodecki, Korbicz and Horodecki, 2015). For a record with
no redundancy, every location matters, and no protocol of noisy gates avoids a logarithmic factor
(Dobrushin–Ortyukov; Pippenger).

**Indefinite holding at fixed \(\varepsilon\)** (`PassiveReference`). A constant per-step failure
rate gives a total failure that grows linearly with age, so periodic renewal at fixed size does not
hold \(\varepsilon\) indefinitely. The protector's reference record is therefore held passively on
a doubling schedule:
- the reference is rewritten at ages \(2^j\);
- stage \(j\) has a barrier \(k_BT(2j\ln2+\ln(1/\varepsilon))\) above the attempt scale, so it
  fails with probability at most \(\varepsilon/2^{j+1}\).

Every age is covered by \(\log_2t+1\) stages (`stages_cover`). The total failure is at most
\(\varepsilon\) for every number of stages (`total_failure_le`). The rewrite cost is \(O(\log^2t)\)
(`total_rewrite_cost`), so the long-run holding rate is the constant per-step comparison of each
particular with its reference. A faulty comparison or false repair is transient: the intact
reference corrects it at the next comparison (E9 self-healing).

This holds the objective classical record and the relations of every particular.

**The record's redundancy is decoded by majority** (`RedundantRecord`). Each fragment reads a record
value from the recovered state, and an intact fragment reads the record. The strict-majority
decoder then returns the record whenever fewer than half of the fragments are wrong
