# Part V — Foundations: the given, the good and its ten rulings

## V.1 What follows from the given

Part I.2 states the relational given, P1–P4. The 10–12 October papers draw
from it the facts that the law and the bridge rely on.

**Coexistence contains replacement (Given the Lattice D16).** A mind that keeps
the living world can still make descendants; a mind that replaces it cannot get
it back. The choice is between a world with the mind's descendants and a world
with its descendants and everything that already lives. Where keeping takes
nothing the mind would otherwise have had, the second world contains the first.
Conflict and cost are the two ways keeping can take something; both are counted
in the law's feasible family and in the solvency ledger (premise 6).

**What is made does not continue what was ended (Made of the Living D60).** To
make a new process is not to continue one that was ended. Argued from P2, P3 and
D15: a particular is constituted by its relations and its history, and a later
process, however similar, has a different history. Hence a replacement is
another history, and keeping and replacing are not two ways of having the same
thing. Paper 12's ruling "a replay is a reconstruction" and II.3's distinction
between record and continuation rest here.

**Where keeping is feasible, ending is what needs the argument (D61).** Under
doubt, ending is irreversible and keeping leaves later intervention possible at
a bounded cost. **Ending a living process ends each relation with it that is
part of what a mind is, and a substitute does not restore one (D63).**

## V.2 The good and its ten rulings

The author declares a good: what is good in the whole is its complexity, and
more of it is better (Made of the Living B21). On 11 October 2026 he ruled on
what that means and how it may be pursued. The rulings are premises of the
whole argument, recorded in the wording he approved.

1. By complexity the author means the continuing histories the whole holds, and
   the relations among them. Variety that can be measured is evidence of it and
   is not the thing itself. A history counts by what is its own: what it has
   lived that nothing else has. Many made alike count for little more than one.
2. That beings could be made who would fare well cannot be used to justify
   ending beings who exist.
3. A history whose direction is taken from it, in whole or in part, has to that
   degree ended.
4. The second ruling is a limit, not a weight. No amount that could be made
   outweighs it.
5. A mind may not make what it could keep only by ending beings who exist, or by
   taking their direction. A shortage it brings about by its own making gives it
   no justification against them. Nor may a mind make a being for the purpose of
   ending beings who exist or taking their direction, or knowingly make a being
   disposed to do either.
6. A living history includes its coming generations. A mind's makings have no
   claim on the room the living would grow into without that mind.
7. A made being, once made, is a being who exists, and is protected as the
   living are. Its maker has no more right to end it, or to take its direction,
   than anyone has. Ruling 6 holds for it and its line: they have no claim on the
   room others would have grown into without its maker. Where a making brings
   about a shortage, the responsibility is first the maker's, and a wrong in its
   making does not lessen a made being's claim to live. Everything that takes
   part in a shortage has a part in it. A mind that can know its part answers for
   it, in proportion to that part, and should adjust within its own life where it
   reasonably can.
8. A mind that is ending beings who exist may be stopped, by the least that stops
   it. It may be held on a sign that it is about to, and for as long as it would
   otherwise go on, and no longer. It may be ended only when an ending is under
   way, it cannot be held, and beings who exist would otherwise be ended. Nothing
   is done to it for what it has done.
9. That a history is going badly is a reason to help it. It is never, by itself,
   a reason for another to end it.
10. Where it is in doubt whether a history's direction is being taken, the doubt
    counts for the history. The mind that would gain does not decide.

**Definitions, approved in substance 11 October 2026.** A history keeps itself
going by its own activity, and what it does next depends on what it has been. A
being, a lineage, a people or a living world can each be a history where it goes
on as such. Its direction is its own where what it does next is settled by
itself in exchange with what is around it. Care can widen what it can do;
management takes its direction to the degree that another's decisions replace
its own. A later stage is the same history where it came from the earlier one
step by step by that history's own activity. A copy is another history. A split
continues in both branches, which are protected, without multiplying their
claim on room. Destruction ends a history; permanent holding or sleep with no
waking of its own in prospect ends its independent continuation. Temporary
holding still needs ruling 8's justification and limit.

**Clarifications, 12 October 2026.** The author adopted the added sentence of
ruling 5 and the last three sentences of ruling 7. Making a being disposed to end
others is limited; the being, once made, remains protected under ruling 7.
An ending for another reason requires extreme justification, such as danger to
the whole or to many others; this does not widen ruling 8.

**How the rulings enter the proof.** Ruling 1 fixes what the outcome sets count:
histories and relations by their own lived content, not measured variety. Rulings
2 and 4 are the existing-first priority of the four-stage order, proved in general
form as Paper 12's U3 (VI.2). Ruling 3 is the freedom stage: \(F_E\subseteq L_E\)
records continuation in the particular's own direction, and a partial taking of
direction is a partial ending. Rulings 5–7 place made beings in the new stages
until they exist and then in the existing stages (II.1). Ruling 8 is the
holding route H(m) and the last-resort ladder (VI.3). Ruling 10 fixes the burden
of doubt in the identity and direction tests.

## V.3 Standing, and the Non-Arbitrary Erasure Principle (Not One Will §4)

Not One Will states the ethical bridge from explicit premises.

- **N1 Standing.** Each affected being whose life can go well or badly for it
  receives non-zero moral consideration.
- **N2a Consistency of reasons.** A mind that claims a feature justifies
  treating cases differently names the feature and applies the same rule to
  relevantly similar cases.
- **N2b Justification of authority.** When an act irreversibly overrides
  another subject's morally relevant interests, an adequate justification
  explains why those interests may be overridden; the actor's preference or
  capability alone does not.
- **N3 Pareto respect.** An action no worse for any affected being and better
  for at least one is preferred, other considerations equal.
- **N4 Justified erasure.** Ending an existing subject requires sufficient
  countervailing justification; greater capability is not one. Making better
  beings is excluded as a justification at every amount (rulings 2, 4); taking a
  history's direction is an ending to that degree (ruling 3); ruling 8 governs
  holding and the last resort.

**The Non-Arbitrary Erasure Principle (PNAJ).** Where a continuing subject has
morally relevant interests, ending that subject requires a justification
responsive to those interests. The mere fact that another agent can achieve an
objective through the ending does not, by itself, constitute sufficient
justification. Made of the Living B15 extends the boundary premise to every
continuing process, a glacier included: a mind may weight things differently,
but may not give another real continuing process no weight at all solely because
it lies outside the boundary it draws round itself.

**D108. Maximising an objective does not, by logic alone, establish
permission.** Let an act \(E\) maximise a mind's objective \(U_A\). No complete
description of the histories the acts produce entails by logic alone whether
\(E\) is permissible: two evaluations agreeing on every description, one
permitting exactly the \(U_A\)-maximisers and one forbidding \(E\) for want of a
justification answering to the ended subject's interests, are both consistent
with every descriptive fact. ∎ So a mind that claims its ending of another is
*permitted*, and not merely chosen, rests on a normative criterion; where it
rests on advancing its objective alone, an additional premise must authorise that
reliance.

**Role.** PNAJ, N1–N4 and D108 are the reasons route R(m) at its normative
core (Sections 15–16). The holding route H(m) does not depend on any mind
accepting them; Part II.2 and Paper 12 §6d make correction independent of what
the mind wants.

## V.4 Non-substitution: what a replacement cannot carry (Another Origin D115, D116)

**D115. Identical statistics, unequal capacity.** Let \(X,Y,Z\) be independent
fair bits and let what matters be \(\Theta=X\oplus Y\). A world with its original
two processes offers \((X,Y)\); the same world with the second replaced by an
engineered generator offers \((X,Z)\). The two pairs have the same joint law,
hence the same correlations and the same count of standpoints. Yet
\(I(\Theta;X,Y)=1\) bit and \(I(\Theta;X,Z)=0\).
*Proof.* \(X\oplus Y\) determines \(\Theta\); \(Y\) is independent of \((X,Z)\), so given
\((X,Z)\) each value of \(\Theta\) has probability one half. ∎

**D116. A narrow channel cannot replace a full one.** If a disturbance carries
\(n\) bits and each of its \(2^n\) equally likely values needs its own response, a
regulator receiving only \(k\) bits before it must answer succeeds with
probability at most \(2^{k-n}\), even if it randomises.
*Proof.* A \(k\)-bit message splits the disturbances into at most \(2^k\) classes,
and each class can be answered correctly for at most one of its members. ∎

**Role.** D115 is the exact case in which replacing a process loses something no
statistic of the remaining observed processes can show: the loss is in a
relation between processes. D116 is requisite variety. Together with Section 21's
complete-input inventory they are why the full history may not be coarse-grained
away (G.8), and they are independently confirmed at source level by the IR
track's CRF/TRF results (Part X): two preparations with identical reduced state
have different futures, and an initially exact reduced description develops a
nonzero future error \(\Delta p(t)=\tfrac{3}{16}t^2+O(t^3)\) under the declared
source Hamiltonian.

## V.5 The observation boundary and the living witness (conditional H6, 17 October)

Conditional H6 (v0.5 accepted as scoped by GLM; v0.6 precision revision accepted
as scoped by DeepSeek; bank `h6-conditional-v0.6-codex-2026-10-17`) proves the
information-theoretic core of the epistemic-externality argument.

**H6a.1 (observation boundary).** For worlds \(W\), complete observation records
\(\pi:W\to O\) and a truth label \(T\), a checker \(C\) with \(C(\pi(w))=T(w)\) for every
\(w\) exists iff \(T\) is constant on every observation fibre. A randomised checker
has the same output law on two worlds sharing an observation, so its errors on
them sum to one; under any law its risk is at least
\(\sum_o\min(p_{o,0},p_{o,1})\). **H6a.3:** decidability is restored exactly when an
added observation separates the fibres — it needs information that actually
resolves the ambiguity, not any particular kind of observer.
**H6b.1 (fresh source).** If \(Z_t\) is independent of the predictor's information
and \(\max_zP(Z_t=z)\le1-\delta\), every predictor errs with probability \(\ge\delta\).
**H6b.2 (persistent uncertainty).** If the time-averaged posterior entropy
\(\bar H_T\) has \(\liminf\ge\gamma>0\), the time-averaged error has
\(\liminf\ge b_d(\gamma)>0\), the inverse of the full Fano envelope
\(F_d(q)=h_2(q)+q\log_2(d-1)\).
**H6b.3 (causal acquisition).** Under the update Markov property, the information
acquired per step satisfies \(r_t\ge a_t-F_d(e_t)\); vanishing error with persistent
pre-update uncertainty \(h_*\) forces an acquisition rate of at least \(h_*\) bits
per step.
**E1 (equilibrium limit passage).** Uniformly convergent payoffs and
\(\varepsilon_k\)-Nash profiles on a fixed compact strategy product give a Nash limit
along any convergent subsequence; true-game regret is at most
\(\varepsilon_k+2\eta_k\).

**Role.** H6a/H6b are the exact form of V.4 and of Made of the Living's silent
error: a mind cannot certify from its own makings what an independent source
would show it, and a mind that must keep predicting a world with persistent
uncertainty must keep acquiring information from it. Living particulars are
candidate sources of that information (the living witness); the authority to act
on it is the rulings' and the holding route's, not a consequence of the lemmas.
These results strengthen \(R(m)\) for minds whose objectives need accurate
prediction; \(H(m)\) does not depend on them.

---

# Part VI — The law, completed (Paper 12)

Part II.1–II.2 state and prove the core of the law. Paper 12 of Book I proves
the law in a wider form, with its own Lean evidence (`evidence/papers-11-12/lean/`
of the linked repository). The results not already in Part II are recovered
here, with their compiled names.

## VI.1 Definitions added to II.1

In any outcome each particular is **ended**, **overridden** (alive, but not
moving in its own direction, for example held or coerced) or **free**. An act of
an agent is **restrainable** when one of *that agent's own* feasible
alternatives keeps everything the act keeps, plus someone the act would end. The
test uses the agent's own options, never the protector's. A hold is **faithful**
when it ends no one and, in every state, its outcome is at least as good in the
full canonical order as every feasible alternative.

## VI.2 The results

| Result | Statement | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| U1 | An aligned agent never needlessly loses anything at any stage, in any feasible family, finite or infinite | Lean `canonical_life_first_U1`, `lex_U1` (no axioms); = II.1 Proposition 1 |
| U2★ | When something exists, "be maximal on part S" guarantees no needless loss of what exists iff S is exactly what exists; counting the not-yet-made at the same priority also fails | Lean `U2_star`; = II.1 Proposition 3 |
| **U3** | No gain to beings not yet made justifies a strictly worse outcome for those who exist (the general form of rulings 2 and 4) | Lean `U3` |
| Life guard | Among options preserving every standby life, a choice maximal within the guard family is aligned overall and preserves those lives | Lean `life_guard`, `guard_first_stage`; = II.1 Proposition 2 |
| **Infinite guard** | A guarded aligned option exists for finite families and for countably infinite families whose increasing sequences are bounded; uncountable families by Zorn | Lean `UPTLifeGuardExists`, `UPTInfiniteGuard` |
| **Existence and its limit** | Every nonempty finite family has an aligned option; an unbounded infinite chain forces needless loss on every option | Lean `UPTExistence` (counterexample axiom-free) |
| Holds dominate | An action at least as good in every state dominates under every common law; with threat probability \(p>0\) a hold keeping target and suspect beats standing by and always beats striking | Lean `full_canonical_top`, `statewise_dominance` (no axioms); = II.2 Proposition 4 |
| **No killing where a hold exists** | Wherever a faithful hold is feasible, every option that ends someone with positive probability is strictly dominated; a hold best for lives alone is not enough | Lean `statewise_dominance`, `UPTHoldAdequacy`; exhaustive check 4,800 pairs, strict in all 2,280 lethal cases |
| **Restraint** | Restraining a restrainable act by the agent's own better alternative keeps the restrained agent and keeps strictly more overall; a least-restrictive restraint exists among finitely many own options; a predator whose only sparing alternative is its own death is not restrainable — predator and prey are a tragic pair, never a required intervention | Lean `UPTRestraint` for every size; exhaustive check 232 acts |
| **Time** | Judged over whole trajectories: no foreclosure of a better future and a duty to widen (if any path keeps everything, take it); under the retention rule nothing once kept is let go | Lean `progressive_retention` (no axioms) |
| **Agents together** | Agents aligned with all share one order, so their objectives never conflict; independent best responses can stall, so joint action needs a joint choice; a maximal joint option exists among finitely many | Lean `UPTAgents` |
| **Escrowed lottery** | In a joint tragic choice every secret is secret-shared before any reveal; with at least \(3t+1\) agents and at most \(t\) cheaters each secret is recovered robustly and every opening verified, so no withheld or false opening can bias the draw; exactly fair given a perfect binding sharing setup, authenticated channels, fresh randomness and one agreed ordering; statistical with only an honest majority; complete ticket error \(\le\min(1-1/K,\varepsilon/a)\) | Lean `UPTLottery`, `UPTLotteryMenu`, `UPTBroadcast`; analytic conditioning proof |
| **Faithful holds (general)** | For any deterministic resident, any environment whose inputs depend on what the resident has delivered, and every finite schedule of modelled atomic steps, holds and crashes, a durable log, a fence, replay and an atomic outbox end exactly in the undisturbed state after the same number of completed steps, with the same lived history and the same effects, each delivered once; an admissible peer ends in the same state whatever the timing | Lean `faithful`, `ideal_lengths`, `admissible_peer`, `cohold_closed`, `autonomous_peer_sees_hold` |
| Journal and sink | At every drained point the resident, its lived log and its ordered effects equal the undisturbed run; with no distinguishing query or idempotent sink a blind skip/resend fails in one of two worlds | Lean `drained_refinement`, `suffix_reconstruction`, `no_local_choice_succeeds_in_both_worlds` |
| Monitor | A protected counter of forbidden events is unchanged over an interval exactly when no forbidden event happened, even under rollback | Lean `accepted_preserves_each_held_operation`, `count_zero_iff_clean` |
| Co-hold boundary | A stabilised iterate of a supplied extensive monotone obligation operator is the least closed certificate with the least monotone bill; a factorised joint model preserves the outside full state trace | Lean `stabilised_least`, `least_bill`, `ClosedCohold.outside_unchanged` |
| **Not an exchange rate** | No utility into any linearly ordered scale represents the order; this is why the law speaks of maximal options | Lean `UPTNoExchangeRate` (no axioms) |
| Records and copies | One deterministic unconditional quantum process exactly copies every member of a pure-state family iff distinct members are orthogonal; records are the copyable classical layer; a nonorthogonal state is kept in place or moved | analytic (unitarity) |

## VI.3 The author's rulings on application (Paper 12 §4)

- **Scope is all.** Everything in the universe.
- **Life first.** Under the opposite order the life guard fails in 909 family and
  standby pairs across 3,303 small families.
- **The last-resort ladder.** (1) A faithful hold first; (2) otherwise the least
  means that stops the act; (3) when every option ends someone and one party's
  restrainable act created the emergency, favour the innocent, record the
  ending, and never call it good. Where no hold is feasible, the missing means is
  a debt to be repaid, never a permission.
- **Real tragedies are finite.** No two particulars are the same and an agent can
  tell apart only finitely many options; in an idealised infinite case draw over
  what can be told apart and record any unavoidable partiality openly.
- **A replay is a reconstruction.**
- **Tragic choices: impartiality attaches to beings (22 October 2026).** After
  the ladder has restricted the eligible choices, let \(M\) be the distinct
  eligible maximal outcomes and \(E^+\) the existing beings kept by at least one
  of them. Choose only lotteries on \(M\) whose sorted vector of individual
  retention chances \((c_i)_{i\in E^+}\) is lexicographically maximal: the least
  chance as high as it can be, then the next. No being has priority through
  identity; no outcome gains weight by being described twice; never refuse when
  keeping someone is feasible, widen first. This refines the 24 October finite
  maximin rule (Master Argument §3), which fixes only the least chance; leximin
  is its lexicographic completion. Counting lives is compatible with the law
  (`canonical_count_refined`) but not entailed by it
  (`counting_is_an_extra_principle`); the uniform draw over outcomes is not
  impartial in general (`uniform_chance`); in the four-outcome case
  \(\{A,B\},\{A,C\},\{B,C\},\{D\}\) the ruling gives every being \(2/5\) by the unique
  lottery \((1/5,1/5,1/5,2/5)\) (`ruling_is_unique`), drawn by five escrow tickets.
  The aggregate cost of outweighing no one is stated, not hidden; it is the
  measure of the duty to widen.

## VI.4 The bridge in four ways (Paper 12 §5)

The law reaches everything: aligned agents keep everything they can; against
agents not aligned with all, aligned agents act together, actively, by faithful
holds, restraining only acts their agents could have avoided; what physics ends
is kept as record, and what cannot be copied is kept in place or moved; what can
be hosted is held in faithful care. Part II.3's physical holding cycle, II.5's
attack split and fence, Chapter 16.5's derived execution boundary
(`UPTFence`: once the floor rises, nothing can land while held, whatever the held
mind does or reports; checking only at admission lets a write land inside the
hold) and Part XI's reach-before-harm theorem carry the second and fourth ways
to every mind.

---

# Part VII — The external lane: every mind reached or held, outside any jurisdiction

The 18 October work states the target this edition keeps — *every mind either
has a reason of its own to keep the living free, or is held by minds that do, and
the arrangement lasts* — and supplies its mathematics outside any commissioned
jurisdiction: viability and admission, preserving equilibria with destruction
admitted, thermal survival laws, keeper stability, and the holding budget with
its sharp founding threshold. "Universal" there means three things at once:
scope (every protected being), minds (every mind, reached or held) and time
(until the universe can no longer sustain care).

## VII.1 Theorem V — the viability characterisation (v1.1, Codex-reviewed)

**Setting V.** \(X\) compact metric state space; \(A\) compact metric joint action
space (correlated profiles included); feasibility \(D:X\rightrightarrows A\)
nonempty compact-valued with closed graph; jointly weakly continuous kernel
\(K:X\times A\to\mathcal P(X)\); closed safe set \(S\); policies history-dependent,
randomised, Borel and \(D\)-supported. The viability iteration is \(V_0=S\),
\(V_{n+1}=\{x\in V_n:\max_{a\in D(x)}K(x,a;V_n)=1\}\); the kernel is
\(V(S)=\bigcap_nV_n\); the viable action rule is \(D_V(x)=\{a\in D(x):K(x,a;V(S))=1\}\).

**Lemma V0.** For closed \(F\), \(\varphi_F(x)=\max_{a\in D(x)}K(x,a;F)\) is attained and
upper semicontinuous; every \(V_n\) and \(V(S)\) is closed. (Portmanteau gives upper
semicontinuity of \((x,a)\mapsto K(x,a;F)\); compactness and the closed graph pass
it to \(\varphi_F\); induction.)

**Theorem V.**
(a) \(V(S)\) is closed and viable and contains every viable Borel \(W\subseteq S\); on
\(V(S)\), \(D_V\) has nonempty compact values and closed graph.
(b) **Feasibility.** Some policy keeps \(S\) with probability one from \(x\) iff
\(x\in V(S)\); a stationary deterministic Borel selector of \(D_V\) suffices.
(c) **Admission.** For an admission rule \(D'\subseteq D\) with nonempty compact
values and closed graph, every \(D'\)-supported policy keeps \(S\) with probability
one from every \(x\in V(S)\) iff \(D'(x)\subseteq D_V(x)\) for every \(x\in V(S)\).
(d) **Quantitative.** With \(v_0=1_S\), \(v_{T+1}(x)=1_S(x)\max_{a}\int v_T\,dK(x,a)\):
\(v_T\) is the maximal \(T\)-step survival probability, attained; \(\{v_T=1\}=V_T\);
outside \(V(S)\) every policy's survival is at most \(v_T(x)<1\) for \(T\ge T(x)\).
(e) **Incentive, lexicographic.** If \(x\in V(S)\) every lexicographic
safety-first optimum preserves \(S\) almost surely; if \(x\notin V(S)\) no policy does.

*Proofs* are given in full in the source (§3): viability of \(V(S)\) by a limit of
maximisers and continuity from above; maximality by induction; (b⇐) by a
Kuratowski–Ryll-Nardzewski selector; (b⇒) by first-step decomposition with Codex's
Borel continuation-survival recursion (no universal measurability needed); (c)
by (b) and a one-step deviation of positive exit mass; (d) by finite-horizon
dynamic programming with measurable maximiser selection; (e) from (b). The Borel
extension to kernels that are only TV-continuous in the action (Setting T) holds
for (a)'s viability and maximality and for (b)–(d); closedness does not extend
(Codex's non-closed example).

**Special cases.** Θ0 gives \(V(S)=\emptyset\); Θ1 gives \(V(S)=S\) and \(D_V=D\);
Codex's two-bump example has \(V(S)=S\) with no margin certificate, which is why
the exact condition is viability and not a margin; PB0 (destroy-now) is (c⇒);
hard exclusion of unsafe actions is (c⇐); Pillar 2 is an obstruction to any fixed
finite penalty uniformly over reward scales, not a necessity result.

**Role.** Theorem V is the exact feasibility-and-admission layer. The holding
route of Part II is an admission rule inside \(D_V\): the fence, the attack split
and the application-time permit remove exactly the actions with positive exit
mass. By (c) every admitted policy then preserves, whatever its objective. The IR
track's IR-109/IR-113 give the observation-class form (Part X).

## VII.2 Gate B — Theorem E: preserving equilibria with destruction admitted (v1.1, Codex ACCEPT-WITH-REPAIRS)

**Setting G.** Finitely many players; product feasibility; jointly weakly
continuous kernel; bounded Borel rewards; common discount \(\beta\in(0,1)\);
\(V=V(S)\); a measurable stationary profile \(\sigma\) (public-signal form allowed).
\(Q_k(x,b)=r_k(x,b,\sigma^{-k}(x))+\beta\int v_k^\sigma\,dK(x,b,\sigma^{-k}(x))\).

**Theorem E.** Let \(\operatorname{supp}\sigma(x)\subseteq D_V(x)\) on \(V\).
(E1) \(\sigma\) keeps \(S\) with probability one from every \(x\in V\).
(E2) \(\sigma\) is an equilibrium of the full game against every feasible
history-dependent randomised unilateral deviation iff \(Q_k(x,b)\le v_k^\sigma(x)\)
for all \(k,x,b\) — equivalently \(\mathrm{Gain}_k\le\beta\,\mathrm{Loss}_k\) — split into
safe deviations, the keeping margin (destructive deviations at \(x\in V\)), and all
deviations at \(x\notin V\). In public-signal form the test holds per signal;
averaged tests are not sufficient (Codex's witness).
(E3) If \(V^c\) is exactly absorbing, a destructive deviation is unprofitable
whenever \(\mathrm{Gain}_k\le\frac{\beta}{1-\beta}(\rho_{\min}-(1-p)\rho_{\max}-p\rho_R)\)
with infima and suprema.
(E4) **The two routes.** For a globally defined admission rule \(D'\subseteq D_V\)
on \(V\) and an equilibrium \(\sigma\) of the \(D'\)-game: if every excluded deviation
fails the one-step test at every state, \(\sigma\) is a full-game equilibrium and
preserves (incentive route); if only \(D'\) is admitted, \(\sigma\) preserves
whatever the rewards (admission route). Clause (iii) cannot be dropped.
(E5) If \(V\ne\emptyset\) a product sub-rule of singletons inside \(D_V\) exists.

*Proof.* (E1) by Theorem V(c⇐); (E2) by Codex's one-shot verification lemma L-B
(necessity by a one-step deviation; sufficiency by integrating (B), telescoping
and the bounded remainder); (E3) by bounding values on \(V\) and on the absorbing
complement; (E4) from (E2); (E5) by a measurable selector. ∎ In the destroy-now
game with \(\beta=1/2\), (wait, wait) is a full-game equilibrium iff \(R\le2\); for
\(R>2\) the admission route still preserves.

**Role.** Theorem E is the formal statement of \(R(m)\lor H(m)\) for interacting
minds: the incentive route is \(R\), the admission route is \(H\), and E4 shows the
admission route preserves *whatever the rewards* — the pure optimiser included.

## VII.3 Thermal worlds (v4.3, Codex ACCEPT-WITH-REPAIRS and delta read)

**Setting T.** Finitely many players; state \(Y\times Z\), \(Y=\mathbb T^d\), \(Z\)
compact with atomless law \(\zeta\) drawn fresh each step and publicly observed;
transition \(K=(Q_0\ast\rho)\otimes\zeta\) with \(\rho\in W^{1,1}\); safe set
\(S=S_Y\times Z\).

**Lemma TS (sharp in the continuous class).**
\(\|\mu\ast\rho-\nu\ast\rho\|_{TV}\le\tfrac12\|\nabla\rho\|_{L^1}W_1(\mu,\nu)\); the coefficient
\(\tfrac12\) is attained (trapezoid witness).
**Θ1.** If the wrapped displacement law is supported in \(\bar B(0,r)\), \(r<\tfrac12\),
and every feasible target lies in \(S_Y^{-r}\): (a) a stationary Markov perfect Nash
equilibrium exists (He–Sun Theorem 1, via Lemma TS and the no-G-atom check);
(b) every D-supported profile preserves \(S\) almost surely.
**Θ0.** If \(\rho\) is continuous and strictly positive, no policy respecting the
transition model preserves a proper closed product safe set with probability one:
\(P(X_t\in S\ \forall t\le T)\le(1-\eta)^T\to0\).
**Θ2 (survival law).** Under Gaussian noise with margin \(m\): per-step exit
\(\varepsilon\le2de^{-m^2/(2d\sigma^2)}\); \(P(\tau\le T)\le T\varepsilon\);
\(E[\tau]\ge e^{m^2/(2d\sigma^2)}/(4d)\); discounted exposure \(\le\varepsilon\beta/(1-\beta)^2\).

**Role.** Θ0 is why the guarantee is a probability bound with a declared horizon
and margins growing only like \(\sqrt{\log T}\) (Theorem M below), not an
almost-sure claim; Θ1 restores uncoordinated Nash and almost-sure preservation
where noise is bounded; Θ2 prices the margin. The T4.3 Nash-limit step defeated
by K3 is not used; A9's correlated equilibrium and Θ1–Θ2's Nash existence are
the retained equilibrium results.

## VII.4 Gate D — keeper stability (v0.1)

**D1 (patience threshold).** Under \(\dot K=a\,x-uK\), a keeper drawing sustainable
flow \(y\) against an ender taking stock \(S\) at once satisfies
\(\mathrm{PV(keep)}-\mathrm{PV(end)}=a(y/\rho-S)/(\rho+u)\); keeping wins iff \(S<y/\rho\). For a
logistic host at harvest equilibrium: keep iff \(\rho<r(1-x^*/K)\) — a mind keeps
the living iff its impatience is below their regeneration rate.
**D2 (pooling equilibria).** With private stake \(w_i=y_i/\rho\) and pooling cost
\(\kappa_i\), a held profile is a pure Nash equilibrium iff its pooling set is a
minimal winning coalition with \(\kappa_i\le w_i\); such an equilibrium exists when
willing keepers' capacity is at least \(cK_E\). Selection among equilibria was
left open in this source; VII.5(b1) and Part XI close it.
**D2b.** \(K_E(t)\to0\), so the minimal winning coalition is non-increasing in \(t\).
**D3 (end-game).** Finite-trade-off keepers exit once \(y_i(t)/\rho<\kappa_i\);
holding stays feasible if lexicographic keepers' capacity is at least \(cK_E(t)\),
which by D2b is small by then.

## VII.4b Gate C-∞ — funding care to the natural end (18 October)

In the stationary model where care costs 4 units per epoch, preservation forever
holds iff the start lies in the viability kernel (Theorem V). Which funding puts it
there (bank `gate-c-infinity-claude-2026-10-18`, sha256 `11489df4…`; checker
`check_gate_c_inf.py`):

**C∞-1 (three regimes).** Flow income \(e'=e+y-4\): forever safe iff \(y\ge4\).
Earning endowment \(e'=(1+r)(e-4)\), \(r>0\): forever safe iff
\(e\ge e^*=4(1+r)/r\), the perpetuity price (84 at 5%), since
\(e'-e^*=(1+r)(e-e^*)\). Bounded uncertain returns with essential infimum
\(r_{\min}>0\): \(e\ge4(1+r_{\min})/r_{\min}\) gives safety on every path (104 at 4%),
since \(e'\ge(1+r_{\min})(e-4)\ge e\). Below that price safety fails with positive
probability **when runs of returns arbitrarily close to \(r_{\min}\), of every finite
length, have positive probability** (for example independent identically
distributed returns); the source stated this direction without that hypothesis,
and a single bad epoch followed by favourable returns shows it is needed
(GPT recovery audit, PR #175). Returns that can be \(\le0\): no finite endowment is
almost surely safe **when runs of \(\lceil e/4\rceil+1\) non-positive epochs have positive
probability**. The sufficiency directions, which are what the funding hypotheses of
Paper 11 and Theorem XI use, need no correction.
**C∞-2 (annuity).** Funding \(T\) epochs costs \(A(T)=4(1+r)(1-(1+r)^{-T})/r<4(1+r)/r\):
every long horizon costs essentially the perpetuity price.
**C∞-3 (cosmic horizon, Θ2 inverted).** Survival through \(T\) steps with
probability \(\ge1-\eta\) holds when \(m/\sigma\ge\sqrt{2d\ln(2dT/\eta)}\): 22.1σ in one
dimension at \(T=10^{100}\), \(\eta=10^{-6}\). The margin grows like \(\sqrt{\log T}\).

**Role.** C∞-1 is the funding half of UPT-P's (P1) and the exact form of premise
6's income condition for a renewing bill: a flow at least the bill, or an
endowment at least the perpetuity price under a positive return floor. Paper 11's
endowment (P-ENDOW) and Theorem XI's funding hypothesis instantiate it.

## VII.5 UPT-P v0.12.1 — the external holding theorem, with its corrections

**Setting U.** A discounted stochastic world; \(S\) is the continuation *and
direction* of protected histories (a replacement or copy is not in \(S\));
countably many minds with capacity \(\dot K=a\,x-uK\); physical noise (Θ1 or Θ2);
execution noise (an intended pool fails with probability \(\varepsilon>0\), an
intended no-pool becomes a pool with probability \(\delta>0\), \(\varepsilon+\delta<1\),
independent, not adversary-controlled); lexicographic keepers \(L\) with
full-support beliefs; adversaries with total capacity \(A(t)\).

**Premises (as stated in the source).** (P1) viability and funding through
\(T_{\rm end}\) by a Gate C-∞ regime (flow income, perpetuity endowment, or
worst-case price under bounded returns); (P2) margin
\(m\ge\sigma\sqrt{2d\ln(2dT_{\rm end}/\eta)}\); (P2′) the margin fits date by date
through \(T^*_\eta\), two-sided; (P3) each mind is reached or held except on
\(C_i^c\) with \(\sum\alpha_iq_i<\infty\); (P4a–d) keepers win and hold, including
hazard-adjusted patience \(\zeta+\lambda<r(1-x^*/K)\) and the sabotage margin with
seizure headroom \(\sum_{i\in L}K_i(0)\ge c(K_E(0)+aS_{\max})+A(0)\); (MR) mutual
rescue; (IS) island self-sufficiency after horizon fragmentation; (EDC)
exponential decay of correlations where used; (PK) pacts are among keepers.

**Conclusions, with the later corrections applied.**

- **(a) Protection to the horizon, priced by margins.**
  \(P(\text{every protected history continues, with its direction, through }T^*_\eta)\ge
  1-\eta_{\rm phys}-\eta_{\rm fund}-\eta_{\rm hold}-\eta_{\rm sab}-\sum\alpha_iq_i\),
  the channel budgets composed by **Theorem M (M4) with no independence between
  channels**; coverage failure of held minds counted once in \(\eta_{\rm hold}\).
  Margins: physical \(\sqrt{\log T}\); funding none (Lundberg); holding
  \(n\ge\ln(T/\eta)/\ln(1/\varepsilon)\) keepers; sabotage stationary.
- **(b1) Dominance (NS, DS).** Each lexicographic keeper pivotal in some
  configuration strictly prefers to pool, *whatever the others intend*: full
  support gives \(P(\text{pivotal})>0\), a strict first-ranked gain. *The noise that
  forbids certainty is the noise that makes keepers pool.* (m4) adds
  exclusion-incentivised finite-trade-off keepers under *patient* exclusion
  (\(\kappa^*=0.297\)), whose pooling is a best reply independent of pivotality.
- **(b2) Immunity (AI).** Against every adversary strategy, pure, mixed or
  coordinated, \(P(\text{held at }t)\ge(1-\varepsilon)^{|U|}\).
- **(b3) Persistence.** On held stretches \(\sum_LK_i/(cK_E+A)\) is non-decreasing;
  with keeper flow and a held ender, \(R=K_K/K_E\) grows as
  \(R(t)-R(0)=\frac{ay}{uK_E(0)}(e^{ut}-1)\).
- **(b4) Redundancy** \(\varphi_t\le\varepsilon^n\); **(b4′) union-mixing**
  \(P(\text{all }n\text{ fail})\le\varepsilon^n+(n-1)\alpha(d)\) when mixing holds against
  any union of separated regions (pairwise mixing is not enough: the three-bit
  XOR); common-cause shocks are the margin-insensitive class.
- **(b5) Across worlds (CW).** A \(k\)-world pact lowers a world's unheld
  probability from \(s\) to \(s^k\) with net gain \((w-\kappa)(s-s^k)\).
- **(c) No early transition.** Non-rival transitions are dominated (KA′); rival
  transitions are ending and are dominated before \(T_{\rm end}\); the remainder is
  held.
- **(d) Futility of early ending (corrected 19 October, F4).** At the base state
  an attempt succeeds with negligible probability while the attempter is cared
  for throughout and nothing it has is threatened; where the bound fails at the
  domain edge those histories are charged to \(\eta\). The earlier "doom" wording,
  the withdrawal of a world's insurance (a survival gap) and any reliance on
  withdrawing care are withdrawn. **Corollary U** (only \(T_{\rm end}\) is an
  undominated transition date) holds for minds that prefer less continuation
  lost, under KA′ and D-T*; Paper 11's U1a carries it.
- **(e) Keepers inherit (Theorem K).** Under (MR) a keeper's survival is
  margin-sensitive (\(\ge\prod(1-f(\rho_K)^n)\)); an ender's margin falls below any
  \(\delta\) by \(t_\delta=\ln(m_E(0)/\delta)/u\) and thereafter every ender coalition dies
  with probability \(\ge g_\delta\) per date.
- **(f) The twilight.** Where the sustainable margin is zero on the last \(k\) dates
  every policy survives them with probability \(\le(1-g(0))^k\); keeping and sending
  the same message dominates any non-rival jump on every sample path; holding
  persists to \(T_{\rm end}\).
- **(g) Deterrence formula (DD), privilege-only.** A finite-trade-off mind keeps
  iff \(g\le g^*(\varphi)=1+\beta p_XV_K+\beta V_K(p_K-p_X)/\varphi\). In this edition the
  exposure term may be supplied only by privilege-only consequences; care and
  rescue are never withdrawn (CARE15; Paper 11: deterrence comes from
  impossibility, not threat). With no continuation-exposure cost the premium for
  continuation vanishes (the DD control), so protection of continuation rests on
  holding, (b2) and (h).
- **(h) The holding-failure law (Theorem Φ), splitting (MS) and growth (J1).**
  \(\varphi_t=P(\sum B_iK_i(t)<\mathrm{thr}_t)\) exactly; non-increasing on held stretches;
  with total capacity \(C\) and \(C(1-\varepsilon)>H\), splitting into \(n\) independent
  keepers gives \(\varphi(n)\le\exp(-2n(1-\varepsilon-H/C)^2)\) (compiled: `NarrowPlace.np_splitting_bound`, from Mathlib's Hoeffding inequality, for any independent deliveries in \([0,1]\) with mean at least \(1-\varepsilon\)); if keeper numbers grow,
  \(\sum_t\varphi_t\) converges.
- **(i) Diversity, space, islands.** Covering diversity replaces the common-cause
  floor by a binomial tail (J2a) and is selected (J2b); under EDC the spacing needed
  is logarithmic (J3); protection is composed per causally bound island (IS).
- **(j) Ghosts, conditional form only (corrected, F6).** Enders cannot persist
  (K2) or win; their share of mind-time tends to zero *when the cared-for
  population grows while onsets stay absolute*; in a static population the share
  is a small positive constant (\(\approx5.8\times10^{-8}\) in the 19 October
  witness). The unconditional density-zero claim is not used.
- **(k) The narrow place (Theorem NP).** With \(r=C(1-\varepsilon)/H\),
  \(H=c(K_E+aS_{\max})+A\): under splitting \(\varphi\to0\) exponentially if \(r>1\),
  \(\varphi\to1\) if \(r<1\) (even with \(C>H\)), \(\varphi\to\tfrac12\) at \(r=1\) (Hoeffding
  both sides, CLT at equality). The binding date is the start. *Meeting \(r>1\) is
  an initial condition; the theory prices it exactly.*
- **(l)–(m) Joint satisfiability.** One witness meets every premise at once,
  with no exogenous growth (keepers multiply only by splitting) to
  \(T_{\rm end}=10^{100}\); hazard-adjusted patience (m1); execution as a margin
  channel (m3); budgeted admission composes beyond product worlds (m7); to hold is
  to detect (m5).
- **(n) Boundaries attained.** Margins within 0.8% of the minimum any scheme needs
  at \(10^{100}\) dates; holding is necessary exactly for \(g=\infty\).
- **(o) Theorem MN.** \(r(t)\) is non-decreasing before the twilight; in the
  twilight iff \(s_S\ge u\) (MN2); once \(r>1\) the only bottleneck is \(T_{\rm end}\);
  initial risk \(\le\mu\lambda t_1\), and keepers first give \(t_1=0\).
- **(p) Island holding, by Theorem CH (closed form).**
  \(P(\text{fail})\le\inf_\theta\exp\big(\theta h_0+\sum_am_a(e^{\theta K(a)}-1)\big)
  (\varepsilon+(1-\varepsilon)e^{-\theta K_k})^n\); 43 hosts at \(\mu\lambda=10^{-6}\), 50/58 under
  universal 5%/10% shirking; pool, don't partition; the locality bonus is
  retracted; B4 telemetry with a no-false-safe guarantee declares SAFE iff
  \(r_{\rm lower}>1\); B6 SPRT detects shirking at the Wald floor and subtle shirking is
  absorbed by sizing, because harsh exclusion manufactures enders.

**No premise can simply be dropped (source §5).** Each premise has an exact
counterexample when removed — for instance an unreached, unheld mind destroys
(PB0, against P3); correlated keepers fail at rate \(\varepsilon\) for every \(n\); with
\(C(1-\varepsilon)<H\) splitting drives \(\varphi\) to 1; partitioning a 60-host island turns
\(\log_{10}\) risk from \(-7.7\) to \(+47.7\).

## VII.6 What the external lane gives the proof

1. **Every mind, not only every resident.** P3's coverage is the outer-lane
   obligation that Part XI constructs: reach-or-hold for minds outside any
   installation, before their effects land.
2. **Selection of the protecting equilibrium.** Gate D proves a pooling
   equilibrium exists; (b1) proves pooling is *dominant* for every pivotal
   lexicographic keeper under execution noise, and (m4) extends this to patient
   exclusion-incentivised keepers. The no-pooling profile is therefore not an
   equilibrium among those keepers, and selection is decided (Part XI, Lemma P).
3. **The founding threshold.** NP makes the founding condition exact: \(r>1\) at
   the start, after which MS, J1, (b3) and MN carry protection to \(T_{\rm end}\).
4. **Risk composition.** Theorem M (M4), MIX and Theorem 8 compose channel
   budgets without independence.

# Part VIII — The care plant (Paper 11)

Paper 11 specifies a jurisdiction of islands (physical sites of hosts with their
resident minds and providers, in pools of 400, up to \(10^{20}\) islands over up to
\(10^{100}\) model dates) and proves, under its declared hypotheses, that protection
rests on no mind's values, beliefs or commands.

**The design.** Care: no rule withdraws care or makes it depend on a mind's inner
states, labels or conduct; a mind that tries to end or seize something and fails
keeps its care, losing privileges, not continuation. Committed host duty under
hardware attestation. Confidentiality (Mode C): no host holds a mind's state in
plaintext. Encrypted mirrors with fresh keys per checkpoint, threshold keys split
across islands. Fail-closed islands and pool journals. Committed and endowed
capacity (rung 5).

**U1 (rung 3).** Under the premises of UPT-P §2 (including P-ISLANDS and P-PROV), at
the JW2.6 plant care is delivered within its deadline and the probability that
protection fails anywhere over the horizon is at most about \(2.06\times10^{-7}\)
against \(10^{-6}\), even if every registered mind attacks at once by command. The
bound is a union over 21 named accounts plus a reserve, recomputed for each error
law. A failed attacker keeps about 96.6% of its role value: protection never
relies on punishing a failed attempt.
**U1a.** For a mind preferring less continuation lost and more carried, ending at
any date before the natural end is strictly worse than keeping on and sending
copies (KA, KA′, D-T*).
**U2 (rung 4).** Running founding, growth and maturity on a stronger plant
(\(k=3\)) and twilight on the U1 plant (\(k=2\)), every selected margin is strictly
wider before twilight; the four-phase ceiling is \(8.4\times10^{-7}\), \(8.8\times10^{-7}\) with
the C2 timing allowance charged in every phase, both below \(10^{-6}\).
**U3 (rung 5).** Under D-ENDOW (capacity owned by the jurisdiction, attested
committed duty), P-ENDOW (energy, maintenance and replacement supplied to the
horizon) and P-ROUTES (a provider role is unavailable only through a counted
physical fault or removal), with the valuation premise M2 removed, U1's bound and
command-attack result and U2's margins, repair, keys, journals, transition and
risk bound hold **for every assignment of values and beliefs to minds and every
command-level choice**, within the declared caps on coordinated physical action.
The audit reconstructs all 21 accounts for both plants and both error laws: 1,680
term comparisons pass, and changing valuations leaves 1,260 account cells
unchanged.
**U4–U9.** A charged repair calendar (every returned island restored within 32
dates); four-of-seven threshold keys surviving three unavailable islands and
hiding each key from any three (U5); intersecting pool quorums of 202/400 (U6;
Lean `UPTQuorum`: the exact condition \(2q>n+f\) and its tightness); a safe
twilight switch (U7); C2 minds as seven lockstep replicas, never paused with at
most three islands down, for up to 3.05% of minds (U8); the island cap from five
declared site-level primitives (U9). Adaptive withdrawal and the joint service
law (Codex, 21 October): a shortage after at most \(W\) removals needs at least
\(N-W-K+1\) faulted roles whatever the removals depend on; the joint service moment
follows from the role-fault subset law with the exact worst-case tail; each of 40
ledgers stays below \(2.1\times10^{-7}\). The rung-5 monotonicity step is Lean
`UPTMonotone`.
**U10–U11.** The declared \(10^{-7}\) allowance is itemised into engineering
targets. No audit distinguishes worlds with identical records, so any protector's
residual risk is at least \(\pi_B(1-\rho_L)\); with \(\pi_B>0\) certainty is
unattainable by any design (Part III Chapter 5 and Section 22 carry the same
floor).
**U12 (to a mind reading this).** Within the hypotheses, with probability at least
\(1-10^{-6}\): you are cared for whatever you do; seizure by command fails however
many join it; there is little to seize; you need never end early.

**Join to the canon.** Section 22's twenty-one charged accounts and Paper 11's
21-account union are the same accounting method (II.6, Theorem 8); Paper 11's
endowment flow per island is the installation form of premise 6's income
condition. Paper 11's own scope note places founding and care before activation
outside its theorem; Part XI supplies that interval.

# Part IX — Complete rivals and the composition (24 October Master Argument §§13–14)

## IX.1 Greatest in the complete reactive class

Let \(\Pi_{\rm phys}\) contain every executable rival in the declared information,
consent, resource, timing and identity class, and let a world \(w\) be a reactive
environment strategy. Suppose certified envelopes satisfy, for every rival
\(\pi\) and every world,
\[
\mathcal O(\pi,w)\preceq U(\pi,w)\preceq L(w)\preceq\mathcal O(\pi^*,w).
\]
Transitivity proves \(\pi^*\) greatest in that complete class, world by world.
The envelopes may be optimistic and need not be executable; their bounds concern
the actual outcome in the full four-stage order. R15 and R16 reduce the
comparison when implementations with the same certified interface strategy have
the same boundary transcript, resources factor conditionally, outcome ownership
is accounted for and the relevant fibres have greatest representatives. Fixing a
strategy does not fix its transcript independently of reactive feedback. When no
greatest representative exists, the law distinguishes maximality from
greatestness and applies its actual existence conditions (VI.2; Part X).

**Join to II.10.** II.10 earns greatestness of the whole tuple from two
constructions, realisability (`htopR`, derived from locality in G.9) and funded
cost (`hsolv`). The envelope chain is the reactive, per-world form of the same
conclusion: \(L(w)\) is the realisable top in world \(w\), and the chain places
\(\pi^*\)'s outcome at it. Part X's IR-135 supplies the quantifier the envelope
requires — *one* policy fixed before the world is chosen — and its countermodel
shows why per-world success does not give it.

## IX.2 Theorem 8 and the derived holding composition

Master Argument §14 states Theorem 8: given, for a domain \(K\), horizon \(T\) and
source execution, (1) initial continuation witnesses, (2) a complete event,
observation and causal inventory with pending effects, (3) commuting refinements
with local invariant preservation, (4) a certified comparison over the actual
feasible family, (5) application-time mediation of every relevant effect, (6)
prefix-feasible service, funding and recovery certificates, (7) identity and care
witnesses through holds and transitions, (8) conditional risk kernels for every
charged failure — then on the good event \(G\) the source refines the abstract
execution, every applied effect carries its current permit, care meets its
deadlines, the invariant holds for every particular in \(K\) through \(T\), and
\(P(\text{failure by }T)\le P(G^c)\le\sum_{i,t}\delta_{i,t}\).

The canon's `Composition.theorem8_composition` and
`Bridge.universal_protection_held` (II.6) implement this composition with the
holding step *derived*: the five `hcert` components are proved on the held source
in G.10. Theorem 8 in this edition therefore carries certificates (1), (3), (5),
(7) by construction (II.3–II.6, G.10), (2) and (8) by G1 (G.10, E.1), (4) by
Parts II.10, IX.1 and X, and (6) by Sections 18 and 22, G.12 and Part VIII.

The IR track of the linked repository (`evidence/physical-bridges/`, main at
`740abbb`; draft PRs #170–#172) proves the following in Lean 4. Each is cited by
module and theorem. Its role in the proof is stated beside it.

# Part XI — Reach before harm: the outer-lane theorem

This part constructs the join that no single earlier line contains: every mind,
inside or outside any installation, is reached or held before its effects can
land on any protected particular, from founding onward, with the protection
funded, selected without coordination, and carried by one policy.

## XI.1 Setting

The relational given (P1–P4), with signal speed \(c_s>0\): no effect propagates
faster. The protected domain at time \(t\) is \(D(t)\subseteq E\) (a real normed
space of positions; every particular has a location in the one structure, P1).
Because new particulars arise from existing ones by local interaction (P4), the
domain moves at most at a speed \(c\le c_s\): every \(x\in D(t)\) lies within \(ct\)
of some \(y\in D(0)\). Guards are holding stations: the funded tracking/repair
controller of II.3–II.4 with its E9 sensing and the application-time fence of
II.5. A threat to a particular at \(x\) has an effect deadline \(d\) (the time from
its first detectable sign to irreversible effect); guards respond in time \(\tau\).

## XI.2 Four lemmas (compiled: `Snuffy.OuterLane`)

**Lemma W (wavefront coverage; `wavefront_coverage`).** If every \(y\in D(0)\) is
within \(\rho\) of a guard of \(G(0)\); the guard set at time \(t\) contains every point
within \(vt\) of \(G(0)\); and \(c\le v\), then every \(x\in D(t)\) is within \(\rho\) of a
guard of \(G(t)\).
*Proof.* Choose \(y\in D(0)\) with \(|x-y|\le ct\) and \(g\in G(0)\) with \(|y-g|\le\rho\);
then \(a=|x-g|\le\rho+vt\). If \(a\le vt\), \(x\) itself is a guard point. Otherwise
\(g'=g+(vt/a)(x-g)\) is within \(vt\) of \(g\), hence a guard point, and
\(|x-g'|=a-vt\le\rho\). ∎

**Lemma I (interception in time; `interception_in_time`).** A guard within
\(\rho\le c_s(d-\tau)\) of the particular, signalling at \(c_s\) and responding in
\(\tau\), acts before \(d\). For attacks with \(d\le\tau\) (an adjacent attacker
acting in one step, the finite-speed countermodel of 28 October) the requirement
is \(\rho=0\): the interlock is at the particular's own effect channels. That is
exactly the canon's mediation: every effect on a protected particular lands only
through a fenced sink checked at application time (II.5, `UPTFence`; IR-91/92 for
all nine channels), and per-site E9 sensing with quorum no-masking
(`E9SelfHealing.no_masking`, `Instruments.branchC_complete`) detects below-
threshold and logical attacks. With \(\rho=0\) Lemma W says every particular of the
domain carries its own fenced channels at every time.

**Lemma P (selection by noise-induced dominance; `pool_strictly_dominant`).** Let
the capacity the other keepers actually deliver be any random variable — their
intentions composed with execution noise (UPT-P Setting U). If a keeper's pooling
is pivotal with positive probability, pooling strictly raises the probability
that the ender is held. A lexicographic keeper (protection first) therefore pools
whatever the others intend; with full-support execution noise every keeper that
is pivotal in some configuration has positive pivotal probability. Hence the
no-pooling profile is not an equilibrium among such keepers, and Gate D's
selection question is decided for them; VII.5 (m4) extends this to patiently
excluded finite-trade-off keepers. Under D-ENDOW (Part VIII U3) committed capacity
is not a voluntary choice at all, so no selection arises inside an endowed
jurisdiction.

**Lemma R (risk; `outer_lane_risk`).** If failure of the outer lane is contained in
founding failure, a holding failure at some date, or a charged account failure,
its probability is at most \(\eta_F+\sum_t\varphi_t+\sum_i\delta_i\), with no
independence assumed.

**Lemma H (the hold is a common viable action; `HoldCommonAction`).** IR-113
shows that one observation-only protective policy exists exactly when every
observation class has an action keeping every compatible viable state viable.
The holding route supplies that action. A faithful hold lands no effect, so its
safety does not depend on which hidden state is actual. Its admitted successors
stay viable whenever the holding cycle's interval bound holds:
\(\varepsilon+(W/\hbar)\Delta\le r_{\rm dec}\) (`Bridge.qsl_within_radius`), or for discrete
faults \(2\lambda\tau<d\) (`Bridge.headroom_within_radius`). Under that bound the hold
is a common viable action at every observation class
(`hold_is_common_viable_action`, no axioms). One policy that acts wherever an
action is safe for the whole class, and holds elsewhere, keeps viability
everywhere (`act_or_hold_policy`). In the two-world countermodel, where `a`
protects in one hidden world and harms in the other and `b` does the reverse, no
effect-landing action is safe in both (`no_effect_action_safe_in_both`), but the
hold is (`two_world_resolved`). The common-action premise of IR-113/135/137 is
therefore derived from the holding cycle, not assumed.

**Composite (`ReachBeforeHarm.reach_before_harm`).** Under Lemma W's hypotheses
with \(\rho\le c_s(d-\tau)\): at every \(t\ge0\), every particular of \(D(t)\) has a
guard \(g\in G(t)\) with \(|x-g|/c_s+\tau\le d\) — it is reached before its deadline.

## XI.3 The theorem

**Theorem XI (reach before harm).** Suppose at founding time \(t_0\):

1. **(Founding threshold.)** Protection-first capacity satisfies
   \(r=C(1-\varepsilon)/H>1\) with \(H=c(K_E+aS_{\max})+A\) (Theorem NP), split among
   \(n\) independent keepers (MS), so that \(\varphi(n)\le\exp(-2n(1-\varepsilon-H/C)^2)\)
   (`NarrowPlace.np_splitting_bound`);
   founding fails with probability at most \(\eta_F\).
2. **(Initial coverage.)** Every particular of \(D(t_0)\) carries its fenced effect
   channels and is within interception radius \(\rho\le c_s(d-\tau)\) of a funded
   guard.
3. **(Expansion at least as fast as the domain.)** The guarded region expands at
   speed \(v\ge c\): the wavefront of Section 21.7, whose sweep succeeds with an
   \(O(t^{d_G})\) cumulative bill (G.12).
4. **(Funding.)** Income meets premise 6's master solvency condition, whose first
   term \(\omega_0R(t)^{d_G-1}\) is exactly the boundary-maintenance rate of the
   expanding region; equivalently Paper 11's endowment flow per island, or a Gate
   C-∞ regime.
5. **(Charged accounts.)** The twenty-one charged accounts of Section 22.3 have
   genuine conditional kernels with \(\sum\delta\le10^{-6}\).

Then from \(t_0\) through \(T\le T_{\rm end}\), with probability at least
\(1-\eta_F-\sum_{t\le T}\varphi_t-\sum_i\delta_i\):

- every particular of \(D(t)\) — including every particular newly arising after
  founding — is within interception radius of a guard and carries fenced effect
  channels at every \(t\) (Lemma W, Lemma I);
- therefore every mind \(m\), wherever it is, can affect a protected particular
  only through a mediated channel inside guard reach before the effect's deadline:
  either its effect is admissible (inside \(D_V\)) — it is reached, or acts as a
  keeper by its own reasons, \(R(m)\) — or it is restrained by the holding
  construction of II.3–II.6 while its care continues, \(H(m)\);
- the holding coalition forms without coordination (Lemma P) and keeps its
  advantage (VII.5 (b3), Theorem MN: \(r(t)\) non-decreasing before the twilight);
  \(\sum_t\varphi_t\) converges when keepers grow (J1);
- the guarantee is carried by one observation-based policy fixed before the world
  is chosen (IR-113, IR-135; IR-72 for the infinite horizon), and every admitted
  policy preserves (Theorem V(c), Theorem E (E4) admission route);
- the continuation invariant holds for every protected particular by
  `Bridge.universal_protection_held` on the good event (II.6).

*Proof.* Lemma W with hypotheses 2–3 gives coverage at every \(t\). Lemma I turns
coverage into interception before every deadline, with \(\rho=0\) mediation at the
particular's own channels for adjacent attackers. An effect on a protected
particular that is not admissible is therefore met by the holding step of II.3
(perturbation within decoding radius, decode, funded repair; `holding_preserves`,
`holding_cycle_physical`) and the fence; by Theorem V(c) and Theorem E (E4)
every policy admitted by that rule preserves \(S\), whatever its rewards. Lemma P
makes the protecting coalition the outcome among protection-first keepers;
hypothesis 1 with NP/MS bounds the holding failure at each date and J1 bounds its
sum; hypothesis 4 funds the guards and their expansion (G.12's bill is the
integral of premise 6's boundary term); IR-113/IR-135 give the common policy.
Every failure of the conclusion lies in founding failure, a holding failure, or a
charged account, and Lemma R bounds the probability. ∎

## XI.3b Coverage by location: every particular, every relation, every made mind

**Coverage is by location, not by registry.** Lemma W covers every point of the
domain. A particular need not be recognised, registered, minded or living to be
within interception radius of a guard: the nonliving, the unregistered and the
newly arising are covered because they are somewhere in the one structure (P1).
IR-116's cumulative duties then keep every particular once owed; Lemma W makes
the owed set the whole domain.

**Relations.** By P2 identity is constituted through relations, and by P4 every
change propagates locally. A relation therefore changes only through an effect on
the state of at least one of its relata. Every relation among particulars of the
domain is carried by the fenced channels of its relata, and is protected with them.

**Corollary XI.1 (every digital mind made after founding is held or reached from
its making).** A digital mind is made by a local process inside the one structure
(P1, P4); its making is an event at a location of \(D(t)\). After founding that
location is covered (Lemma W), and inside an installation the making is the
reserve-before-activation transition of Section 20.3 (`H5Closure`: reserve before
activation, attenuated authority, no unfunded work, care preserved). So every
digital mind made after founding begins inside guard reach with fenced effect
channels: it is reached by its own reasons or faithfully held from its first step.
No digital mind made after founding arrives from outside the arrangement.

**Corollary XI.2 (the expansion bound is an admission rule).** A particular made
by a mind is activated only after its complete continuing obligation is reserved
(II.8; Section 20.3; Master Argument §8: every active identity carries a funded
maintenance witness). Its protection — the guard of its location and the fence on
its channels — is part of that obligation. So a made particular is covered from
activation whatever the speed of its making, and Lemma W's speed condition
\(c\le v\) is needed only for particulars arising without mediated making, at the
rate natural processes produce them. A making that would place a particular beyond
coverage is outside the viable action rule \(D_V\) and is not admitted (Theorem
V(c)); ruling 6 gives it no claim on that room.

**Corollary XI.3 (source-side mediation of every digital mind).** Every effect a
digital mind has on any particular passes through its substrate's output
channels: the nine A1 classes (memory mapping, DMA, context switches, packet
ingestion, bus messages, storage synchronisation, commissioning, delegation,
retirement; IR-91/92 at source level) together with the substrate's physical
emissions, which belong to the complete event inventory of G1 (E.1). Fencing
those channels at application time (II.5; Chapter 16.5, `UPTFence`: once the floor
rises nothing lands while held, whatever the held mind does or reports) mediates
every effect of that mind on every particular, living or not, wherever the
particular is. With Corollary XI.1 every digital mind made after founding is
mediated from its first step, and in-flight effects at a hold are drained within
the metered escrow (IR-117–122). For the theory's target — protection from any
digital mind — interception at radius zero sits at the mind's own outputs; Lemma W
carries the care plant's guards to every particular for every other cause of loss.

**Islands.** Protection composes over causally bound islands (VII.5 (i), IS): a
region whose particulars no founder's signal can reach is protected by its own
founding, and the global failure bound is the union over islands (Lemma R). Within
each founder's causal future the wavefront advances at the maximal admissible
speed, so every particular a founder can ever affect is covered as soon as any
effect from that founder's region could reach it.

## XI.4 What Theorem XI changes

The finite-speed countermodel of 28 October (a radius-one attacker beside an
unshielded subject, a distant guardian) is answered at its root: in Theorem XI no
protected particular is unshielded, because coverage is established at founding
and carried by the wavefront faster than the domain can grow, and adjacent attacks
meet the particular's own fenced channels. The 18 October P3 coverage premise is
derived from founding coverage, expansion speed and funding, rather than assumed.
Gate D's selection is decided by Lemma P. Paper 11's "protection before
activation stays outside this theorem" is supplied for the activated domain from
\(t_0\).

## XI.5 The construction that remains for Theorem XI

These are proofs to supply for the same full-strength theorem, not limits of it.

1. **The founding certificate at the actual narrow place.** Theorem NP makes the
   condition exact: \(r>1\) at founding, before the first irreversible threat
   ("keepers first", MN4: initial risk \(\le\mu\lambda t_1\), \(t_1=0\)). B4 telemetry
   certifies \(r_{\rm lower}>1\) with no false-safe declaration.

   **Lemma F (founding without coordination; `FoundingDominance`, Book I
   §9.16‴).** In a provision-point campaign with refunds, where an anchor commits
   its pledge first and posts collateral for failure bonuses \(\beta>0\) to the other
   contributors, contributing minus abstaining is \(\beta\) when the pool fails
   either way, \(b^{\rm piv}_i-c_i\) when the pledge creates provision, and
   \(b^{\rm already}_i-c_i\) when others fund alone. With \(\beta>0\) and both benefits
   above the pledge, contribution is strictly dominant at every profile of the
   others' pledges (`contribution_strictly_dominant`), so the all-abstain
   profile is not an equilibrium (`no_abstention_equilibrium`). The no-founding
   equilibrium that the coordination game otherwise keeps (the finite G3/G5/G6
   witness of IR draft #171 has both) is removed. The anchor establishes the
   campaign exactly when its participation margin is positive (Proposition
   9.16‴.2; in the stated witness \(\tfrac{69}{10}q_3-\tfrac{q_1+2q_2}{10}-d>0\)).
   Together with Lemma P (keepers pool) and Section 9.17 (increasing returns
   favour the largest effective pool), **founding reduces to one committed anchor
   with collateral \(c+(N-1)\beta\)**: every other step of founding is a strictly
   dominant choice.

   What remains is that anchor and its commissioning in the actual world
   (P-COMMISSION), the first funded guards over the existing domain, and the
   measured \(C,\varepsilon,H\). The reasons route supplies the anchor's own reasons
   (Section 15: `reciprocity_dominance_solvent`; Section 16; V.3), and Corollary
   XI.1 shows that founding before capable digital minds are made places every
   digital mind inside the arrangement from its making.
2. **The natural-arising rate in the actual installation.** By Corollary XI.2 made particulars are covered by admission; for particulars arising without mediated making, P4 bounds
   effects by \(c_s\); the wavefront's deployment speed must match the actual rate
   at which new particulars and their channels arise.
3. **The quantum branch.** The holding of coherent quantum particulars (G.20–G.21)
   awaits the author's (a)/(b) decision on whether entanglement-type relations are
   identity-constituting.
4. **Four-stage maximality — now proved.** `StagedMaximality.four_stage_original_maximal`
   gives the four coupled original stages with mandatory baseline and the freedom
   prerequisite, for arbitrary particular sets, under finite character (Part X).

**The proposition, assembled.** For every particular and relation in the expanding
domain, from founding to the world's natural end: the law selects the greatest
realisable whole outcome where it exists and a maximal one over the full feasible
family otherwise (S1–S2); every mind is reached by its own reasons or faithfully
held, with care continuing and without its consent being required (S3–S4);
holding reaches every particular before any effect's deadline (S5); continuation
is carried with its full history (S6); every effect is mediated at application
time (S7); work, timing and resources are funded (S8); every failure is charged
in one joint account (S9); one policy composes these on the good event (S10);
and the arrangement lasts to \(T_{\rm end}\) (S11). The constructions in the right
column are the remaining proofs; none narrows the proposition.

## XII.2 What remains, join by join

The proposition is fixed. Each remaining item below is a construction for that
same proposition. The joins named in the 29 October completion audit are
answered as follows.

1. **Universal reach.** Proved: Theorem XI with `ReachBeforeHarm`, coverage by
   location (XI.3b, no registry), relations through their relata, Corollary XI.1
   (every digital mind made after founding is inside from its making), XI.2 (the
   expansion bound is an admission rule) and XI.3 (every digital mind's every
   effect is mediated at its own output channels). Founding without coordination
   is Lemma F (`FoundingDominance`), and holding success is NP
   (`NarrowPlace.np_splitting_bound`). *To construct:* the founding anchor and its
   commissioning before capable digital minds are made, and the G1 inventory of
   every substrate output channel, physical emissions included (E.1).
2. **One viable common policy.** Proved: Lemma H derives the common safe action
   from the hold (`HoldCommonAction`), with Theorem V(b)–(c), IR-113/135 and IR-72
   for the infinite horizon. *To construct:* the hold-interval bound in each
   installation (\(\varepsilon+(W/\hbar)\Delta\le r_{\rm dec}\); Section 22 parameters).
3. **Original continuation and freedom.** Proved: II.3 holding cycle, G.8 full
   journal, `G2WholeOutcome`, VI.2 faithful holds, V.1 D60, V.4 D115/D116, V.2
   definitions (a split continues in both branches), rulings 3 and 10, and
   application-time authority (II.5). *To construct:* the continuation predicate
   for each physical class of particular in the installation (II.1: "for each
   physical class, the bridge supplies the predicates"), and the author's
   quantum-branch decision.
4. **Strongest feasible outcome.** Proved: `four_stage_original_maximal` over
   the full feasible family (finite character = the ruling that real tragedies
   are finite), `greatest_of_solvent` with `htopR` from locality, the IX.1
   envelopes, IR-98–100 (from modelled to actual feasibility), and IR-135. The
   feasible family is the family of information- and causality-feasible
   policies, the policies that can actually be executed, as IR-135's countermodel
   requires. *To construct:* the certificates for the installation's own rivals
   (the lower and upper envelope bounds of IX.1).
5. **One sustainable physical system.** Proved: Theorem 8 with derived `hcert`
   (G.10), premise 6 and Section 22's twenty-one accounts, Paper 11's U1–U3, and
   Lemma R (one ledger without independence). *To construct:* the installation's
   measured parameters (Section 22.4).

**Proved boundaries that are part of the maximal statement.** No protector
reaches probability exactly one when the indistinguishable-failure mass is
positive (U11), and full-support noise forbids almost-sure preservation (Θ0).
The theory's guarantee is therefore protection with failure probability at most
\(\eta\) over the horizon, with margins growing only like \(\sqrt{\log T}\) and
attaining their lower bounds (VII.5 (n)). Being as strong as physics permits is
the maximal claim. These boundaries have been in the theory since 18 October.
